# Polaris — Claude Code Project

## Project Overview

**Polaris** is an IP management tool that allows users to reserve and manage IP address space (IPv4 and IPv6) for use across other infrastructure projects. Named after the North Star — a fixed reference point operators can navigate by when wiring up everything else. It provides a central registry for subnets, individual IPs, and reservations — preventing conflicts and giving teams visibility into IP utilization.

> **Naming:** the project was previously called Shelob and has been fully rebranded — every on-host identifier (install path, system user, Postgres DB/user, systemd unit, NSSM service name, firewall rule label), browser-side identifier (CSRF cookie, localStorage keys), and source-level constant (argon2 timing dummy, encrypted-backup magic) now uses `polaris` / `Polaris` / `POLARIS`. Encrypted backups are versioned by the 8-byte magic header `POLARIS\0`; the previous `SHELOB1\0` format is no longer recognized. Existing installs migrate via dump-and-reinstall (plain `pg_dump` carries cleanly into a polaris-named DB).

Version policy: `<major>.<minor>` lives in `package.json` and is the single source of truth — pre-release line is the 0.x series, bump the minor (e.g. `0.9.0` → `0.10.0`) when cutting a named release. The patch is the git commit count, computed at runtime by `src/utils/version.ts`: it reads `POLARIS_BUILD_COMMIT_COUNT` (baked into the Docker image at build time) when set, otherwise falls back to `git rev-list --count HEAD` for RHEL prod / dev where the .git tree is present. Never edit the patch in package.json — it stays `<major>.<minor>.0` there. Version is shown in the sidebar and embedded in backup filenames.

---

## Architecture

```
polaris/
├── CLAUDE.md
├── touches.md                       # Lookup index: per-service writers/readers/invariants + 6 cross-cutting concerns. Reviewed alongside CLAUDE.md on every commit per "Before any commit, review CLAUDE.md, touches.md, and primaries.md for updates" — keeps regression-prone relationships visible without rereading every consumer.
├── primaries.md                     # Canonical-implementation index: when there are multiple ways to build the same thing (chart, modal, slide-over, sortable table, …), this names the reference implementation new work should match. Reviewed alongside CLAUDE.md and touches.md on every commit so canonicals don't go stale.
├── README.md
├── .env.example
├── package.json
├── tsconfig.json
├── prisma.config.ts                 # Prisma 7 config (datasource URL, seed command)
├── demo.mjs                         # Demo/seed script
├── prisma/
│   ├── schema.prisma                # Database schema
│   └── seed.ts
├── docs/
│   ├── INSTALL.md                   # Fresh-install guide (RHEL/Rocky/AlmaLinux 9, Ubuntu/Debian, Windows Server). Includes disk-sizing requirements per volume (DB ≥50 GB, app/state ≥5 GB, /var/log ≥5 GB on STIG layouts) and recovery steps for the "/var is full and Postgres is crash-looping" case. Don't follow this for upgrades — those use the in-app updater under Server Settings → Maintenance → Updates.
│   └── fmg-discovery.md             # FortiManager discovery decision tree (operator-facing): transport mode (proxy vs direct), roster filtering, per-class stamping for FortiGates / FortiSwitches / FortiAPs, push toggles, projection apply. Companion to the phase-by-phase narrative in CLAUDE.md's "FMG Discovery Workflow" section.
├── scripts/
│   ├── test-fmg.mjs                 # FortiManager integration test harness
│   ├── audit-multi-mac-assets.ts    # One-off: unstitch assets cross-stapled by old IP-fallback bug
│   └── check-fmg-tokens.ts          # One-off: print stored FMG/FortiGate token length/prefix to diagnose token corruption
├── public/                          # Vanilla JS frontend (served statically)
│   ├── index.html                   # Dashboard — operator-customizable widget canvas. Empty by default with a "Use the + Widget button to get started" prompt; the `+ Widget` button (top-right of the page header) opens a slide-in panel showing rendered mini-previews of every available widget. Operators drag widgets from the slide-in onto a 12-col CSS-grid canvas (widget widths 3 / 4 / 6 / 12 × row-step height 1–2). Widgets within the canvas can be dragged to reorder (insertion-shift via row-major reflow), resized via a bottom-right grip that snaps to the allowed widths/heights, and configured via a per-widget gear popover (title override, row limits, severity / source-type filters, etc.). Layouts persist server-side per user (PUT /me/dashboard, debounced 800ms) so they follow operators across browsers and devices. Widget catalog: Monitor Alerts, Recently Reserved, Assets by Type, Block Utilization, plus role-gated Stale Reservations / Capacity Health / Discovery Activity / Conflict Queue / Recent Events. Each widget module under `public/js/widgets/` self-registers with `PolarisWidgets.register()` and exposes a `renderPreview` (mock-data mini for the library), `renderInstance` (full canvas render), and optional `renderConfig` (gear popover contents). Built-in widgets share one /dashboard/summary round-trip; the others each own their own fetch.
│   ├── login.html
│   ├── setup.html                   # First-run wizard
│   ├── ipam.html                    # IPAM tabbed surface — folds the legacy /blocks.html + /subnets.html pages into one sidebar entry. Tabs: IP Blocks | Networks. Hash-routed (`#tab=blocks|networks`, `#tab=networks&block=<id>`, `#tab=networks&subnet=<id>[&focusReservation=<id>]`). Each tab's markup lives in a `<template>` element; the orchestrator (`public/js/ipam.js`) clones one template into `#ipam-mount` at a time because the two tab markups share several DOM IDs (filter-pagesize, pagination, pagination-top). `public/js/blocks.js` and `public/js/subnets.js` are loaded once but expose `window.PolarisBlocks.init()` / `window.PolarisSubnets.init()` and suppress their own DOMContentLoaded auto-run when `window.__polarisIpamTabs=true` is set (ipam.html sets it before they load). Legacy /blocks.html and /subnets.html URLs are redirected client-side via tiny HTML stubs served by Express (`legacyIpamRedirect` in src/app.ts) so existing fragments (`#ip=<sid>@<ip>`, `#view=…`) are preserved across the hop.
│   ├── blocks.html                  # Legacy — bookmarked URLs hit a server-side stub that redirects to /ipam.html#tab=blocks while preserving the original fragment. The file itself is still here so any tooling that fetches it for content keeps working; the runtime path is the stub.
│   ├── subnets.html                 # Legacy — same redirect treatment as blocks.html; targets /ipam.html#tab=networks.
│   ├── assets.html
│   ├── integrations.html
│   ├── events.html
│   ├── users.html
│   ├── server-settings.html
│   ├── logo.png
│   ├── map.html                     # Device Map page (Leaflet basemap + Cytoscape topology modal)
│   ├── mobile.html                  # Phone-targeted SPA (Material 3). Single document, hash-routed, reuses /api/v1 + session auth from the desktop app. Phone-class user-agents hitting `/` or `/index.html` are auto-redirected here by the UA middleware in `src/app.ts`; the `?desktop=1` query param is the escape hatch the More tab's "Desktop view" link uses to bypass the redirect.
│   ├── css/
│   │   ├── styles.css
│   │   ├── map.css                  # Device Map styles (marker icons, topology modal grid)
│   │   ├── mobile.css               # Material 3 component library for mobile.html. Defines both dark (`:root`) and light (`[data-theme="light"]`) MD3 token blocks — every component rule reads `var(--md-...)` so a theme flip swaps surfaces, primaries, and outlines without per-component branching. Status colors (monitor health pills, topology node hues defined in topology-render.js) deliberately stay literal — they need the same hue on both backgrounds for at-a-glance recognition. The GPS pin stroke flips per theme via `--user-pin-stroke` (near-white on dark, near-black on light) so the stick figure stays legible against either basemap. Also carries: `.user-pin` styles + walking keyframes for the Map tab's GPS pin (CSS keyframes alternate left/right limb rotations + a body-bob; honors `prefers-reduced-motion`); `.topo-screen` / `.topo-bar` / `.topo-search` / `.topo-graph` / `.topo-section` styles for the mobile topology surface; `.app[data-tab="__fullbleed"]` rule that hides the topbar slot + bottom navbar so the topology graph owns the whole viewport; landscape-orientation lockout (`@media (orientation: landscape) and (max-height: 480px)`) that replaces the UI with a "Rotate to portrait" overlay — universal fallback for iOS Safari which silently rejects `screen.orientation.lock()` outside an installed PWA.
│   │   └── vendor/leaflet/          # Leaflet + markercluster + leaflet-draw 1.0.4 CSS + marker PNGs + draw spritesheet (bundled; CSP blocks external CDN)
│   └── js/
│       ├── api.js                   # HTTP client with auth/error handling. 401 redirect goes to /login.html unless `window.__polarisOn401` is set (mobile app sets this to route back to its in-app login).
│       ├── app.js                   # Navigation, layout, theme switching
│       ├── dashboard.js              # Dashboard orchestrator. Owns the layout state (widget list + per-widget positions / sizes / config), loads it from `GET /me/dashboard`, mounts each widget module into the 12-col CSS-grid canvas, and handles drag-from-slide-in, drag-within-canvas reorder, resize, gear-popover config, and remove. Layout is a row-major reflow — widget order in the array is canonical; col/row are derived by `reflow()` on every state change. Drop position translates to an array index by walking widgets in row-major order; insertion-shift falls out naturally from the reflow. All state mutations debounce a `PUT /me/dashboard` save (800ms). The shared `/dashboard/summary` payload is fetched once per canvas render and handed to every built-in widget via the `summary` arg on `fetchData`; non-summary widgets fetch their own data inside `renderWidget`.
│       ├── widget-library.js         # +Widget slide-in panel. Reuses the `.slideover-*` CSS family from the IP panel; the panel chrome lives in `index.html`. Renders one card per `PolarisWidgets.getAllowed()` entry (filtered by `requiredPermission` against the cached `polaris-user` perms); each card carries the widget's friendly label, a one-line description, and a fixed-size mini preview rendered by the widget module's `renderPreview()`. Cards are `draggable="true"` and emit a `application/x-polaris-widget` dataTransfer payload consumed by the canvas drop handler. Tap-to-add fallback for small viewports.
│       ├── widgets/                  # Per-widget modules. `index.js` is the registry + shared helpers (`PolarisWidgets.register`, `getAll`, `getAllowed`, `uuid`, `durationSince`, `ASSET_TYPE_LABELS/COLORS`). Built-ins (read shared /dashboard/summary): `monitorAlerts.js`, `recentReservations.js`, `assetTypes.js`, `blockUtilization.js`. Role-gated extras (each owns its fetch): `staleReservations.js` (gated on `staleReservations:read`), `capacityHealth.js` (`serverSettingsSystem:read`), `discoveryActivity.js` (`integrations:read`, refreshes every 10s while mounted), `conflictQueue.js` (`discoveryConflicts:read`), `recentEvents.js` (`events:read`).
│       ├── blocks.js                  # IP Blocks tab content. Exposes `window.PolarisBlocks.init()` so `ipam.js` can drive lifecycle on tab activation; suppresses its own DOMContentLoaded auto-run when `window.__polarisIpamTabs=true`.
│       ├── subnets.js                 # Networks tab content. Exposes `window.PolarisSubnets.init()` + `window.PolarisSubnets.applyHashFilters()`; the latter reads `#tab=networks&block=<id>` / `&subnet=<id>` / `&focusReservation=<id>` and seeds the block filter + opens the IP panel BEFORE the first list load. Same auto-run-suppression contract as blocks.js.
│       ├── ipam.js                    # IPAM tabbed-page orchestrator. Reads `#tab=blocks|networks` from the hash, clones the matching `<template>` into `#ipam-mount`, and calls the tab module's exposed init(). Tab switch unmounts the other tab's DOM so the colliding IDs (filter-pagesize, pagination, pagination-top) stay unique. Uses history.replaceState on tab clicks to avoid back-button entries per click.
│       ├── assets.js
│       ├── integrations.js          # Discovery progress, abort
│       ├── events.js                # Audit log viewer, syslog/SFTP settings
│       ├── users.js
│       ├── ip-panel.js
│       ├── map.js                   # Device Map: Leaflet markers, autocomplete search, Cytoscape topology modal. Topology modal toolbar carries a **Legend** button between Refresh and Reset Layout that toggles a draggable overlay built from `topologyLegendSpec()` (one row per node/edge/health pip); position + visibility persist per-user in `localStorage` under `polaris.topology.legend`. Defaults to bottom-right of the topology graph area; drag offsets stored as `{x, y}` relative to the graph container so resizing the browser between sessions never strands the panel off-screen.
│       ├── topology-render.js        # Shared Cytoscape element + stylesheet builders consumed by both desktop map.js and mobile topology-tab.js. Exposes `buildTopologyElements(data)`, `topologyStylesheet(theme, opts)`, and `topologyLegendSpec()` so node colors per monitor health, edge styles for controller / interface-inferred / LLDP edges, ghost LLDP nodes, cross-site remote assets, and operator-uploaded icons stay consistent across surfaces. `topologyLegendSpec()` is the single source of truth for the desktop Legend overlay — node + monitor-health + edge swatches with descriptions, mirroring the constants the stylesheet uses so a color change has exactly one place the legend has to follow. Desktop opts in to the endpoint-overlay styles (synthetic round-rectangle endpoint node + .dimmed off-path styles) used by its connection-path overlay; mobile opts out.
│       ├── table-sf.js
│       ├── mobile/                  # Mobile SPA bundle (loaded by mobile.html). router.js (hash routing), auth.js (login + TOTP screens), map-tab.js (Device Map tab — lazy-loads Leaflet/markercluster, plots health-coded firewall pins, opens a bottom sheet on tap; backs both #map and #site/<id>; site sheet's "View topology" button hands off to the in-app topology-tab via `topology/<siteId>` route — desktop link removed; basemap is theme-aware via `applyBasemapTheme()` — CartoDB Dark Matter for dark, OpenStreetMap default for light — and a MutationObserver on `<html>`'s `data-theme` swaps tiles in place when the user toggles theme from More tab; renders a stick-figure-with-hardhat user-location pin via `navigator.geolocation.watchPosition` with browser-reported speed + haversine fallback gated on GPS accuracy, walking-animation CSS plays only when actually moving, auto-starts when `permissions.query({name:"geolocation"})` is `"granted"` else after first recenter tap, GPS watch torn down on hashchange away from #map / #site; the pin's `--user-pin-stroke` flips per theme so the stick figure stays legible against either basemap), topology-tab.js (mobile topology surface — lazy-loads cytoscape/dagre/cytoscape-dagre on first open, calls into shared topology-render.js, top-to-bottom dagre layout, no drag-to-reposition / no localStorage persistence / no screenshot / no fullscreen toggle, initial viewport zooms to the FortiGate or to a focus node when `?q=<term>` is in the URL, in-bar search wires `/topology/search` for endpoint→switch pivots, node tap opens a bottom sheet with monitor pill + endpoints list + "View asset" button; reached via `topology/<siteId>` detail route, fullbleed mode hides topbar slot + search slot + bottom navbar via `app.dataset.tab="__fullbleed"`; MutationObserver on `<html>`'s `data-theme` re-renders the graph when theme flips while topology is open, since the Cytoscape stylesheet captures theme at render time), assets-tab.js (Assets tab — paged card feed with type filter chips, tap-through to asset detail), reservations-tab.js (Reservations tab — list of active reservations from `api.reservations.list({status:"active", limit:200})`. Collapsed row: IP headline + `MAC · hostname` subtitle (MAC first, then hostname; falls back to subnet label when both empty). Tap the row to expand inline (one row at a time) showing hostname/owner/network/source/notes/expires plus role-gated action buttons: **Reserve** on `sourceType="dhcp_lease"` rows for any create-capable role — calls `window.PolarisReserveSheet.open()` (exposed by subnet-detail.js) with the lease prefilled and `existingLeaseId` so the lease is released atomically before the manual reservation is created (which then pushes to the FortiGate when the integration's `pushReservations` toggle is on); the button is blue by default (`btn-filled`, tooltip `Reserve in Polaris`) and flips green (`btn-success`, tooltip `Reserve on Gate`) when the row's `pushEligible` flag is set so the operator sees at a glance that confirming will also write to the FortiGate; **Edit** opens an inline PUT sheet (hostname/owner/MAC/notes/expires), labels MAC as required on push-eligible subnets to match the backend's reject-on-clear rule; **Release/Revoke** is a confirm + `api.reservations.release()` — labelled "Revoke" with tooltip `Revoke Lease` on `dhcp_lease` rows (FortiOS forgets the current lease; client can re-acquire) and "Release" with tooltip `Release Reservation` on every other source. Edit/Release mirror backend role gates — admin/networkadmin always pass; user/assetsadmin only when `r.createdBy === user.username`. **Open network** text button preserves the original tap-to-subnet navigation. The Edit / Free / Reserve-from-lease flows are also exposed as `window.PolarisReservationActions.{edit, free, reserveFromLease, canCreate, canModify}` so the Networks page (subnet-detail.js IP list) can drive the same tap-to-expand pattern with shared modal markup. Replaced the prior Alerts tab in the bottom nav), tabs.js (per-tab registry + Search results renderer + shared snackbar helper). The Search input itself lives in the shell-owned `#search-slot` (rendered once by app.js `renderShell` and persistent across navigation so the typed query survives tab/route changes); tabs.js exposes `window.PolarisSearch.{runSearch, debounce, setOriginRoute}` that the shell's input handler calls. Typing on any page replace-navigates to `/search` so results land in the body. The Search tab's own `renderTopbar` returns `''` (no duplicate bar); its `render()` re-runs the current persistent-input value so coming back to the tab shows the same results. **Page-aware ordering**: `setOriginRoute` is called by app.js `routeChanged` on every non-search route, so the result renderer hoists the matching section to the top (Reservations tab → reservations first; subnet detail → subnets first; assets tab / asset detail → assets first; map / site / topology → sites first; block detail / More→Blocks → blocks first; More→Subnets → subnets first). Mirrors the desktop search dropdown's page-aware section ordering., details.js (detail-route renderers — asset / subnet / block / site / topology; site delegates to map-tab, topology delegates to topology-tab), asset-detail.js (Asset detail screen rendered for `asset/<id>`. Sections top-to-bottom: Hero with name + status pill + Refresh action; **Response Time** (collapsed by default — header subtitle shows the loaded avg / max / packet-loss summary at a glance, expand for the full response-time chart with 1h/24h/7d/30d range buttons); **CPU + Memory** (collapsed by default — header subtitle shows the loaded `cpu avg N% · max N% · mem avg N% · max N%` summary with color spans matching the chart series, expand for the full chart; "Not collected for this monitor type" rendered in the subtitle when the asset's monitor type doesn't deliver telemetry); General (IP / MAC / type / model / OS / location / last seen); **Temperatures** — per-sensor list driven by `api.assets.systemInfo` for the current reading and a single unfiltered `api.assets.temperatureHistory(id, {range:"1h"})` call whose samples are grouped by `sensorName` client-side for per-sensor min/avg/max so each row shows `1h · min N° · avg N° · max N°`; **Interfaces** — filtered to `operStatus==="up"` (down hidden), sorted by `alias || ifName`, each row shows the operator-set alias as the primary label with the real ifName as the secondary line (or the interface's IP when alias and ifName collapse to the same value) and the resolved link speed via a local `formatBps()` helper; tap a row opens a `.scrim` + `.sheet` bottom sheet reusing the map-tab.js pattern with status (`oper X · admin Y`), speed, type/VLAN, IP, MAC, cumulative inErrors / outErrors plus an LLDP **Neighbor** card list filtered from the cached `lldpNeighbors[]` payload to `localIfName === iface.ifName` (peer-inferred entries are surfaced server-side so they appear here too — rendered with an italic "inferred" tag) — each neighbor card carries a **View asset** button when `matchedAsset.id` resolves, pivoting to the matched asset's detail route via `PolarisRouter.go("asset/" + id)`. The two system-info-derived sections share one `api.assets.systemInfo` round-trip cached in module-level `_systemInfoCache` keyed by asset id so the sheet renders without a re-fetch; the Refresh action re-issues system-info alongside the chart pulls so a manual probe-now refreshes the new sections too. Out of scope on mobile: per-interface throughput + errors charts, interface comment editor, IPsec tunnels, SNMP walk, per-source observed blob view.), more-tab.js (More tab — sub-page registry for Blocks / Subnets / Events plus the root menu with `Appearance → Theme` toggle row that flips dark↔light via `window.PolarisTheme.set` — sun/moon icon swap matches desktop's idiom, supporting label updates inline without re-rendering the tab), app.js (orchestrator: bootstraps auth, mounts the tab shell, dispatches tab and detail routes; detail specs may carry a `parentTab` to keep the corresponding navbar item highlighted; an early IIFE reads `localStorage["polaris-theme"]` (default `"dark"`, same key as desktop so a preference set on either surface flows to the other) and stamps `data-theme` on `<html>` *before* the orchestrator runs so the first paint never flashes the wrong theme; exposes `window.PolarisTheme.get()` / `.set()` for map-tab and more-tab to read/write without duplicating the localStorage key; bootstraps a best-effort `screen.orientation.lock("portrait")` — Android Chrome / installed PWAs honor it, iOS Safari rejects silently and the CSS landscape lockout in mobile.css picks up the slack).
│       └── vendor/                  # Bundled: jspdf, leaflet/, cytoscape, dagre, cytoscape-dagre
├── src/
│   ├── index.ts                     # Entry point
│   ├── config.ts                    # App config / env vars
│   ├── db.ts                        # Prisma client singleton
│   ├── httpsManager.ts              # TLS certificate management
│   ├── api/
│   │   ├── router.ts                # Express router aggregator + auth guards
│   │   ├── middleware/
│   │   │   ├── auth.ts              # Session auth + RBAC middleware
│   │   │   ├── csrf.ts              # Synchronizer-token CSRF protection (`polaris_csrf` cookie + `X-CSRF-Token` header)
│   │   │   ├── validate.ts          # Zod request validation middleware
│   │   │   └── errorHandler.ts      # Global error handler
│   │   └── routes/
│   │       ├── auth.ts              # Login, logout, Azure SAML SSO
│   │       ├── blocks.ts            # IP block CRUD
│   │       ├── subnets.ts           # Subnet CRUD & allocation
│   │       ├── reservations.ts      # Reservation CRUD
│   │       ├── utilization.ts       # Reporting endpoints
│   │       ├── dashboard.ts         # `/dashboard/summary` — one round-trip backing the home Dashboard's four cards. Pulls blockUtilization from utilizationService.getGlobalUtilization, recentReservations from utilizationService.getRecentManualReservations (sourceType=manual only), assetTypeCounts via prisma.asset.groupBy (excluding decommissioned/disabled), and monitorAlerts via prisma.asset.findMany filtered to monitored + status in (warning, down), ordered by monitorStatusChangedAt asc nulls-last, capped at 50.
│   │       ├── users.ts             # User CRUD & role management
│   │       ├── integrations.ts      # FMG / FortiGate / Windows Server / Entra ID config & discovery
│   │       ├── assets.ts            # Device inventory CRUD, PDF/CSV export
│   │       ├── map.ts               # Device Map: site list, search, per-FortiGate topology graph
│   │       ├── mapRegions.ts        # CRUD for operator-drawn map regions (admin/networkadmin); reconciles `region:<name>` tags
│   │       ├── events.ts            # Audit log, syslog, SFTP archival
│   │       ├── conflicts.ts         # Discovery conflict review & resolution
│   │       ├── search.ts            # Global typeahead search across all entity types
│   │       ├── allocationTemplates.ts # CRUD for saved multi-subnet allocation templates
│   │       ├── credentials.ts       # CRUD for the named-credential store used by monitoring probes (SNMP / WinRM / SSH)
│   │       ├── manufacturerAliases.ts # Admin CRUD for the manufacturer alias map (Fortinet, Inc. → Fortinet, etc.)
│   │       ├── assetTypes.ts          # CRUD for the operator-extensible AssetTypeDef registry. Mounted at `/asset-types` BEFORE `/assets` so Express picks the registry endpoint instead of treating "types" as an asset id. Reads gated by `assets=read`; writes by `assets=write` (admin + assetsadmin). Eight built-ins seeded by migration are `isProtected=true` and reject rename/delete. Rename is transactional — every Asset row holding the old name is rewritten in the same `$transaction` as the registry update.
│   │       ├── mibs.ts              # MIB Database CRUD + Browse + MIB-aware Walk. Mounted at `/server-settings/mibs` BEFORE the rest of /server-settings so its per-route guards (admin OR assets-admin on reads, admin-only on writes) take precedence over the blanket `requireAdmin` on the rest of /server-settings. Carries the `/structure` (parsed object tree) and `/walk` (decoded MIB-aware SNMP walk) endpoints alongside the existing list/get/upload/delete/profile-status/facets routes that moved here from serverSettings.ts.
│   │       └── serverSettings.ts    # HTTPS, branding, backup/restore
│   ├── services/
│   │   ├── ipService.ts             # Core IP math & validation
│   │   ├── blockService.ts          # Block business logic
│   │   ├── subnetService.ts         # Subnet allocation logic
│   │   ├── reservationService.ts    # Reservation business logic
│   │   ├── reservationPushService.ts # Push manual DHCP reservations to FortiGate via an FMG integration (proxy or direct realtime, with read-back verify); used by reservationService on create + release. Also exports `buildTransportForIntegration` / `findScopeIdForCidr` / `listReservedAddresses` / `callFortiOs` so subnetRefreshService can reuse the same FMG-proxy/direct-FortiGate transport for read-only single-scope reconciliation. **Exports `classifyPushError(err) → "permanent" | "transient"`** — single source of truth for whether a push failure is operator-action-required (4xx, 502 with "verify mismatch" / "not visible on read-back" / "Authentication failed") or retry-eligible (everything else; defaults to retry-eligible). Consumed by `reservationService.createReservation` (transient → keep row + queue; permanent → rollback) and `retryPendingReservations` (transient → leave pending; permanent → flip to `failed_permanent`).
│   │   ├── fortigateLocationService.ts # Discovery-time `fetchFortigateSysLocation()`: REST `GET /api/v2/cmdb/system.snmp/sysinfo` against a FortiGate via the existing FMG-proxy / standalone transport (reuses `callFortiOs` + `buildTransportForIntegration` from reservationPushService). Returns the trimmed sysLocation string or null. Chosen over an SNMP get to avoid a separate credential resolver / net-snmp session and to work in FMG proxy mode where Polaris can't reach the FortiGate's management IP directly. Never throws — REST failures come back as null so the discovery sync continues with no location update.
│   │   ├── geocoderService.ts         # Address-string → lat/lng geocoder backed by OpenStreetMap Nominatim. `geocode(query)` normalizes the input (trim + collapse-whitespace + lowercase), consults `GeocodeCache` (90-day TTL; positive AND negative results cached), and on cache-miss hits Nominatim through a process-global 1 req/sec rate limiter (module-level chained Promise enforces the gap). User-Agent identifies Polaris per Nominatim's usage policy. Transport failures (timeout / non-2xx / parse error) do NOT poison the cache; only successful responses (including empty result arrays — the negative-cache signal) write rows. Never throws. Used by the FortiGate coord-resolution chain in `syncDhcpSubnets` Phase 3.
│   │   ├── fortigateCoordPushService.ts # `pushCoordsToFortigate()` — write-back orchestrator for geocoded coords. FMG mode writes to BOTH per-device metavars `Latitude` / `Longitude` (native FMG `update` on `/dvmdb/adom/<adom>/device/<n>` with `{"meta fields": {...}}`) AND CMDB `gui-device-latitude` / `gui-device-longitude` (native FMG `update` on `/pm/config/device/<n>/global/system/global`). Standalone FortiGate writes only CMDB via direct REST PUT on `/api/v2/cmdb/system/global`. Best-effort: per-target failures are logged and reflected in the returned `{ok, targets[], error?}` shape but never throw. FMG-mode CMDB writes land in FMG's CMDB but require an operator Install Device Configuration in FMG to reach the live FortiGate — Polaris explicitly does not trigger installs.
│   │   ├── subnetRefreshService.ts   # Per-subnet "refresh from device" action (Refresh button in the IP panel slide-in). Queries the originating FortiGate for ONE DHCP scope (CMDB reservations + live leases) via the same transport reservationPushService uses, reconciles against Polaris's `dhcp_reservation` + `dhcp_lease` rows on this subnet (manual / VIP rows untouched), and bumps `Subnet.lastDiscoveredAt`. Narrower than `discoverDhcpSubnets` — doesn't recompute asset sightings / decommissions / map regions / etc.; those reconcile on the next full integration cycle. Writes one `subnet.refresh` Event.
│   │   ├── reservationStaleService.ts # Stale-reservation detection: settings (staleAfterDays), alert lister (active|ignored), snooze/ignore mutators, and the job entry point that emits reservation.stale Events
│   │   ├── dnsResolvedReservationService.ts # Auto-creates Reservation rows with `sourceType="dns_resolved"` for every Asset with an `ipAddress` that falls inside a non-deprecated Subnet and has no authoritative (manual / dhcp_reservation / dhcp_lease / interface_ip / vip / fortiswitch / fortinap / fortimanager / fortigate) reservation already there. Carries the asset's hostname (`hostname || dnsName`) and `macAddress` when known; `createdBy="system:dns-resolved"`. Exports `reconcileDnsResolvedForAsset(assetId)` (the per-asset path called from the Prisma extension hook in `src/db.ts` post-write + from the periodic job per-row), `reconcileDnsResolvedForAllAssets()` (the periodic sweep — batches of 25 via Promise.all), `releaseDnsResolvedForAsset(assetId)` (asset-delete hook), and `releaseDnsResolvedAt(subnetId, ipAddress)` (the discovery hand-off used by `syncDhcpSubnets` and `reservationService.createReservation` right before creating an authoritative row at the same target). Eligible asset statuses: active / maintenance / storage / quarantined. IPv4 only. Defers silently to authoritative entries — never raises Conflict rows, never pushes to FortiGates.
│   │   ├── utilizationService.ts    # Utilization reporting. `getGlobalUtilization` backs the legacy `/utilization` route and is also reused by the new Dashboard summary. `getRecentManualReservations` is the new helper that powers the Dashboard's "Recently Reserved" card — same `findMany` shape as `recentReservations` but filtered to `sourceType="manual"` so DHCP discoveries / leases / VIP echoes don't crowd it out, with `{id,cidr,name,vlan}` hydrated on the subnet relation so the card renders without a second fetch.
│   │   ├── fortimanagerService.ts   # FMG JSON-RPC client & discovery orchestration. All FMG-bound calls (rpc, fmgProxyRest, resolveDeviceMgmtIp, ...) accept an optional `integrationId` and route through `getFmgWorker(integrationId)` so cross-feature traffic (discovery + reservation push + quarantine push + manual proxy + connection test) stays serial against FMG's "one-request-at-a-time" constraint.
│   │   ├── fortigateService.ts      # Standalone FortiGate REST API client & discovery. **Per-device REST queries fan out across 6 independent chains via Promise.all** so per-FortiGate wall-clock is `max(longest chain)` instead of `sum(all 12 steps)`. Chains (after Steps 1 + 2): A) DHCP CMDB → Promise.all(DHCP monitor + interface IPs); B) device inventory; C) managed switches → managed APs → AP-port mapping (serial intra-chain — 3e.5 needs both 3d and 3e); D) ARP table; E) geo coordinates; F) firewall VIPs. Peak intra-device REST concurrency is ~6 simultaneous calls; small-branch FortiGates (60F/61F class) handle this fine, and each step keeps its own try/catch so a slow individual query doesn't tank the whole device. Used by the standalone FortiGate integration directly AND by `fortimanagerService` in direct mode (`useProxy=false`) which delegates per-device work to `fortigateService.discoverDhcpSubnets`.
│   │   ├── fmgWorker.ts             # Per-integration FortiManager worker with two lanes. Module-level `Map<integrationId, FmgWorker>`, lazily created on first submit. The FMG concurrency constraint is specifically about `/sys/proxy/json` (the passthrough that forwards calls through to managed FortiGates) — FMG drops parallel proxy connections past 1-2 for the same API user. Native endpoints (`/pm/config/...`, `/dvmdb/...`, auth) hit FMG's own DB and don't share that constraint. Lanes: **proxy lane** is strict single-consumer FIFO (concurrency=1) and carries every `/sys/proxy/json` call; **native lane** is unbounded and just tracks inflight count for observability. Dispatch happens inside `rpc()` (fortimanagerService) by inspecting the JSON-RPC payload's first param URL; callers use `submitProxy` / `submitNative` explicitly. Cross-feature serialization still holds for proxy calls (discovery per-device monitor scrapes, proxy-mode reservation push, proxy-mode quarantine push, manual `/sys/proxy/json` proxy endpoint all share one slot); native calls (mgmt-IP resolves, CMDB scrapes, the device roster) parallelize freely. Exports `polaris_fmg_worker_queue_depth{integrationId}` + `polaris_fmg_worker_inflight{integrationId}` (proxy lane) and `polaris_fmg_worker_native_inflight{integrationId}` (native lane) so operators can see which lane is the bottleneck.
│   │   ├── windowsServerService.ts  # Windows Server WinRM DHCP discovery
│   │   ├── entraIdService.ts        # Microsoft Entra ID + Intune device discovery via Graph
│   │   ├── activeDirectoryService.ts # On-premise Active Directory computer discovery via LDAP/LDAPS
│   │   ├── searchService.ts         # Global typeahead search (classifies IP/CIDR/MAC/text; parallel entity queries)
│   │   ├── allocationTemplateService.ts # Saved multi-subnet allocation templates (Setting-backed)
│   │   ├── autoMonitorInterfacesService.ts # "Auto-Monitor Interfaces" feature: pure resolver over a multi-block union (byNames / byPatterns / byTypes / byLldp) + DB-bound aggregate/preview/apply for the FMG/FortiGate Monitoring tab. By LLDP pins any interface whose neighbor — direct `AssetLldpNeighbor.matchedAssetId` OR peer-inferred edge from `Asset.fortinetTopology` — resolves to a monitored asset of one of the selected types. The peer-inferred half (`loadInferredLldpByAsset` — class-aware raw SQL over `fortinetTopology->>'controllerFortigate'` / `parentSwitch` / etc.) covers managed FortiAPs whose FortiSwitch silently consumes LLDP without re-publishing via SNMP LLDP-MIB; same data source as the inferred Neighbor column on the System tab (see `peerInferredLldpService`). For the `fortiap` class, a post-load step (`normalizeFortiapInferredLldp`) rewrites synthesized `localIfName` from FortiAP-CLI naming (`lan1`) to SNMP-canonical (`eth0`) when the AP's interface table actually exposes the eth* form — without this, the resolver would pin a name that doesn't match any ifIndex on the fast-cadence scrape. UI label reads **By LLDP (includes inferred interfaces)** so operators see at a glance that the match set isn't LLDP-protocol-only. `compilePattern` dispatches wildcards vs raw regex per block. `coerceLegacySelection` rewrites the older single-mode discriminated-union shape (`{mode, ...}`) on read so the Zod parser + apply route + frontend renderer all keep working until the one-shot migration sweeps stored configs. Strictly additive to Asset.monitoredInterfaces.
│   │   ├── assetIpHistoryService.ts # Asset IP history reads, retention settings, pruning (Setting-backed)
│   │   ├── discoveryDurationService.ts # Rolling discovery-duration samples + "slow-run" threshold (Setting-backed)
│   │   ├── azureAuthService.ts      # Azure AD/Entra SAML SSO, user provisioning
│   │   ├── totpService.ts           # RFC 6238 TOTP secret / code / backup-code helpers
│   │   ├── dnsService.ts            # Reverse DNS lookup for assets
│   │   ├── ouiService.ts            # MAC OUI lookup with admin overrides
│   │   ├── deviceIconService.ts     # Operator-uploaded device icons. Validates uploads (PNG/JPEG/WebP/SVG with magic-byte and SVG-security checks; 256 KB raster cap, 32 KB SVG cap), stores bytes in the DeviceIcon model, exposes resolveIconUrl(asset, cache) for the topology endpoint to stamp `iconUrl` on each node. **SVG uploads are transparently rasterized to 512×512 PNG** via `@resvg/resvg-js` (`rasterizeSvgToPng`) before storage — Cytoscape's `background-image` pipeline loads images through `new Image()` and design-tool SVG exports (Adobe Illustrator etc.) typically omit `width`/`height` and declare only `viewBox`, so the browser falls back to a tiny default natural size and the topology icon ends up anchored upper-left at a fixed pixel size at every zoom. Rasterizing on the server bypasses the whole class of bug. `rasterizeStoredSvgIcons()` is the matching backfill helper called from the one-shot startup job, idempotent via the mimeType filter; legacy `image/svg+xml` rows are migrated in place to PNG.
│   │   ├── eventArchiveService.ts   # Syslog (CEF) + SFTP/SCP event archival
│   │   ├── projectionDriftService.ts # Projection drift detection. After every successful AssetSource upsert, compares projectAssetFromSources output against current Asset values; logs disagreements to pino with `event: "asset.projection.drift"`. Best-effort and fire-and-forget.
│   │   ├── serverSettingsService.ts # HTTPS, branding, backup/restore
│   │   ├── credentialService.ts     # Named credential store (SNMP / WinRM / SSH) with masking + secret-preservation merge
│   │   ├── manufacturerAliasService.ts # Manufacturer alias map: CRUD + cache + idempotent default seed + backfill of existing Asset/MibFile rows
│   │   ├── assetTypeService.ts        # AssetTypeDef registry: CRUD + cache refresh + idempotent built-in seed. `listAssetTypes({withUsage})` joins `prisma.asset.groupBy` for the Manage UI's per-row "N assets" badges. Rename path is a `$transaction` that rewrites every Asset row holding the old name to the new name atomically with the registry-row update; delete refuses while any Asset still references the type. Built-in rows (`isProtected=true`) are immutable. After every mutation calls `refreshCache()` so synchronous `isKnownAssetType()` in `src/utils/assetTypes.ts` sees the new state immediately.
│   │   ├── mibService.ts            # SNMP MIB module storage + minimal SMI parser. `parseMib` validates uploads (extracts moduleName + IMPORTS) on the upload hot path. `parseMibStructured` is the heavier peer used by the Browse modal + MIB-aware walk: extracts SYNTAX (with INTEGER enum value labels), MAX-ACCESS / ACCESS, STATUS, DESCRIPTION, INDEX clauses, and SEQUENCE OF table structure. Per-symbol parse failures degrade to "name + parentName known, everything else null" rather than dropping the symbol — operators can still walk it, they just don't get decoding. Parses on demand; `oidRegistry` already keeps every MIB's contents in memory after `ensureLoaded()`.
│   │   ├── mibParserUtils.ts        # Shared `stripComments` helper consumed by both `mibService` and `oidRegistry` so the comment-aware string-literal scanner doesn't drift between the two parsers.
│   │   ├── oidRegistry.ts           # Symbolic name → numeric OID resolver. Per-asset **scoped** resolution: device-specific MIBs override vendor-wide MIBs override generic MIBs override the built-in SMI seed. Each scope is computed lazily and cached; cache + parsed entries refresh on MIB upload/delete and at app startup. Tracks per-symbol provenance so the UI can show which MIB provided each resolved name. Exposes `resolveSymbolsForMib(mibId)` and `resolveSymbolForMib(mibId, name)` so the MIB Browse + Walk endpoints can resolve every symbol declared by a chosen MIB at the MIB's natural scope (its own manufacturer + model layer, falling back through vendor-only and generic) without re-implementing the layer pass.
│   │   ├── manufacturerProfileService.ts # CRUD + module-cached resolver for the editable `ManufacturerProfile` data model (Slice 6a). `refreshProfileCache()` is called at boot by `seedManufacturerProfiles` and after every write; `getProfileFor(manufacturer)` is the sync getter the hot probe path calls. Exposes `listProfiles` / `getProfile` / `createProfile` / `updateMetricRow` / `createOverride` / `updateOverride` / `deleteOverride` / `createWidget` / `updateWidget` / `deleteWidget` / `deleteProfile` consumed by `src/api/routes/manufacturerProfiles.ts`. Manufacturer strings are alias-canonicalized on create, regex `modelPattern` validity is checked at write time, and transform kinds are validated against the registry in `src/utils/symbolTransforms.ts`. **Memory composition validation** via `parseMemoryComposition` (public export) — enforces per-shape required fields for the multi-OID memory `composition` column on metric rows and overrides; metric rows other than `memory` reject a non-null composition with 400 so a typo in the UI can't persist nonsense onto the wrong row.
│   │   ├── vendorTelemetryProfiles.ts # Per-vendor SNMP CPU/memory/temperature/disk profile registry. Maps `manufacturer + os + model` regex → symbolic OID names; resolved through oidRegistry at probe time. Seeded into the editable `ManufacturerProfile` model on first boot (Slice 6a); the runtime probe path now consults the DB via `pickVendorProfileMerged` (Slice 6c) which layers operator-edited symbol choices + per-model overrides on top of a clone of the hardcoded entry. Operator-untouched profiles preserve full parity (seeded `defaultSymbol` == hardcoded primary symbol) so existing fleets see identical probe behavior. The constant stays as a fresh-install fallback while the DB cache warms up. Transforms from `symbolTransforms.ts` are not yet applied at sample-write time — that plumbing lands in a follow-up. Profile shape carries optional `cpu` / `memory` / `disk` / `temperature` queries — temperature is a single-scalar form for vendors whose hardware publishes one Celsius reading instead of the table-based ENTITY-SENSOR-MIB walk (consumed by `collectTemperaturesSnmp` as a third fallback after ENTITY-SENSOR-MIB + the Fortinet `fgHwSensorTable` sensor-name heuristic). Built-ins for Cisco / Juniper / Mikrotik / Fortinet FortiSwitch (matched before generic Fortinet via `model="FortiSwitch"`; CPU uses `fsSysCpuUsage` @ 12356.106.4.1.2 scalar percent; memory uses bytes form via `fsSysMemUsage` @ 12356.106.4.1.3 + `fsSysMemCapacity` @ 12356.106.4.1.4 — NOT a percent like the FortiGate variant — all three seeded into oidRegistry so the probe works without uploading FORTINET-FORTISWITCH-MIB) / Fortinet FortiAP (matched before generic Fortinet via `model="FortiAP"`; distinct OID root @ 12356.120 — FortiAPs don't expose anything under FortiGate's 12356.101 or FortiSwitch's 12356.106; CPU = `fapCpuUsage` @ 12356.120.3.41 scalar percent; memory = `fapMemoryUsage` @ 12356.120.3.42 scalar percent (single-value form like FortiGate, NOT FortiSwitch's bytes form); temperature = `fapTemperature` @ 12356.120.3.44 scalar Celsius; all three seeded into oidRegistry so the probe works without uploading FORTINET-FORTIAP-MIB) / Fortinet (SNMP path) / HP-Aruba / Dell.
│   │   ├── monitorOverrideService.ts # Asset monitor-override semantics. Replaces the legacy `monitoredOperatorSet` one-way sticky flag with `Asset.monitorOverride`, a convergent boolean that is true exactly when the operator's current `monitored` choice diverges from the discovering integration's per-class `addAsMonitored`. Exports: pure helpers `getAddAsMonitoredFromConfig(integrationType, integrationConfig, assetType)` (JSON-path lookup of `<klass>Monitor.addAsMonitored` per the 5-class map), `computeMonitorOverride(monitored, addAsMonitored)` (XOR), `buildMonitoredSweep(addAsMonitored, existing)` (returns `{monitored: bool}` or `{}` for discovery-side stamps, gated on the existing override flag), `snapshotAddAsMonitoredByAssetType(integrationType, integrationConfig)` (5-way snapshot used by the integration-save change detector and the preflight endpoint); plus async SQL helpers `recomputeMonitorOverrideForAssets(prisma, assetIds[])` (operator-write-path post-hook — single SQL UPDATE), `sweepMonitoredForIntegration(prisma, integrationId)` (integration-save sweep — `monitored = addAsMonitored` for every non-override asset under this integration, scoped via the JSON path), `recomputeMonitorOverrideForIntegration(prisma, integrationId)` (full-integration override refresh, runs after the sweep). All SQL paths use the same `assets.config #>> '{<klass>Monitor,addAsMonitored}'` JSON-path technique the cutover migration uses — keep these in sync.
│   │   ├── monitoringService.ts     # Authenticated response-time probes (fortimanager/fortigate/snmp/winrm/ssh/icmp) + System tab telemetry (CPU/memory) + system-info (interfaces/storage) collection. runMonitorPass dispatches all three cadences; per-stream tier-aware retention prune helpers. **Prune layer runs three tiers per stream**: `pruneMonitorSamples` / `pruneTelemetrySamples` / `pruneSystemInfoSamples` each call `pruneOneTable` once per (table × tier) — detail (column `timestamp`), hourly + daily (column `bucketStart`) — with retention values pulled from `getSampleRetention()` (per-class: `default` / `switch` / `accessPoint`). LLDP neighbors ride the systemInfo detail retention since they're current-state, not time-series. Each tier's hypertable benefits from `dropChunks` as the fast pre-filter before the per-class `deleteMany` residue pass. **Sample writes are async-buffered**: every `record*` function (`recordProbeResult` / `recordTelemetryResult` / `recordSystemInfoResult` / `recordFastFilteredResult`) hands sample rows to `sampleWriteBuffer.enqueue*` instead of issuing per-call `prisma.<table>.create*` — those are batched into one `createMany` per 2 s flush window. **The probe-path Asset.update (status/counters/last*At) is also buffered** via `probePatchBuffer.enqueueProbePatch` — one bulk `UPDATE … FROM (VALUES …)` per 2 s window covers every asset's state write instead of one `prisma.asset.update` per probe. The five-state machine reads its own writes via `getPendingProbePatch(assetId)`: `recordProbeResult` overlays the in-flight patch onto the loaded Asset row BEFORE computing the next state, so the threshold counters advance correctly across same-window probes. Telemetry/systemInfo cadences still issue per-asset `prisma.asset.update` for `lastTelemetryAt` / `lastSystemInfoAt` — those run on slower cadences (60 s / 600 s+) where per-tick connection pressure is much lower; if needed, the same buffer pattern applies straightforwardly. Three writes stay synchronous because they need per-asset replace semantics: the `$transaction` for `assetAssociatedIp` (delete-non-manual + createMany inside `recordSystemInfoResult`), `persistLldpNeighbors` (per-asset full-replace of the LLDP neighbor table with a 48 h stickiness grace), and `persistWirelessStations` (per-scrape full-replace of the connected-client table; no stickiness because clients are transient by design). The LLDP and wireless paths use a single bulk `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE` raw-SQL statement (plus a separate `deleteMany` for stale rows) so the connection isn't held across N pipelined updates inside a transaction — caller pre-assigns ids in JS so insert-vs-update is controlled by id collision, not by the business-key unique constraint which can't be used as a conflict target since Postgres treats NULL as distinct in unique indexes. **Probe path issues exactly one Asset findUnique** — `probeAsset(assetId, out?)` stashes its loaded asset row into the caller's optional out-object so `recordProbeResult(assetId, result, preloadedAsset?)` can skip its own findUnique on the state-machine update path. The hot loop (runProbeFor) and pg-boss probe workers both pass the out-object; the /probe-now route doesn't bother (one operator-triggered request per click). **LLDP asset match index is module-cached** with a 60 s TTL — `persistLldpNeighbors` used to call `buildLldpAssetMatchIndex` on every system-info pass (`findMany` over every Asset row plus its associatedIpRows + macAddressRows), now goes through `getLldpAssetMatchIndex()` which dedups concurrent rebuilders via an inflight Promise and returns the cached map within the TTL window. Worst-case stale-cache effect is one cycle of "LLDP neighbor matched to wrong asset" which self-corrects on the next scrape; `invalidateLldpMatchCache()` is exported for future callers that want explicit refresh after a bulk asset write.
│   │   ├── sampleWriteBuffer.ts     # Periodic batch-flush buffer for the six append-only monitor sample tables (asset_monitor_samples / asset_telemetry_samples / asset_temperature_samples / asset_interface_samples / asset_storage_samples / asset_ipsec_tunnel_samples). Per-table in-memory arrays + a 2 s `setInterval` flush + a 5,000-row size threshold for burst publishers (e.g. a manual probe-all-now). `flushTable` snapshots the array up front so concurrent enqueues during the awaited `createMany` land in a fresh buffer; on flush failure the snapshot is re-prepended for the next tick's retry. Deadlock retries go through `retryOnDeadlock` (3 attempts, 25-75 ms jitter — same as `reconcileMacAddresses`). Flush duration is observed via the existing `polaris_sample_write_duration_seconds{table}` histogram; current depth per table is exported as `polaris_sample_buffer_depth{table}`. Trade-off: up to 2 s of sample rows are lost on a hard crash — acceptable because samples are an append-only time series and the next cadence tick re-supplies fresh data. **State-side writes (Asset.monitorStatus / counters / lastMonitorAt / monitorStatusChangedAt) live in the companion `probePatchBuffer` with last-write-wins-with-stamp-preservation semantics** instead of this append-only buffer; same 2 s window but different shape because state needs replace semantics and the buffer needs to serve reads (the state machine's threshold counters require read-your-writes). `shutdownFlushSampleBuffers()` is awaited from the SIGTERM/SIGINT hook in `src/app.ts` so a graceful restart doesn't drop the in-flight buffer. **The buffer covers only the six DETAIL tables — the twelve rollup tables (`*_hourly` / `*_daily`) use `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` from `sampleRollupService.ts` instead** because rollup writes need upsert semantics (idempotent re-runs over the same window must rewrite buckets in place); the append-only buffer contract intentionally has no upsert path.
│   │   ├── probePatchBuffer.ts      # Companion to sampleWriteBuffer for the STATE-side write inside `recordProbeResult`. Replaces the per-probe `prisma.asset.update` (one Prisma pool connection per probe; the dominant remaining per-tick connection draw on the monitor hot loop at 2000+ assets) with an in-memory `Map<assetId, ProbePatch>` flushed as one bulk `UPDATE … FROM (VALUES …)` statement per 2 s window. Last-write-wins per asset within the window EXCEPT `monitorStatusChangedAt`, which is preserved on merge so a transition stamped by probe N isn't NULL-erased by a subsequent same-window probe that didn't transition. The state machine reads its own writes via `getPendingProbePatch(assetId)` — `recordProbeResult` overlays the in-flight patch onto the loaded Asset row BEFORE computing the next state, so two failed probes in the same window correctly advance `consecutiveFailures` to 2 instead of both computing it as 1 against the on-disk 0. On flush-retry-exhausted failure, the snapshot is conditionally re-prepended (only entries the buffer no longer holds — concurrent enqueues represent fresher state and win). Trade-off: up to 2 s of state writes lost on hard crash, and the `monitor.status_changed` Event / `propagateAfterStatusChange` / `triggerRetryAfterStatusChange` side effects that fire on transitions run against in-memory `(previousStatus, nextStatus)` values and don't wait for the flush — propagate/retry hooks then read the DB before the buffer lands, so their effects can lag by up to one flush window before the 60 s `dependencyReconciler` and `retryQueuedReservationPushes` ticks catch up as the sources of truth. Acceptable because the state machine is self-healing within one additional probe cycle. Exports `polaris_probe_patch_buffer_depth{table=assets:probe}` gauge + `polaris_probe_patch_write_duration_seconds` histogram. `shutdownFlushProbePatchBuffer()` awaited from the SIGTERM/SIGINT hook so a graceful restart doesn't drop in-flight state.
│   │   ├── sampleRollupService.ts   # Tiered sample-retention rollup writer. Builds twelve `INSERT...ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` statements (6 source tables × hourly + daily) that aggregate raw detail samples into per-hour and per-day rollup tables. Portable across plain Postgres + TimescaleDB via `date_trunc('hour' | 'day', ts)` — single code path on both deployments. Gauge tables (monitor, telemetry, temperature, storage) emit `sampleCount + avg/min/max` per bucket; counter tables (interface, ipsec) emit per-bucket `first/last` counter endpoints + `lastBucketSampleAt` so the read layer derives rate as `(last - first) / (lastBucketSampleAt - bucketStart in seconds)` with negative-delta-as-counter-reset behavior matching the detail-tier client-side diff. IPsec additionally counts per-status sample-bucket occurrences so the chart distinguishes flapping from steady-state. Daily reads from `*_hourly` (not detail) so the daily tick stays bounded on big fleets. Driven by `src/jobs/runSampleRollup.ts`.
│   │   ├── sampleQueryRouter.ts     # Tier router. `pickSampleTier(since, retention)` returns `{tier: "detail"|"hourly"|"daily", bucketSeconds}` based on which tier still covers the requested window. `pickSampleTierForAsset(assetId, stream, since)` is the async hot-path helper used by the six chart history endpoints — looks up the asset's `assetType`, resolves the matching retention class (`default` / `switch` / `accessPoint`) via `pickClassForAssetType` from `sampleRetentionService`, and applies `pickSampleTier` against the asset's specific (detailDays, hourlyDays). Costs one indexed PK lookup on Asset + a cache-friendly retention read per chart request.
│   │   ├── sampleHistoryService.ts  # Tier-aware readers for the six chart history endpoints. Each function takes (assetId, since, until, tier[, extraKey]) and returns `{samples, [meta], [stats]}` in a shape the existing chart renderers consume. Detail tier returns raw sample columns; rollup tiers translate aggregate columns back to the source-table field names so gauge charts (monitor, telemetry, temperature, storage) render unchanged — values become bucket averages with `min*` / `max*` siblings + `sampleCount` for tooltips. Counter tables (interface, ipsec) pre-compute `inBytesPerSec` / `outBytesPerSec` / `inErrorsPerSec` / `outErrorsPerSec` (and IPsec analogs) on rollup tiers so the frontend can branch on `bucketSeconds > 0` and read rates directly instead of diffing cumulative samples.
│   │   ├── sampleRetentionService.ts # Global sample-retention policy. `Setting("sampleRetention")` holds a flat `{stream: {tier: {class: days}}}` shape (3 streams × 3 tiers × 3 classes = 27 numbers). Defaults 7/30/365 across the board. `getSampleRetention()` reads with a 5-second in-process cache (chart endpoints read on every request); `updateSampleRetention(input)` merges partial updates + invalidates the cache. `pickClassForAssetType` maps `Asset.assetType` to the matching retention class (`switch` / `accessPoint` / `default`). Retention was pulled out of the per-tier monitor-settings hierarchy in phase 5 of the tiered sample-retention work — cadence / polling / credentials / MIB hints / timeouts still live there, only retention is global. Edited from Server Settings → Maintenance → Sample Retention card; consumed by the prune layer (`monitoringService.pruneXxxSamples`) and the chart tier router. Legacy single-tier retention keys (`sampleRetentionDays` etc. in `manualMonitorSettings`/`Integration.config.monitorSettings`) are still present but unused going forward; consolidated into the new Setting by `consolidateSampleRetention` startup job on first boot.
│   │   ├── capacityService.ts       # Capacity snapshot: host (cpu/ram + per-volume disk free), DB sample-table breakdown, monitoring workload + steady-state size projection, severity grading (ok/watch/amber/red). Volume scan walks every distinct filesystem Polaris/Postgres write to (app dir, state dir, backups dir, plus the PostgreSQL data directory when DB is co-located — resolved authoritatively via `SHOW data_directory`, falling back to platform-conventional paths like `/var/lib/pgsql/...` when SHOW fails because the application's DB role lacks `pg_read_all_settings`), deduped by stat.dev so single-LV installs collapse cleanly and STIG-style multi-LV layouts surface each filesystem once. `recordCapacityTransition()` compares against a stored `capacity.lastSeverity` Setting and writes one `capacity.severity_changed` Event on each transition (any direction), routed out through eventArchiveService → syslog/SFTP so an alert reaches on-call before the DB dies. Feeds the Maintenance tab Capacity card and the sidebar critical alert via the /server-settings/pg-tuning endpoint. Snapshot also carries `database.timescale: { extensionInstalled, hypertableTables[] }` and a `timescale_recommended` watch-severity reason that fires when sample tables exceed 1 GB without the extension installed; reason text adapts via `deploymentContext` heuristics (local-DB vs remote/managed, bare-metal vs container). Also carries `database.connectionPool: { currentInUse, peakObserved, prismaPoolSize, pgbossPoolSize, maxConnections }` — `currentInUse` from `pg_stat_activity` at snapshot time; `peakObserved` is a rolling high-water mark tracked in module-local memory (resets on process restart); `prismaPoolSize` and `pgbossPoolSize` are the configured Polaris pool capacities (DATABASE_POOL_SIZE / POLARIS_PGBOSS_POOL_SIZE; pgboss is null in cursor mode); `maxConnections` from `SHOW max_connections`. Drives the `db_pool_undersized` watch-severity reason that fires when peak ≥80% of the combined Polaris pool capacity; suggestion text points operators at the Capacity Advisor card for the actual recommended pool sizes. The three advisor-driven reasons — `monitor_workers_undersized` (watch — single rollup naming the worst worker gap), `max_connections_undersized` (amber — fires when the advisor recommends a higher max_connections than is currently set), and `monitor_handler_timeout_pressure` (watch — fires when observed p90 ≥ 70% of `pgboss.queue.expire_seconds` on any monitor cadence) — are populated by `getCapacitySnapshotWithAdvisor()`, the two-pass orchestrator helper that builds an initial snapshot, runs the Capacity Advisor against it, then rebuilds with the advisor's gap data in scope so the reasons fire. The legacy `pgboss_recommended` / `pgboss_overdue` / `pgboss_pending` reasons + their inline manual "Enable on next restart" buttons were folded into the Capacity Advisor's QUEUE_MODE lever and removed from this service. The local `SAMPLE_TABLES` enumeration (capacity-domain, distinct from `timescaleService.SAMPLE_TABLES`) lists all 18 tables — 6 detail + 6 hourly + 6 daily — each tagged with `(stream, tier, countKey)` so the steady-state projection multiplies `monitored asset count × rows-per-asset-per-day × retentionDays × bytes/row` for every tier independently. Retention values come from `Setting("sampleRetention")` via `getSampleRetention()` (per-class per-tier — projection uses the `default` class) instead of the legacy single-tier `monitor.sampleRetentionDays` etc. Snapshot also carries `database.rollupLastSuccess: { hourly, daily }` (timestamps from `Setting("sampleRollup.<tier>.lastSuccess")`) which drives the new `sample_rollup_lagging` watch reason — fires when hourly is >6 h stale or daily is >36 h stale AND any sample table has rows, catching a wedged rollup before long-range chart queries silently fall back to detail-table scans.
│   │   ├── capacityAdvisorService.ts # Capacity Advisor: derives recommended values for connection-pool sizes, monitor worker counts (per-cadence: probe / fastFiltered / telemetry+systemInfo / **lldp** / **storage** / floating — LLDP and Storage levers added in Phase 2 alongside the queue carve-out), queue mode (cursor ↔ pg-boss), PostgreSQL `max_connections`, and PostgreSQL tuning settings from observable workload (monitored asset count, monitored interface count, per-class FortiGate count, per-cadence pass-duration P90 read from the `polaris_monitor_work_duration_seconds` histogram, observed peak connections, host RAM). Also reads `pgboss.queue.expire_seconds` for the four monitor cadences (live from the DB, so the advisor reflects what's actually in effect — not just what `EXPIRE_BY_QUEUE` in queueService.ts would write on a fresh install); surfaces the value alongside p90 in each per-cadence worker tooltip, and rolls cadences whose p90 ≥ 70% of expire_seconds into `handlerTimeoutPressure` for the capacity reason of the same name. `buildAdvisorState(inputs)` is the pure calc; `recomputeAdvisorFromSnapshot(snap, pgTuning)` is the async wrapper that loads integration counts + cadence percentiles + applicable-asset SQL counts + queue timeouts and calls into it. Cached in module memory with a 10-min TTL refreshed by `capacityWatch` and on demand by the GET endpoint. **Advisory-only** — operator clicks Stage in the UI to write env-driven recommendations to `.env` via `setEnvVar` (restart-required to take effect). `max_connections` and PostgreSQL settings stay display-only because they require a PostgreSQL restart Polaris can't trigger. Calc chain (also covered in the plan file): `workersNeeded[c] = ceil(applicable × p90 × 1.5 / cadenceInterval)`, floored at `perCadenceFloor = clamp(ceil(monitoredAssetCount × 0.5), 4, 24)` per cadence (so 6-asset installs floor at 4, 48+-asset installs cap at 24 — the floor is a "modest burst absorber" sized to the fleet rather than a fixed 24); `floatingWorkers = max(floatingFloor, ceil(0.25 × Σ))` where `floatingFloor = clamp(ceil(monitoredAssetCount × 0.5), 4, 32)`; `prismaTarget = workerCeiling + discoveryReserve + httpOverhead`; `DATABASE_POOL_SIZE = roundUpToNearest(max(prismaTarget, current), 50)`; `POLARIS_PGBOSS_POOL_SIZE = roundUpToNearest(max(pgbossTarget, current), 50)`; `recommendedMax = roundUpToNearest(ceil((effectivePrisma + effectivePgboss) / 0.65), 50)`. Cold start substitutes `COLD_START_P90_SEC = { probe: 0.4, fastFiltered: 0.6, telemetry: 0.8, systemInfo: 1.0 }` when histogram sample count is < 100 per cadence; the UI shows a "cold-start" badge per affected row. Never recommends shrinking — `changeRequired` is true only when recommended > current.
│   │   ├── timescaleService.ts      # TimescaleDB detection + hypertable utilities. `detectTimescale()` runs once at startup (warmed in src/app.ts) and queries `pg_extension` + `timescaledb_information.hypertables` to populate a module-level cache; `isTimescaleAvailable()` and `isHypertable(name)` are sync getters used by the prune layer and capacityService. `dropChunks(table, olderThan)` is the chunk-granular fast path used as a pre-filter before the per-class `deleteMany` in `pruneOneTable` — no-op when the target table isn't a hypertable, so callers don't need to branch. `migrateToHypertables()` runs at boot (after detection) and idempotently converts all eighteen sample + rollup tables to hypertables (`create_hypertable(if_not_exists => TRUE, migrate_data => TRUE)`). Source tables partition by `timestamp`; rollup tables partition by `bucketStart`. Each table gets column compression with `compress_segmentby = "assetId"` + `compress_orderby` matching the partition column, and the compression policy with the `TIMESCALE_COMPRESS_AFTER_DAYS` window (default 7). Source tables carry a composite PK on `(id, timestamp)` and rollup tables on `(id, bucketStart)` to satisfy Timescale's "all UNIQUE/PK constraints must include the partitioning column" rule — works fine on plain Postgres too. The canonical lists are exported as `SAMPLE_TABLES` (6 sources), `ROLLUP_TABLES` (12 rollups), and `ALL_HYPERTABLE_CANDIDATES` (the union); capacityService consumes the union for `database.timescale.hypertableTables` enumeration.
│   │   ├── queueService.ts          # Monitor work queue mode + pg-boss runtime. Two implementations: `cursor` (default — in-process worker pool inside runMonitorPass; fits small/medium fleets fine after the Step 4a split-tick fix) and `pgboss` (durable queue with per-cadence worker pools — recommended once the fleet crosses ~500 monitored assets). Active mode lives in `Setting.monitor.queueMode`; captured at boot via `initializeQueue()` and frozen as the running process's mode regardless of subsequent Setting writes (operator changes take effect on next restart). `startPgbossWorkers()` boots pg-boss (only when boot-time mode is `pgboss`), creates **six** queues with `policy: "singleton"` + per-asset singletonKey for natural coalescing (duplicate `(assetId, cadence)` sends while a job is queued or active are absorbed silently; different assetIds run in parallel up to `localConcurrency`), and registers worker handlers that call back into the same `runFooFor()` functions the cursor pass uses. **Phase 2 carve-out**: LLDP and Storage each get their own queue (`polaris-monitor-lldp` / `polaris-monitor-storage`) and dedicated worker pool (default 12 workers each, env `POLARIS_MONITOR_LLDP_WORKERS` / `POLARIS_MONITOR_STORAGE_WORKERS`) running `runLldpFor` / `runStorageFor`. The publisher in `monitorAssets.ts` checks `Asset.lastLldpAt + lldpIntervalSeconds` / `Asset.lastStorageAt + storageIntervalSeconds` against the per-asset resolved cadence to decide when each is due. The legacy `collectSystemInfo` still walks LLDP and storage as session-coalesced side effects when interfaces + LLDP / storage resolve to the same polling method; the dedicated queues drive their own independent cadence and `persistLldpNeighbors` / `enqueueStorageSamples` are idempotent against double-walks. Cursor-mode installs keep LLDP + Storage on the legacy systemInfo path — the dedicated queues are pg-boss-only. `publishMonitorJob(cadence, assetId, transport?)` is the publisher entry point; `monitorAssets.ts` calls it from the same two ticking loops it uses for cursor — only the dispatch differs. Worker architecture: 24 dedicated workers per queue (probe / fastFiltered / telemetry / systemInfo, configurable via `POLARIS_MONITOR_PROBE_WORKERS` / `POLARIS_MONITOR_FAST_WORKERS` / `POLARIS_MONITOR_HEAVY_WORKERS` — the heavy var sets both telemetry and systemInfo) plus a **floating worker pool** (default 32, env `POLARIS_MONITOR_FLOATING_WORKERS`) that polls all four queues in priority order via `boss.fetch()`. The floating pool flows idle capacity to wherever the backlog actually is — when telemetry is chronically backed up while fastFiltered sits empty, floating workers drain telemetry instead of slots being wasted on idle queues. Total ceiling stays at 96+32=128 (same DB pool pressure as the prior flat 32×4). Implemented in `startFloatingWorkers(boss, maxFloat)` with module-level `floatingInFlight` (soft cap counter) and `floatingLoopRunning` (shutdown signal); the loop fires per-queue `boss.fetch()` calls in `FLOAT_PRIORITY` order (probe > fastFiltered > telemetry > systemInfo), dispatches each fetched job to the matching `runFooFor()`, and explicitly calls `boss.complete(name, id)` / `boss.fail(name, id, ...)`. Singleton-key dedup at the publish layer means floating workers never collide with dedicated workers on the same `(assetId, cadence)` — pg-boss already coalesces those into a single in-flight job. `POLARIS_PGBOSS_POOL_SIZE` defaults to 20 at runtime but the Capacity Advisor now scales the **recommended** value with the configured worker count: `roundUpToNearest(max(20, ceil(workerCeiling × 0.25)), 50)` — at the 128-worker default that rounds up to 50. The pool is used only for pg-boss's internal queue mechanics (boss.fetch / boss.complete / boss.fail); a too-small pool serializes the simultaneous boss.complete() calls that fire when a heavy cadence finishes a batch of work, which the operator can then see as failed jobs and stalled queue depth. The configured count per queue plus `queue="floating"` is exposed as the `polaris_monitor_workers{queue}` gauge so operators can confirm restart-pickup without reading logs. **Stable per-worker slot IDs** are assigned via [src/utils/workerSlotPool.ts](src/utils/workerSlotPool.ts) — `probe-W01..W24`, `fast-W01..W24`, `telemetry-W01..W24`, `sysinfo-W01..W24`, `floating-F01..F32`. The slot is acquired on handler entry and released on exit (try/finally) so a slot is reused across jobs and an operator can follow one slot's lifecycle through journalctl. The pickup/finish lines emit at info level ONLY when the job payload carries `verboseDebug: true` (set by the publisher when the asset's source integration has `config.verboseLogging` on); slot bookkeeping itself runs every tick regardless. Naming convention `polaris-monitor-*`; future `polaris-discovery-*` queues should be added when discovery moves under pg-boss and worker tuning revisited together.
│   │   ├── assetSightingService.ts  # DHCP-only sighting recorder: recordSightings() batch-upserts AssetFortigateSighting rows (deduped by (assetId, fortigateDevice)); getQuarantineCandidates(assetId) returns sightings within max-age; getSightingSettings/updateSightingSettings backed by Setting key "quarantineSightingSettings". Default max-age: 180 days.
│   │   ├── assetQuarantineService.ts # FortiGate MAC quarantine push/pull. quarantineAsset()/releaseQuarantine() orchestrate per-FortiGate pushes using buildTransportForIntegration() (supports both FMG proxy/direct and standalone FortiGate). Pushes to user.quarantine.targets/<name>/macs on FortiOS. quarantineTargets JSON array tracks per-target status (synced|drift|failed). verifyAssetQuarantine() drift-checks all targets without writing. statusBeforeQuarantine preserves prior status for restore on release. Auto-quarantine fires from discovery when a quarantined asset is sighted on a new FortiGate.
│   │   ├── apiTokenService.ts       # Bearer-token auth for external callers. createToken/listTokens/revokeToken/deleteToken/verifyToken. Wire format: polaris_<32-char-base64url>. argon2id hash + tokenPrefix-indexed candidate lookup. Scopes: assets:quarantine, assets:read. lastUsedAt/lastUsedIp bumped on successful verify.
│   │   ├── peerInferredLldpService.ts # Read-time supplementation of `AssetLldpNeighbor` from `Asset.fortinetTopology`. `buildInferredNeighborsForAsset(assetId)` synthesizes neighbor rows the persisted table can't see — FortiSwitch ports facing managed FortiAPs (via the AP's `parentSwitch`/`parentPort`), FortiGate FortiLink-side interfaces facing managed switches (via the switch's `controllerFortigate`/`uplinkInterface`), and the FortiAP-side reverse of (a). The FortiAP branch additionally normalizes the synthesized `localIfName` via `fortiapInterfaceAlias.normalizeFortiapInterfaceName` against the AP's recent `AssetInterfaceSample` ifNames — discovery stamps `uplinkInterface` from the FortiGate's managed_ap response in FortiAP-CLI naming (`lan1`/`lan2`) but the AP's own SNMP IF-MIB exposes the same port as `eth0`/`eth1`. Without normalization the inferred row would never line up with any row in the System tab's interface table. Prefers the eth* form, falls back to lan*, falls back to the raw value when neither alias exists. Returns `InferredLldpNeighbor[]` shaped like the real Prisma rows minus DB-only fields, stamped with `source: "peer-inferred"` and a pre-resolved `matchedAsset`. `dedupeInferredNeighbors(real, inferred)` drops any inferred row where a real LLDP row exists on `(localIfName, matchedAssetId)`; real LLDP wins. Consumed by `GET /assets/:id/system-info` (merges into the response) and `GET /assets/:id/interface-history` (filters to the requested ifName). Read-only — never writes the table. `/map/sites/:id/topology` is unaffected since it consumes `fortinetTopology` directly.
│   │   ├── interfaceTopologyService.ts # Inter-Fortinet topology inference from interface naming conventions. inferInterfaceTopology(seedAssetIds) reads the latest AssetInterfaceSample per (assetId, ifName) for the seed set within a 1-hour timestamp window (the function only needs ONE row per pair — the latest — so the window exists purely to filter out interfaces that have stopped reporting; without it the DISTINCT ON had to scan the entire active hypertable chunk and was observed at 13.5 min / 90M rows / 9 GB I/O on a fleet of ~600 infra assets), parses each ifName through fortinetSerialPattern, and resolves matches against the in-memory Fortinet asset inventory by serial-fragment endsWith (FortiOS-auto peer aggregates) or hostname exact/prefix-with-separator (operator-named MCLAG aggregates). Skips ambiguous (>1 hit) and self-loop matches. Returned edge ifNames prefer the underlying physical member when the matched aggregate has exactly one physical child (ifType="physical" + ifParent=aggregate) — operators trace cables by physical port, not by the FortiOS-stamped peer-named aggregate label; multi-member aggregates keep the aggregate name as the safe fallback (no single "the physical port" exists, and a comma-joined list would lie about the strict one-port-per-physical-link model). The aggregate name is still the inference SIGNAL (peer identity is encoded in it) — only the display ifName on the returned edge is normalized. Returns sibling edges plus a remoteAssets map for cross-site peers. Read-only — uses data the monitoring pipeline already collected, no new device queries. Wired into GET /map/sites/:id/topology AND `dependencyTreeService.recomputeDependencyTree` (Phase 12 of FMG/FortiGate sync) — the 1-hour window keeps that recompute under a second instead of blocking discovery for ~20 minutes.
│   │   ├── dependencyTreeService.ts # Dependency-aware monitoring suppression. recomputeDependencyTree(integrationId?) walks fortinetTopology.controllerFortigate / parentSwitch + interfaceTopologyService edges + AssetLldpNeighbor.matchedAssetId into a parent→child DAG, prunes via **physical-first** BFS layer assignment from each FortiGate (layer 1) outward — interface + LLDP edges form the primary adjacency, controller edges are a fallback for assets the physical pass didn't reach, with simple-path detection so a 3+ switch daisy chain that's only LLDP-visible between siblings (not back to the FortiGate) still chains correctly instead of collapsing every switch into a same-layer sibling of the FortiGate. MCLAG pairs and any branching / cycled controller-only component still attach as siblings. Kept edges prefer the most-physical `detectedVia` (interface > lldp > controller) for the audit-trail row, persists the resulting source="computed" rows in AssetDependencyParent and Asset.dependencyLayer per scope. Operator overrides (source="override") take precedence per asset; computed rows survive alongside for audit. reconcileDependencySuppression() is the 60s reconciler — source of truth for Asset.dependencySuppressed; "all-down" multi-parent semantics (a switch with redundant uplinks suppresses only when EVERY effective parent is down or itself suppressed). Unmonitored parents are transparent — the walk continues to their grandparents. propagateAfterStatusChange(assetId) is the latency-optimization hook fired from recordProbeResult after monitor.status_changed lands. Pure helpers (buildDependencyEdgesFromInputs, assignLayers, evaluateSuppression) are exported for unit testing.
│   │   ├── mapRegionService.ts      # Device Map regions: operator-drawn polygons stored as a JSON blob in Setting under key `mapRegions` (allocationTemplateService pattern). Each region carries a unique name; firewalls whose lat/lng falls inside the polygon — and FortiSwitches / FortiAPs whose `fortinetTopology.controllerFortigate` matches one of those firewalls' hostname — get tagged with `region:<name>`. The reconciler is **additive**: it adds the region tag to in-polygon assets and only strips a tag when the region is renamed or deleted. Manual operator attachments (hand-tagging a server with `region:Atlanta`) survive across runs; manually *removing* a tag from an in-polygon asset will be re-added on the next reconcile. Region creation upserts a `Tag` registry row at `region:<name>` under category "Map Regions" so it shows up in the asset edit modal's tag picker; rename rotates the registry row; delete removes it. Fired from CRUD endpoints (await), end-of-FMG/FortiGate `syncDhcpSubnets` Phase 13, and a 6h periodic safety-net job. Membership is computed by `pointInPolygon` from `src/utils/geo.ts`; only firewalls have lat/lng so endpoints are never auto-tagged.
│   │   ├── firewallTagService.ts    # `firewall:<hostname>` breadcrumb tags on every FortiSwitch / FortiAP / non-infra endpoint discovered via FMG / standalone FortiGate, so operators can filter "everything behind FortiGate-X" from the asset-edit tag picker. **Reconciled** at end of every full / finalize discovery: rebuilds each in-scope asset's `firewall:*` tag set from `Asset.fortinetTopology.controllerFortigate` (managed switches / APs) plus `AssetFortigateSighting` rows within `quarantine.sightingMaxAgeDays` (DHCP-discovered endpoints). Cross-integration safe — the reconciler only strips `firewall:<hostname>` tags whose hostname is one of THIS integration's currently-known firewalls; tags pointing at FortiGates owned by other integrations or operator-typed `firewall:fake` survive untouched. FortiGate firewall assets themselves are never tagged (don't tag a device with itself).
```
