```
                                       Integration.config.monitorSettings — used by endpoint
                                       asset types not covered by a per-class subtab set)
                →  systemInfoIntervalSeconds ONLY: derive from integration.pollInterval × 3600
                                      (when discoveredByIntegrationId is set AND tier-3 left it null AND
                                       the per-class streams block didn't set interfaces.intervalSeconds)
                →  manual tier        (when discoveredByIntegrationId is null)
                →  hardcoded floor    (final fallback; never user-visible)
```

- **Tier-1 / per-asset** — columns on `Asset`: `monitorIntervalSec`, `cpuMemoryIntervalSec`, `temperatureIntervalSec`, `systemInfoIntervalSec`, `lldpIntervalSec` *(Phase 2)*, `storageIntervalSec` *(Phase 2)*, `probeTimeoutMs`, `cpuMemoryTimeoutMs`, `temperatureTimeoutMs`, `systemInfoTimeoutMs`. null = inherit. These cadence/timeout fields are the ONLY settings that cascade to the per-asset tier; `failureThreshold` and the three retentions stop at tier-2. CPU/memory and temperature each carry their own per-stream column set as of Slice 2; LLDP and Storage gained their own per-asset cadence columns in Phase 2 alongside the queue carve-out.
- **Tier-2 / class override** — `MonitorClassOverride` row keyed on `(integrationId, assetType)`. **As of Phase 2 this table is manual-scope only** (`integrationId IS NULL`). The `POST` and `PUT` routes reject integration-scoped writes outright with 400; the `migrateMonitorSettingsPerClass` startup job folds historical integration-scoped rows into the per-class streams blocks on each integration and deletes them in the same transaction. Every settings field is nullable; null = inherit. Includes its own `cpuMemoryTimeoutMs` / `temperatureTimeoutMs` / `systemInfoTimeoutMs` alongside `probeTimeoutMs`.
- **Tier-3a / per-class streams (Phase 2 — integration-discovered assets only)** — `Integration.config.<klass>Monitor.streams.<stream>` where the class block is picked by `Asset.assetType`. Per integration type:
  - `fortimanager` / `fortigate` → `fortigateMonitor` (firewall) / `fortiswitchMonitor` (switch) / `fortiapMonitor` (access_point). FortiAP omits storage.
  - `activedirectory` / `entraid` / `windowsserver` → `workstationMonitor` (workstation) / `serverMonitor` (server).
  Each stream cell carries `{polling, credentialId, intervalSeconds, timeoutMs, mibId, failureThreshold?}` — `failureThreshold` only applies on `responseTime`; the resolver ignores it elsewhere. Null on any cell falls through to the flat tier-3 baseline below. Endpoint asset types (printer / router / other / etc.) discovered via FortiGate DHCP or AD/Entra/WinSrv that don't fall into a per-class subtab set bypass this tier entirely and use the flat baseline directly. See `pickClassStreamsBlock` + `applyClassStreamsOverlay` in `monitoringService.ts`.
- **Tier-3b / integration flat baseline or manual** — full tier-settings block (cadence/retention/threshold + the three timeout fields + the Phase 2 LLDP / Storage cadence + timeout fields), complete (no nulls) **except `systemInfoIntervalSeconds` at the integration tier**, which is nullable. Stored at `Integration.config.monitorSettings` for integration-discovered assets, or in the `manualMonitorSettings` Setting row for orphan assets. Existing tier-3 rows written before the LLDP / Storage tuning fields existed fall through to the hardcoded floor (10000ms timeouts, 600s LLDP+Storage interval) via the resolver's `tierFromJson` default until an operator saves new values.
- **Integration pollInterval linkage** (`systemInfoIntervalSeconds` only). When the integration tier doesn't explicitly set this field (operator left it blank, OR the migration job retroactively nulled the literal-default 600s), `loadIntegrationTierSettings` derives the cadence from `integration.pollInterval × 3600` so interface / LLDP / storage / IPsec scrape on the same schedule discovery already runs (1–24h pollInterval → 3600–86400s systemInfo). Ties the heavy-cadence work to a knob operators already understand and cuts interface-sample volume by 24× at the default 4h pollInterval. Explicit per-asset / class / integration-tier values still win. Tracked in `tierSystemInfoFromPollIntervalCache` (parallel to `tierCache`); `resolveMonitorSettingsWithProvenance` reads it to label the provenance `"integrationPollInterval"` so the integration edit modal's Cadence & Retention input renders `Leave blank to follow this integration's discovery interval (currently 4h)`. Manual-tier orphan assets are not eligible (no integration to inherit from) and fall through to the hardcoded floor. Existing 600s defaults are nulled retroactively by the `migrateSystemInfoCadenceLinkage` startup job. The `/assets/:id/effective-monitor-settings` response includes `integration: { id, name, type, pollInterval }` so frontend hints can be rendered without a second round-trip.
- **Hardcoded floor** — values baked into `monitoringService.ts` (60s probe, 60s telemetry, 600s sysinfo, 5000ms probe timeout, 10000ms telemetry timeout, 10000ms system-info timeout, threshold 3, 30d retentions). Final fallback only used when the migration job hasn't seeded tier-3 yet AND the legacy `monitorSettings` Setting row is also gone.

`resolveMonitorSettings(asset)` (in `src/services/monitoringService.ts`) walks the four tiers and returns the effective values. `resolveMonitorSettingsWithProvenance(asset)` returns the same plus per-field `provenance: "asset" | "class" | "integration" | "integration-class" | "manual" | "integrationPollInterval"` labels and the `classOverrideId` for UI deep-links. `"integration-class"` (Phase 2 — sub-label of the integration tier) means the value came from the per-class streams block `<klass>Monitor.streams.<stream>` rather than the flat `monitorSettings` baseline, so the asset modal's tier badge reads "FortiSwitch subtab" instead of generic "FortiManager". `"integrationPollInterval"` is a `systemInfoIntervalSeconds`-only sub-label indicating the value was derived from the integration's discovery `pollInterval` rather than read from explicit tier-3 JSON. **Resolver cache key is now `${integrationId}:${assetType}`** (Phase 2 — was previously `${integrationId}`); each per-class branch gets its own cached snapshot. Routes call `invalidateMonitorSettingsCache({integrationId, assetType?})` after every write — when `assetType` is omitted (integration config save), every `${integrationId}:*` entry is walked and dropped.

There is **no probe backoff for confirmed-down hosts**. Down assets keep probing at the resolved `intervalSeconds`. Heavy cadences (telemetry / systemInfo / fastFiltered) are still suppressed while `monitorStatus = "down"` so a wedged peer doesn't burn worker time, but the cheap response-time probe keeps firing — recovery is detected within one base-cadence tick instead of up to 30 minutes.

#### Where each tier is edited

- **Tier-3 integration (Phase 2 per-class)** — each integration's edit modal has a **Monitoring tab** with **per-class subtabs** (FMG / FortiGate: FortiGate / FortiSwitch / FortiAP; AD / Entra ID / Windows Server: Workstations / Servers). Each class subtab carries the six per-stream subtabs (Response Time / CPU/Memory / Temperature / Interfaces / LLDP / Storage; FortiAP omits Storage). Each per-stream subtab holds the polling method, credential, cadence, timeout, and (response-time only) failure threshold. Save Changes serializes each class's stream values into `Integration.config.<klass>Monitor.streams.<stream>` via the regular integration PUT; the flat `monitorSettings` JSON is still written from the FortiGate / Workstations subtab as the "default for endpoint asset types" fallback.
- **Tier-3 manual** — Assets page header → **Monitoring Settings** button → modal's **Manual Monitoring** section. Same per-stream layout. Used by orphan / manually-created assets. Writes via `PUT /api/v1/monitor-settings/manual`.
- **Tier-2 class overrides (manual scope only — Phase 2)** — Assets-page **Monitoring Settings** modal → **Class Overrides** section. The source picker no longer surfaces integrations; the section is labelled "Manual scope only — assets without an integration source." Backend `POST` / `PUT` reject integration-scoped rows with 400. Operators who used to add overrides scoped to a specific integration now use that integration's per-class subtabs (above) which deliver the same effect via the streams block instead of the override table.
- **Tier-1 per-asset** — the asset edit modal's **Monitoring tab** carries the cadence/timeout overrides (`monitorIntervalSec`, `probeTimeoutMs`, plus the per-asset `cpuMemoryIntervalSec` / `temperatureIntervalSec` / `systemInfoIntervalSec` columns), tier-source badges from `/effective-monitor-settings`, and a **Show other asset overrides under this scope** button that opens a slide-over listing peer assets with their own per-asset overrides.

The integration's Monitoring tab additionally carries a **Discovery Defaults** section on FMG/FortiGate types only, with three subtabs (FortiGates / FortiSwitches / FortiAPs). Each subtab holds discovery-time concerns: `addAsMonitored`, `snmpCredentialId`, `sshCredentialId`, `autoMonitorInterfaces`. **Cadence inputs are no longer in these subtabs** — they live in the Cadence & Retention section above and apply uniformly across the integration's entire fleet. The credential rows are **type-aware**: the SNMP picker is rendered only when at least one of the four integration-tier polling dropdowns is set to SNMP, the SSH picker only when at least one is set to SSH, and both stay hidden when neither is selected. Credentials persist regardless of visibility, so flipping back later restores the prior selection.

### Events — mixed scoping
- `GET    /events`                              *(auth)* — Audit log (filter by level, action, resourceType, message — message is case-insensitive substring)
- `GET    /events/archive-settings`             *(admin)* — reveals SSH host/user/path even with password masked
- `PUT    /events/archive-settings`             *(admin)*
- `POST   /events/archive-test`                 *(admin)*
- `GET    /events/syslog-settings`              *(admin)* — reveals host/port/TLS paths
- `PUT    /events/syslog-settings`              *(admin)*
- `POST   /events/syslog-test`                  *(admin)*
- `GET    /events/retention-settings`           *(auth)*
- `PUT    /events/retention-settings`           *(admin)*
- `GET    /events/asset-decommission-settings`  *(auth)*
- `PUT    /events/asset-decommission-settings`  *(admin)* — `{ inactivityMonths }`; 0 disables auto-decommission

### Conflicts — `requireAuth` (role-scoped list + resolve)
- `GET    /conflicts`                           — List. Role-filtered: admin sees all; networkadmin sees reservation conflicts only; assetsadmin sees asset conflicts only; others see empty list.
- `GET    /conflicts/count`                     — Badge count; same role scoping as the list.
- `POST   /conflicts/:id/accept`                — Reservation: apply discovered values. Asset: upsert an `AssetSource` row (`sourceKind` = `entra`/`ad`, `externalId` = `proposedDeviceId`) tying the existing asset to the proposed Entra/AD identity, plus overlay Entra/AD fields into empty existing fields. **Hostname has one extra rule:** when the conflict was raised via 15-char NetBIOS truncation (`proposedAssetFields.matchedVia === "netbios"`), the longer canonical hostname replaces the truncated one even when the existing field is non-empty — the truncated form is a NetBIOS quirk, not a deliberate choice. **Phase 4d retired the legacy `assetTag = entra:<id>` / `ad:<guid>` write** — the AssetSource row is now the canonical identity link, and audit trail is captured by the auto-resolve syncLog event. (`prev-*` breadcrumb tags are still written pending conflict-resolution refactor.) **Ghost-sibling lookup** (when a different asset already owns the proposed source's externalId) now queries `AssetSource` by `(sourceKind, externalId)` instead of by `assetTag` — the ghost's non-empty fields are absorbed and the ghost is deleted so the unique-constraint upsert lands cleanly. 403 if caller's role doesn't cover this conflict's entityType.
- `POST   /conflicts/:id/reject`                — Reservation: keep existing, dismiss. Asset: create a separate new Asset for the Entra/AD snapshot, then upsert its `AssetSource` row (`sourceKind` = `entra`/`ad`, `externalId` = `proposedDeviceId`) so the next discovery run finds the new asset by source and doesn't re-fire the collision.
- `POST   /conflicts/:id/merge`                 — **Asset conflicts only.** Per-field merge with operator-chosen winners. Body: `{ fieldWinners: { hostname?: "existing"|"proposed", serialNumber?, macAddress?, manufacturer?, model?, os?, osVersion?, assignedTo? } }`. Fields not present in `fieldWinners` fall back to the default accept logic (blank-fill for serial/mac/manufacturer/model/assignedTo, always-overwrite for os/osVersion, NetBIOS-upgrade rule for hostname). AssetSource stamping and ghost-asset absorption are unconditional regardless of selections. Reservation conflicts return 400 — they use the regular accept path. Audit Event message appends the selection map when used.

**Asset-conflict flavours.** `proposedAssetFields.collisionReason` distinguishes two pathways the discovery sync can raise an asset-collision conflict, in decreasing order of confidence:
- `"untagged-collision"` — incoming Entra/AD device's hostname matches an existing asset that has no source tag (DHCP-discovered, manually created, etc.).
- `"duplicate-registration"` — incoming hostname matches an existing asset already tagged by the same source with a *different* deviceId/objectGUID (Entra returned two distinct deviceIds for the same display name — re-enrol, re-image, dual-boot — or AD has two `objectGUID`s for what looks like the same physical computer). Accepting re-keys the existing asset's source row to the incoming externalId (the prior identity is captured in the auto-resolve syncLog Event for audit).

`proposedAssetFields.matchedVia` indicates the matching mechanism: `"exact"` (full hostname equality) or `"netbios"` (matched only after truncating one side to 15 chars). NetBIOS-truncation matching pairs an AD `cn`-derived 15-char hostname with the full Entra `displayName` and vice versa, so neither side missing the FQDN-aware rename causes a phantom split.

**Note:** The legacy `"mac-collision"` flavour (Entra-only, raised when the incoming Intune Ethernet MAC matched a MAC ever recorded on another asset) was retired — the Entra sync now treats Ethernet MAC as a positive identity-match signal and silently cross-links, the same way SID does for hybrid-joined devices. See "Hybrid-join cross-link" below for the cascade. Existing `mac-collision` conflict rows in production databases keep rendering through the events.js compatibility branch; no new ones are created.

### Search — `requireAuth`
- `GET    /search?q=<query>`                    — Global typeahead. Classifies input (IP, CIDR, MAC, or text), runs parallel entity queries, returns grouped results (`blocks`, `subnets`, `reservations`, `assets`, `ips`, `sites`) capped at 8 per group. **Asset search reach** spans direct Asset columns (`hostname` / `dnsName` / `assetTag` / `serialNumber` / `ipAddress` / `manufacturer` / `model` / `macAddress` / `assignedTo` / `department`), plus four parallel cross-search pathways merged into the same 8-row budget: `AssetSource.externalId` (Entra deviceId / AD objectGUID / FortiGate serial — accepts the `entra:` / `ad:` / `fgt:` / `intune:` / `fortiswitch:` / `fortiap:` prefix and strips it), `AssetMacAddress.mac` (full MAC history beyond the primary), `AssetAssociatedIp.ip` (secondary interface IPs), and a JSON-blob substring scan over `Asset.associatedUsers` + `AssetSource.observed` (logged-in users, on-prem SID, UPN, `onPremisesSecurityIdentifier`, etc.). The JSON scan is backed by GIN trigram indexes on both columns (migration `20260507200000_search_json_trgm_indexes`); queries shorter than 3 chars fall back to seq scans, but the 2-char minimum at `searchAll` keeps result sets tiny in that range. `sites` carries firewall assets that have lat/lng coordinates (i.e. pinned on the Device Map); these are queried separately with their own 8-row budget so the Device Map section always has a slot regardless of how many endpoints match. Pinned firewalls also ride along in `assets` (no SQL-level exclusion — `searchAssets` passes an empty baseFilter to `runAssetSearch`) so a search like "HARRIMAN-61F-1" shows the FortiGate in BOTH the Device Map section AND the Assets section; operators expecting to find the firewall under Assets find it there, while the dedicated Device Map slot guarantees a pan-to-marker entry point. The frontend dropdown labels the `sites` group "Device Map" and pan-to-marker on click via `window.polarisMapPanToAsset` (or navigates to `/map.html#site=<id>` from other pages). The `ips` hit resolves the containing subnet and any active reservation. **Scope-prefix shortcuts**: a query starting with `block:` / `b:`, `network:` / `n:`, `asset:` / `a:`, `reservation:` / `r:`, or `map:` / `m:` (case-insensitive, whitespace-tolerant) constrains the search to that one group AND lifts the per-group cap from 8 to `SCOPED_LIMIT=200`. Other groups return empty arrays in the scoped path. Min query length drops to 1 char after the prefix so `block:f` works. `results.query` echoes the original raw input (prefix included) for the desktop dropdown's stale-response check. Both surfaces show a hint panel when the search input is focused with no query yet: desktop `_showSearchShortcutHints` in `public/js/app.js` renders inline rows in the search dropdown; mobile `renderSearchEmpty` in `public/js/mobile/tabs.js` renders a 2×2 MD3 chip grid on the Search tab body (tapping the input from any tab routes to /search so the chips are visible before typing). **Page-aware section ordering**: when the operator is on `/map.html` the Device Map section is hoisted to the top of the dropdown; same logic for `/subnets.html` (Networks), `/assets.html` (Assets), `/blocks.html` (Blocks). All authenticated roles can search; front-end edit modals render in view-only mode for users without write permission.

### Device Map — `requireAuth`
- `GET    /map/sites`                           — Every firewall Asset with non-null lat/lng. Includes subnet count (via `Subnet.fortigateDevice` match), last-seen status, and a monitor health snapshot: `monitored`, `monitorHealth` (`"up" | "degraded" | "down" | "unknown"`, `null` when unmonitored), `monitorRecentSamples`, `monitorRecentFailures`. Health is computed from the last 10 `AssetMonitorSample` rows per asset — all 10 ok → `up` (green pin), any failed → `degraded` (amber, "packet loss"), 10/10 failed → `down` (red). The map intentionally uses this fixed 10-sample window rather than the global `monitor.failureThreshold`. Sidebar page entry: "Device Map" (below Dashboard).
- `GET    /map/sites/:id/topology/search?q=<query>` — Site-scoped endpoint search powering the topology modal's search box. Matches `q` as case-insensitive substring against `hostname` / `ipAddress` / `macAddress` / `assignedTo` / `dnsName`, scoped to endpoints whose `lastSeenSwitch` references one of THIS site's FortiSwitches. Returns up to 25 results: `{ id, hostname, ipAddress, macAddress, assetType, assignedTo, switchId, switchHostname, port, lastSeen }`. The frontend pulses the matching switch on the Cytoscape graph and navigates to the endpoint's asset details on click.
- `GET    /map/sites/:id/topology`              — Graph payload for the click-through modal. Returns `{ fortigate, switches[], aps[], subnets[], edges[], interfaceEdges[], lldpNodes[], remoteAssetNodes[], lldpEdges[] }`. Each entry in `switches[]` carries `endpointCount` (total endpoints learned on the switch's ports via Phase 7.5 of FMG/FortiGate discovery) and `endpoints[]` — top-25 by recency, each `{ id, hostname, ipAddress, macAddress, assetType, assignedTo, port, lastSeen }`. The right-hand info panel renders these as a collapsible "Endpoints (N)" section nested under each switch with click-through to asset details. The full set is reachable via the search endpoint above. The `fortigate` object carries the same `monitored` / `monitorHealth` / `monitorRecentSamples` / `monitorRecentFailures` fields as `/map/sites` so the modal's root node color matches the pin. Every edge id references a node in the same payload. FortiGate→Switch edges are derived from `Asset.fortinetTopology.uplinkInterface` (the FortiLink interface from `managed-switch/status.fgt_peer_intf_name`). AP→Switch edges come from `switch-controller/detected-device` MAC learnings matched against AP base_mac during discovery; APs with no peer switch fall back to a direct FortiGate→AP edge. FortiSwitch and FortiAP nodes use the same monitor-health color scheme as the FortiGate node — green/amber/red/gray driven by `Asset.monitorStatus` (translated via `monitorStatusToHealth` to the three-state `MonitorHealth` shape). Unmonitored switches/APs render gray. The controller probe path (`probeFortinetController`) maintains `monitorStatus` on these assets via the parent FortiGate's managed-switch/managed-AP inventory. **`interfaceEdges`** carries CMDB-inferred peer links from FortiOS interface naming conventions, computed by `interfaceTopologyService.inferInterfaceTopology` against the latest `AssetInterfaceSample` per (assetId, ifName) for every site asset: aggregate names that encode a peer's serial fragment (auto-stamped FortiLink — e.g. `8FFTV23025884-0` ↔ `S108FFTV23025884`) AND operator-named hostname aggregates (custom MCLAG between non-stacked pairs — e.g. `METROR2-T1024E`). Each entry: `{ source, target, sourceIfName, label, via: "interface", matchVia: "serial" | "hostname" }`. Cross-site peers matched via this pathway are added to `remoteAssetNodes` alongside LLDP-matched cross-site assets. Authoritative — duplicates against `edges` (controller-derived) are dropped, and the LLDP dedupe set is seeded with `interfaceEdges` so a peer link confirmed by both signals only renders once. Rendered as solid teal lines (distinct from the muted gray controller-data edges and the dashed orange LLDP edges). **`lldpNodes` / `remoteAssetNodes` / `lldpEdges`** carry LLDP-derived topology that fortinetTopology can't see: every `AssetLldpNeighbor` row whose `assetId` is the FortiGate or any of its sibling switches/APs is examined, neighbors that resolve back to a sibling are skipped (the authoritative FortiLink/MAC edge already covers them), **neighbors matched to a Polaris asset OUTSIDE this site** are surfaced into `remoteAssetNodes` (separate from ghost LLDP nodes — they're real assets, just at another site; rendered with a solid blue border and a tap-handler that pivots to the asset details page), and unmatched neighbors get a synthesized **ghost** node in `lldpNodes` keyed on chassisId so multi-link aggregates collapse to one. Each LLDP edge carries `targetLabel` (hostname/IP/chassisId for the right-hand info panel) and `targetIsAsset` (true → the link in the panel pivots to the asset details page). The cytoscape graph styles LLDP edges as dashed orange lines, ghost nodes with a dashed orange border, and remote-asset nodes with a solid blue border — distinguishing observed-via-LLDP links from authoritative controller data and "real asset elsewhere" from "non-Polaris device." **Node positions are persisted per site** (stored in `localStorage` keyed on site id) so manual layout edits survive page reload. A **Reset Layout** button in the modal toolbar (between Refresh and Fullscreen) drops the saved positions for the current site and re-runs the dagre layout against the cached topology data — useful when inherited drag positions look wrong after a tuning change to dagre's `nodeSep` / `rankSep`.

### Map Regions — `requireNetworkAdmin` (admin + networkadmin only)
Operator-drawn polygons that auto-tag enclosed FortiGates and their managed FortiSwitches / FortiAPs with `region:<name>`. Persisted as a single JSON blob in `Setting` keyed `mapRegions` (allocationTemplateService pattern). The reconciler is **additive**: it adds tags to in-polygon assets and only strips a tag when the region is renamed or deleted. Manual operator attachments (hand-tagging an endpoint server with `region:Atlanta`) survive across runs. Manually *removing* the tag from an in-polygon asset will be re-added on the next reconcile — that direction is authoritative by design so polygon membership always implies the tag. Region creation upserts a `Tag` registry row at `region:<name>` under category "Map Regions" so it shows up in the asset edit modal's tag picker; rename rotates the registry row; delete removes it. The reconciler runs at three points: inline (await) on every CRUD endpoint, end-of-`syncDhcpSubnets` Phase 13 in `mode in {"full","finalize"}`, and a 6-hour periodic safety-net job (`reconcileMapRegions.ts`). Polygon-only edits (drag a vertex without renaming) skip cleanup — assets that fall out of the polygon keep the tag (treated as if hand-attached); operators who want a tighter polygon-driven view rename the region (forces full cleanup) or delete and recreate. Read access also requires admin/networkadmin since regions are only rendered while editing.
- `GET    /map/regions`                         — List every region: `{ id, name, polygon: [[lat,lng],...], createdBy, createdAt, updatedAt }`. Sorted by name.
- `POST   /map/regions`                         — Create. Body: `{ name (1..64 chars, unique case-insensitive, no control chars), polygon ([[lat,lng],...] with ≥3 and ≤1000 vertices, lat in [-90,90], lng in [-180,180]) }`. Returns the created region. Reconciles inline. Writes `region.created` + (when something was tagged) `region.tags_reconciled` Events.
- `PUT    /map/regions/:id`                     — Update. Body: `{ name?, polygon? }`. Same validation. **Rename path:** strips `region:<oldName>` from every asset that carries it, then add-passes `region:<newName>` to current membership. **Polygon-only edit path:** add-pass only — assets that drifted out of the polygon keep the tag. Returns the updated region. Writes `region.updated` Event with `previousName` for audit.
- `DELETE /map/regions/:id`                     — Delete. Strips `region:<name>` from every asset that carries it and removes the `Tag` registry row. Returns 204. Writes `region.deleted` Event.

### Allocation Templates — mixed scoping
- `GET    /allocation-templates`                *(auth)* — List saved multi-subnet templates used by the Networks "Auto-Allocate Next" modal.
- `POST   /allocation-templates`                *(networkadmin)* — Create a template. Body: `{ name, entries: [{name, prefixLength, vlan?} | {skip: true, prefixLength}] }`.
- `PUT    /allocation-templates/:id`            *(networkadmin)* — Update a template.
- `DELETE /allocation-templates/:id`            *(networkadmin)* — Delete a template.

### Server Settings — `requireAdmin`
- `GET    /server-settings`
- `PUT    /server-settings`
- `GET    /server-settings/branding`            — Public; used by login page
- `POST   /server-settings/https`
- `POST   /server-settings/database/backup`
- `POST   /server-settings/database/restore`
- `GET    /server-settings/pg-tuning`           — Capacity + tuning health check. Returns `{ settings, pgConfigFile, capacity }`. `settings` is the per-`pg_settings` row shape `{ name, current, recommended, ok }` consumed by the Database card to render the inline current → recommended rows under a fired `pg_tuning_needed` capacity reason; empty array (with `pgConfigFile=null`) when row counts are below scale thresholds (`assets≥160` / `subnets≥1600` / `reservations≥160000`) — keeps small dev installs from being told to tune `shared_buffers`. `capacity: CapacitySnapshot` from `capacityService.getCapacitySnapshot()` exposes overall `severity` (`ok` | `watch` | `amber` | `red`), an array of `reasons` ({severity, code, message, suggestion}), `appHost.volumes[]` (per-filesystem free/total bytes + the Polaris-named roles each volume serves: `app`/`state`/`backups`/`db`; pre-deduped by stat.dev so single-LV installs collapse cleanly and STIG-style multi-LV layouts surface each filesystem once), `appHost.dbColocated` flag, `database.sampleTables[]` (rows, bytes, deadTupRatio, lastAutovacuum), `database.dataDirectory` (PostgreSQL `SHOW data_directory` when co-located), `workload.monitoredAssetCount`, `workload.monitoredInterfaceCount` (sum of `Asset.monitoredInterfaces` array lengths across every monitored asset — the operator-pinned fast-poll subset), and `workload.steadyStateSizeBytes` — the projected DB size at current monitored-asset count × cadences × retention. Also exposes `database.connectionPool` (currentInUse, peakObserved, prismaPoolSize, pgbossPoolSize, maxConnections) for the Maintenance tab Database card and the `db_pool_undersized` watch reason. Severity tiering: **red** (disk free <10% on any volume, DB > 90% of free disk on its volume, autovacuum stale >7d on a populated sample table, projected size > 8× host RAM) drives the non-dismissible sidebar critical alert; **amber** (disk 10–20%, dead-tup >20%, projected > 4× RAM, `pg_tuning_needed`) renders as a card-only reason row — there is no sidebar amber banner; **watch** (disk 20–30% on any volume, `ram_insufficient`) emits the transition Event but does NOT drive the navbar banner — gives ops a "you have weeks, not minutes" early warning that flows out through eventArchiveService → syslog/SFTP. The route handler also calls `recordCapacityTransition()` (best-effort; void) so a flip into a more severe state writes a `capacity.severity_changed` Event in real time the moment an admin loads the Maintenance tab.
- `GET    /server-settings/queue-mode`          — Returns `{ active, persisted, restartRequired, pgbossInstalled }`. `active` is what the running process is using (captured at boot); `persisted` is the current `Setting.monitor.queueMode` value (= what next restart will use). `restartRequired` is true when they differ. UI uses this for the "Pending: pg-boss on next restart" hint on the Maintenance tab Database card.
- `POST   /server-settings/queue-mode`          — Body `{ mode: "cursor" | "pgboss" }`. Writes `Setting.monitor.queueMode` and returns the same shape as GET. Called by the Capacity Advisor's Stage flow for the QUEUE_MODE lever; remains an explicit endpoint for tooling that wants to flip queue mode directly without going through the advisor. 409 if mode is "pgboss" but the package isn't installed in this build. Writes one `monitor.queue_mode.set` Event.
- `GET    /server-settings/capacity-advisor`    — Returns `{ advisor: AdvisorState, capacity: CapacitySnapshot, pgTuning: { settings, pgConfigFile } }` in a single round-trip. `advisor.recommendations` is the per-lever list (current vs recommended vs rationale + breakdown) consumed by the Maintenance-tab Capacity Advisor card. Computes via `getCapacitySnapshotWithAdvisor()` so the advisor-driven capacity reasons (`monitor_workers_undersized`, `max_connections_undersized`) appear in the embedded snapshot. Best-effort `recordCapacityTransition()` fires on every fetch.
- `POST   /server-settings/capacity-advisor/stage` — Body `{ keys: AdvisorLeverKey[] }` (non-empty). Recomputes the advisor fresh, then for each operator-selected key dispatches: `applyMode="env"` writes `setEnvVar(envName, recommended)` to `.env` (restart-required); `applyMode="queue-mode-endpoint"` calls `setQueueMode()` directly (same path as `POST /queue-mode`); `applyMode="advisory-only"` (PG_MAX_CONNECTIONS and PG_*) returns `status: "skipped"`. **Server picks values from a fresh recompute — never accepts arbitrary values from the client.** Returns `{ ok, results: [{ key, status, oldValue, newValue, reason? }], restartRequired }`. Writes one `capacity_advisor.staged` Event (info) listing applied keys + one `capacity_advisor.stage_failed` Event (warning) when any entry errored.
- `GET    /server-settings/sample-retention` — Returns `{ retention: SampleRetention }` from `getSampleRetention()`. Shape is `{stream: {tier: {class: days}}}` — 3 streams (sample / telemetry / systemInfo) × 3 tiers (detail / hourly / daily) × 3 classes (default / switch / accessPoint). Defaults 7/30/365 across the board on a fresh install.
- `PUT    /server-settings/sample-retention` — Body is a partial `SampleRetention` shape; missing keys inherit from the existing stored value (so the UI can PUT a single cell or the full grid). Validates each numeric to a non-negative integer; out-of-range or non-numeric values fall back to the existing stored value. Writes one `sample_retention.updated` Event. The cache is invalidated immediately; chart endpoints see the new values on the next request, the prune layer on the next daily tick.
- `POST   /server-settings/security-tokens/generate` — Body `{ which: "metrics" | "health" }`. Generates a 32-byte hex token, writes `METRICS_TOKEN` / `HEALTH_TOKEN` into the runtime `.env` via `setEnvVar` (in `src/utils/envFile.ts`), and stamps `process.env[KEY]` so the bearer-token gate on `/metrics` or `/health` takes effect immediately without a restart (both endpoints read the env var per-request). Driven by the [Generate token] button on the `metrics_token_unset` / `health_token_unset` capacity reasons. The raw token is never returned to the caller — operators read the value from `.env` if their monitoring system needs it. Writes one `server.security_token.generated` Event.
- `POST   /server-settings/restart` — Operator-triggered process restart. Responds `{ ok: true }` before calling `restartService()` from `updateService.ts` so the client sees a clean 200 and can switch to its restart-polling overlay. On Linux exits with code 1 so systemd's `Restart=on-failure` brings the process back; on Windows shells out to NSSM. Driven by the **Restart Polaris to apply** button on the Capacity Advisor card after the staging flow flips `_advisorJustStaged=true` and the recommendation list reaches zero outstanding levers. The frontend gates the click with `warnIfDiscoveryRunning("restart")` + a confirm dialog before posting. Writes one `server.restart.requested` Event (warning) with the actor's username.
### MIB Database — mixed scoping
Reads (`list`, `get`, `download`, `facets`, `profile-status`, `structure`, and the MIB-aware `walk`) are open to **admin OR assets-admin** so the team that owns asset onboarding can use the MIB-aware browse + walk surface. Writes (`upload`, `delete`) stay **admin-only**. Routes live in `src/api/routes/mibs.ts` mounted at `/server-settings/mibs` *before* the rest of `/server-settings` so its per-route guards take precedence over the blanket `requireAdmin` on the rest of /server-settings.
- `GET    /server-settings/mibs?manufacturer=&model=&scope=all|device|generic` *(admin OR assets-admin)* — List uploaded MIBs (filters: manufacturer + model exact match, scope filters generic vs device-specific).
- `GET    /server-settings/mibs/facets`         *(admin OR assets-admin)* — `{ manufacturers: [], modelsByManufacturer: { mfr: [models] } }` — distinct values from already-uploaded MIBs **plus** the asset inventory, so the upload-form datalists aren't empty before the first vendor MIB is uploaded.
- `GET    /server-settings/mibs/:id`            *(admin OR assets-admin)* — Full record including raw `contents`.
- `GET    /server-settings/mibs/:id/download`   *(admin OR assets-admin)* — `text/plain` download with the original filename.
- `GET    /server-settings/mibs/:id/structure`  *(admin OR assets-admin)* — Parsed object tree for the Browse modal. Returns `{ moduleName, imports[], symbols: MibSymbol[], tables: MibTable[], mibId, manufacturer, model, unresolvedCount }`. Each `MibSymbol` carries `name`, `parentName`, `oidIndex`, `fullOid` (resolved at the MIB's natural scope; null when a dependency is missing), `kind`, `syntax`, `baseType` (`INTEGER` / `Counter32` / `Gauge32` / `TimeTicks` / `OCTET STRING` / `IpAddress` / `OBJECT IDENTIFIER` / `BITS` / `SEQUENCE` / `SEQUENCE OF` / `OTHER`), `enumValues` (`{label, value}[]` for INTEGER types with named values, e.g. `up(1)/down(2)`), `access`, `status`, `description`, `isTableRow`, `indexNames`. Each `MibTable` carries `name`, `rowSymbol`, `columns` (children of the row in OID arc order), `indexNames`, `description`. Parser lives in `src/services/mibService.ts:parseMibStructured` (peer of `parseMib` so a regression in the heavier parser cannot break uploads); OID resolution is provided by `oidRegistry.resolveSymbolsForMib`. Per-symbol parse failures degrade to "name + parentName known, everything else null" rather than dropping the symbol — operators can still walk it, they just don't get decoding.
- `POST   /server-settings/mibs/:id/walk`       *(admin OR assets-admin)* — MIB-aware SNMP walk. Body: `{ assetId, credentialId, objectName, maxRows? }`. Resolves `objectName` against the MIB's structured parse, walks the asset's IP using the supplied SNMP credential via the same `snmpWalkRaw()` primitive the numeric `/assets/:id/snmp-walk` uses, then post-processes results: each row's OID is matched back against the MIB's symbol map (longest-prefix wins, so a table cell's OID resolves to the column symbol rather than the row), `INTEGER` values whose symbol has `enumValues` are decoded into `up(1)`-style labels, `TimeTicks` are rendered as human duration. Response shape: `{ kind: "table" | "scalars", ... , truncated, durationMs, rowCount, decodedCount, host, oid, objectName }`. When every result row's parent matches a single declared `MibTable.rowSymbol`, returns `kind: "table"` with `table: { name, rowSymbol, columns, indexNames, rows: [{ index, cells: { col → {raw, decoded} | null } }] }` so the UI can render a 2D row-per-INDEX grid; otherwise returns `kind: "scalars"` with `entries: [{ oid, symbol, suffix, syntax, baseType, raw, decoded }]`. Each call writes one `asset.snmp_walk` Event with `{ mibId, mibModuleName, objectName, kind, rowCount, decodedCount, truncated, durationMs }` in the details (info on success, warning on probe failure). `decodedCount < rowCount/2` is the operator's hint that the chosen MIB doesn't match the asset's manufacturer — the UI surfaces a yellow banner.
- `POST   /server-settings/mibs`                *(admin)* — `multipart/form-data` upload. Fields: `file` (required), `manufacturer?`, `model?`, `notes?`. The body is parsed by a minimal SMI validator (`mibService.parseMib`) before insert: rejects empty files, files containing NUL/control bytes, anything missing the `<NAME> DEFINITIONS ::= BEGIN ... END` envelope, or files exceeding 1 MB. `moduleName` and `imports` are extracted from the parse and stored on the row. Duplicate `(manufacturer, model, moduleName)` returns 409. Setting `model` without `manufacturer` is a 400 (generic MIBs can't be model-scoped).
- `DELETE /server-settings/mibs/:id`            *(admin)* — Remove a stored MIB.
- `GET    /server-settings/mibs/profile-status` *(admin OR assets-admin)* — Returns one entry per built-in vendor telemetry profile (`{ vendor, matchPattern, example, symbols: [{metric, symbol, resolved, fromModuleName, fromScope}], ready, partial, modelOverrides: [{model, mibCount}] }`). The MIB Database card uses this to render the **Vendor Profile Status** pill. `fromScope` is `"device" | "vendor" | "generic" | "seed"` and reflects which layer of `oidRegistry`'s scoped resolver provided the symbol.

### Manufacturer Profiles — mixed scoping
Reads (`list`, `get`) open to **admin OR assets-admin**; writes admin-only. Mounted at `/server-settings/manufacturer-profiles` *before* the blanket `requireAdmin` on the rest of `/server-settings`, same precedent as the MIB Database routes. The monitoring path does NOT consume these rows yet — the resolver swap from the hardcoded `VENDOR_TELEMETRY_PROFILES` constant to DB-backed lookup lands in a follow-up commit. This surface owns the operator-editable persistence: list profiles, get one profile in full, create profiles, edit per-metric defaults, manage per-model overrides, manage custom widgets that Slice 7's Custom MIB tab will render.
- `GET    /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles`                  *(admin OR assets-admin)* — `{ profiles: ProfileSummary[], transforms: [{kind, label}] }`. Each summary carries counts of metric rows / overrides / widgets / manufacturer-scoped uploaded MIBs.
- `GET    /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles/:id`              *(admin OR assets-admin)* — `{ profile: ProfileFull }` with metric rows, overrides, and custom widgets fully populated.
- `POST   /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles`                  *(admin)* — `{ manufacturer }`. Manufacturer is alias-canonicalized on insert; pre-populates the seven empty metric rows so the modal opens with the full canvas. 409 if a profile for the canonical form already exists.
- `PUT    /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles/:id/metrics/:metricKey`                              *(admin)* — Set this metric row's default symbol / mibId / type / transform. Body: `{ defaultSymbol?, defaultMibId?, defaultMibStdKey?, defaultType?, defaultTransform? }`. Null/empty values clear the field (= "use built-in seed for this metric"). `defaultMibId` and `defaultMibStdKey` are mutually exclusive — the service rejects requests that try to set both as non-null. `defaultMibStdKey` is validated against the `STD_MIB_KEYS` set; unknown keys are 400'd.
- `POST   /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles/:id/metrics/:metricKey/overrides`                    *(admin)* — Add a per-model override. Body: `{ modelPattern, symbol, mibId?, type?, transform?, order? }`. `modelPattern` is validated as a real regex.
- `PUT    /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles/:id/metrics/:metricKey/overrides/:overrideId`        *(admin)* — Edit.
- `DELETE /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles/:id/metrics/:metricKey/overrides/:overrideId`        *(admin)*.
- `POST   /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles/:id/widgets`     *(admin)* — Add a custom widget. Body: `{ name, symbol, mibId, type?, widgetType, transform?, displayOptions?, order?, modelPattern? }`. `mibId` is REQUIRED — custom widgets always reference an uploaded MIB.
- `PUT    /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles/:id/widgets/:widgetId`  *(admin)* — Edit.
- `DELETE /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles/:id/widgets/:widgetId`  *(admin)*.
- `DELETE /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles/:id`             *(admin)* — Remove the profile (cascades to metric rows, overrides, and widgets). No usage-count refusal yet — the resolver swap is the commit that introduces dependency, today the profile rows are purely additive.

### Device Icons — mixed scoping
Operator-uploaded images that overlay vendor logos on each Device Map topology node's colored status circle. **SVG uploads are transparently rasterized to 512×512 PNG on the way in** via `@resvg/resvg-js` (see `deviceIconService.rasterizeSvgToPng`) and stored as PNG bytes — Cytoscape's `background-image` pipeline can't scale a viewBox-only SVG past the browser's tiny default natural size, so rendering through it leaves the icon stuck at a fixed pixel size and visually anchored upper-left at every zoom. Pre-rasterizing on the server bypasses the whole class of bug, and the `rasterizeStoredSvgIcons` startup job migrates any legacy `image/svg+xml` rows already in the DeviceIcon table. Every icon is keyed to a **manufacturer plus either an asset type or a specific model** — there is no standalone "type-only" / "model-only" / "manufacturer-only" scope, because a "firewall" logo across every vendor would be meaningless. Resolution priority at render time, most-specific-wins: `manufacturer-model: <mfr>/<model>` (specific chassis) → `manufacturer-type: <mfr>/<assetType>` (vendor + role). Both scopes' manufacturer half is canonicalized through the `manufacturerAlias` map so "Fortinet, Inc." and "Fortinet" don't create two rows; the asset's `manufacturer` is canonicalized the same way at resolution time. Type values lowercased; model values preserved as typed. Topology renderer overlays the resolved icon at ~68% of the node so the colored status ring (Up green / Warning amber / Recovering blue / Down red / Unmonitored grey — per the existing five-state machine) stays visible around the logo at a glance. Storage is bytes-in-DB (`DeviceIcon` model). Allowed formats: PNG / JPEG / WebP (256 KB cap) plus SVG (32 KB cap, strict validation). Magic-byte check at upload validates declared mimeType matches actual content for raster formats. **SVG validation is reject-on-pattern, not sanitize** — refusal beats silent rewriting of operator content: the upload must start with `<?xml` or `<svg` (after optional BOM/whitespace) and is rejected outright if any of these patterns is present: `<script>`, `<foreignObject>`, `<iframe>`, `<object>`, `<embed>`, `<!DOCTYPE>`, `<!ENTITY>` (XXE), `<?xml-stylesheet>`, `on*=` event handlers, `javascript:` URLs (entity-escape-aware regex), any external `href`/`xlink:href`/`src` (only in-document `#fragment` refs allowed), `@import` in styles, external `url()` references. Defense-in-depth on the image-serve endpoint: SVG responses get `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` and a strict `Content-Security-Policy: default-src 'none'; style-src 'unsafe-inline'; img-src data:; sandbox` so direct-URL access can't execute anything even if a validator bypass slipped through.
- `GET    /device-icons`                         *(admin)* — List uploaded icons. Each row carries `{ id, scope, key, filename, mimeType, size, uploadedBy, uploadedAt, url }` where `key` is the canonical `"<canonicalManufacturer>/<typeOrModel>"` form and `url` points at the image-serve endpoint below for thumbnail previews.
- `POST   /device-icons`                         *(admin)* — `multipart/form-data` upload. Fields: `file` (required), `scope` ("manufacturer-type"|"manufacturer-model"), `manufacturer` (required; canonicalized via alias map), `typeOrModel` (required; assetType enum value when `scope=manufacturer-type`, free text when `scope=manufacturer-model`). Re-uploading the same canonical key replaces the existing image atomically.
- `DELETE /device-icons/:id`                     *(admin)* — Remove an uploaded icon.
- `GET    /device-icons/:id/image`               *(auth)* — Serves raw bytes with the stored `Content-Type` and `Cache-Control: private, max-age=3600`. SVG responses additionally carry `X-Content-Type-Options: nosniff` + a strict CSP so direct-URL access is locked down. Topology endpoint embeds this URL in each node's `iconUrl` so Cytoscape's `background-image` renders the logo inset over the status-colored node fill (icon at 68% size, centered). Browser HTTP cache deduplicates fetches across re-renders.

---

## Authentication & RBAC

Sessions are PostgreSQL-backed (`connect-pg-simple`), 8-hour max age, HttpOnly/Secure/SameSite=Lax cookies.

**Dynamic-role model (post-cutover).** RBAC is enforced via `requirePermission(functionKey, level)` from [src/api/middleware/permissions.ts](src/api/middleware/permissions.ts). Each route declares the function key it gates + the required access level; the resolver consults the caller's `Role.permissions` matrix denormalized into `req.session.roleSnapshot` at login. The matrix is `{ [functionKey]: "none" | "read" | "write" | "fullwrite" }` over a 25-key catalogue (see "Function-key catalogue" below). The five built-in roles (`admin` / `readonly` / `networkadmin` / `assetsadmin` / `user`) are seeded by `prisma/migrations/20260524000000_roles_table_cutover` so existing accounts keep their pre-cutover access exactly. Admin creates custom roles from the Roles section under Users → Manage Roles; the permissions slide-over maps directly onto the matrix.

| Built-in role | Locked? | Pre-cutover behavior preserved |
|---|---|---|
| `admin` | Protected — cannot be edited or deleted; hidden from the editable list | every function = `fullwrite` |
| `readonly` | Protected | every function readable by non-admins = `read`, admin-only functions = `none` |
| `networkadmin` | Editable, cannot delete | IP space / integrations / map-regions / discovery-conflicts write; read elsewhere |
| `assetsadmin` | Editable, cannot delete | assets / quarantine / monitor-settings write + own-subnet/own-reservation write |
| `user` | Editable, cannot delete | own-subnet / own-reservation write; read elsewhere |

**Ownership model for networks and reservations.** `subnets` and `reservations` carry an ownership dimension: `write` lets the caller edit only rows where `createdBy` matches their username; `fullwrite` bypasses the ownership filter. Built-in `user` and `assetsadmin` are seeded with `write`, `networkadmin` and `admin` with `fullwrite` — reproducing the pre-cutover behavior. Route handlers branch on `req.permissionLevel` (set by `requireOwnership(functionKey)`) to apply or skip the createdBy filter. Allocation **templates** stay at admin (`allocationTemplates=fullwrite`); `GET /allocation-templates` requires `allocationTemplates=read` which the four non-admin built-ins all hold.

**Function-key catalogue (25 keys).** Single source of truth in `src/api/middleware/permissions.ts:FUNCTION_KEYS`. Exposed at `GET /api/v1/roles/functions` so the frontend matrix UI renders without hardcoding the list. Keys: `ipBlocks`, `subnets`, `reservations`, `reservationPush`, `allocationTemplates`, `assets`, `assetsQuarantine`, `assetsProbe`, `assetMonitorSettings`, `mibDatabase`, `manufacturerProfiles`, `manufacturerAliases`, `credentials`, `integrations`, `discoveryConflicts`, `deviceMap`, `mapRegions`, `deviceIcons`, `events`, `staleReservations`, `apiTokens`, `users`, `roles`, `serverSettingsSystem`, `serverSettingsData`. Each carries a `hasOwnershipDimension` flag (only `subnets` + `reservations` today) that drives the UI's "Read-Write = own only / Full Read-Write = any" hint. Adding a new key requires a migration to seed it on every existing Role + a corresponding guard at the route layer.

**Session role snapshot + cache invalidation.** `req.session` carries `roleId` + a denormalized `roleSnapshot = { id, name, isProtected, permissions, updatedAt }` stamped at login. Every `requirePermission` call hits a module-level `Map<roleId, updatedAt>` cache first (O(1)); a Role write bumps the map via `bumpRoleVersion(roleId, updatedAt)` and the next request from any session holding the stale snapshot triggers ONE Prisma fetch + `req.session.save()` to refresh. Changing a user's `roleId` takes effect on next login (the snapshot is regenerated then); changing a role's permissions takes effect on the next request for every session that holds the role. Sub-millisecond when in sync.

**Region tags (storage-only in this slice).** Each `Role` and each `User` carry a `regionTags: String[]` column. Empty = unrestricted. Effective regions for a session are `union(role.regionTags, user.regionTags)`, returned by `GET /api/v1/auth/me` as `{ regionTags: { user, role, effective } }` for the frontend. The consumer (asset / subnet / reservation list filters, map view scoping) lives in a separate change — this slice owns the data model + admin-facing assignment UI only.

**Bearer-token hybrid (`requireSessionOrTokenPermission`).** Routes that an external system needs to reach (the asset quarantine push surface is canonical) accept either (a) a session whose role grants the required `(functionKey, level)` OR (b) a bearer token whose scopes include the named scope (e.g. `assets:quarantine`). Lives in `permissions.ts` alongside the session-only `requirePermission`.

**Last-admin invariant.** `userService.lastAdminEquivalent` refuses any operation that would leave Polaris with zero users holding `users=fullwrite` AND `roles=fullwrite` — covers both demoting the last admin away from an admin-equivalent role AND deleting the only admin user. Enforced on `PUT /users/:id/role` and `DELETE /users/:id`.

Rate limiting: 10 login attempts / 15 min per IP.

Azure SAML SSO is optional; users are auto-provisioned on first login with the `readonly` role.

**FMG auth note:** FortiManager 7.4.7+ / 7.6.2+ removed `access_token` query string support. The service uses the Bearer `Authorization` header exclusively. The standalone FortiGate integration (`fortigateService.ts`) uses the same Bearer header pattern against a REST API Admin token.

**Stale-Secure-cookie gotcha (HTTPS → HTTP downgrade):** Modern browsers enforce the "Leave Secure Cookies Alone" rule — a `Set-Cookie` from a non-secure origin is silently rejected if a same-name `Secure`-flagged cookie already exists, *and* the rejection extends to `Max-Age=0` deletions. This bites operators who run Polaris over HTTPS, then later access it over plain HTTP (port change, reverse-proxy reshuffle, fresh-install on the same host:port that previously had a self-signed cert). The browser holds the prior `polaris_csrf` (and possibly `connect.sid`) but won't send it over HTTP and won't let the server overwrite it; every state-changing request fails the CSRF check. The middleware in `csrf.ts` detects the case (HTTP request with no `polaris_csrf` cookie sent at all) and returns a specific error message instructing the operator to clear cookies for the site. The frontend in `public/js/api.js` does a parallel pre-check before mutating requests and shows a one-shot `alert()` with the same instructions when `_readCookie("polaris_csrf")` returns null on an HTTP page. A best-effort `res.clearCookie(..., { secure: true })` is also emitted from HTTP — Firefox/Chrome ignore it per the same rule, but more lenient browsers (Safari, older builds) will honor it and auto-heal. There is no clean server-side fix on Firefox/Chrome; the operator-clears-cookies path is the only reliable recovery.

**Polling-method redesign (resolved per stream from a four-tier hierarchy).** There is no longer a per-asset `monitorType` column. Each of the six streams (response-time probe / CPU+memory / temperature / interfaces / LLDP / storage) gets a polling method picked from a hierarchy: per-asset `*Polling` columns → `MonitorClassOverride.*Polling` → `Integration.config.monitorSettings.polling.*` (or the manual-tier `manualMonitorSettings.*Polling` Setting for orphan assets) → source default. Source defaults: FMG/FortiGate → **`icmp`** on response-time, `rest_api` on CPU+memory / temperature / interfaces, **`disabled`** on LLDP, **`disabled`** on storage (FortiOS appliances don't expose meaningful mountable storage, so the SNMP storage walk would burn cycles on every scrape — operators flip storage to `snmp` per-asset when they have a device that does); AD / Entra ID / Windows Server / Manual → `icmp` for response-time, null (= "not delivered") on the other five. Response-time defaults to ICMP across every source kind because ICMP is the cheapest universal liveness probe — operators wanting a heavier transport (REST `/sys/status`, SNMP `sysUpTime`) opt in per-asset, per-class, or at the integration tier. The compatibility matrix from `utils/pollingCompatibility.ts` enumerates the methods each source can use plus `"disabled"` (universally allowed; expresses "do not poll this stream") and `"agent"` (Polaris Agent installed locally on the host — allowed on AD / Entra / Windows Server / Manual sources only, never appliances). The storage stream is **SNMP-only when enabled** (HOST-RESOURCES-MIB `hrStorageTable` + the vendor disk fallback in `pickVendorProfileMerged`); operators set `storagePolling=snmp` at any tier to opt in, and the collector reuses the asset's interfaces credential (no separate storage credential / MIB column on Asset or MonitorClassOverride). Methods that don't apply to the asset's source kind are silently ignored at resolution time; the route layer rejects them at write time with a clear 400.

**Polaris Agent polling-method (`"agent"`).** Seventh value in the polling-method enum. Represents a Polaris-managed agent installed locally on the target host (Linux/macOS/Windows × amd64/arm64) that pushes samples back via `POST /api/v1/agents/samples` on its own schedule and holds a long-lived outbound WebSocket for on-demand probes. The four streams it produces: **response-time** measures the agent → Polaris RTT via a `/heartbeat` ping (so a TLS / firewall / DNS failure surfaces as a real packet-loss sample — not a process-local liveness noop); **telemetry** captures CPU% + memory bytes via gopsutil per-OS (Linux `/proc/stat`+`/proc/meminfo`, macOS `host_statistics`+`sysctl`, Windows `GetSystemTimes`+`GlobalMemoryStatusEx`) plus host thermal sensors when available; **interfaces** enumerates every NIC with its MAC, IP, link state, and cumulative byte+error counters via gopsutil/net merged with stdlib `net.Interfaces` for MAC/IP association; **storage** walks every local mountpoint via gopsutil/disk and skips network filesystems (NFS/SMB/AFP) by default to avoid stalls when the server is unreachable. Default cadences: 60 s response-time / 60 s telemetry / 600 s interfaces / 600 s storage — operator-overridable via `*_interval_sec` keys in `agent.conf`. A separate **system-info push** (`POST /api/v1/agents/system-info`) fires on the heartbeat cadence (default 300 s) and carries the host's identity (hostname, OS family + version + kernel, DMI manufacturer / model / serial / BIOS, primary MAC + IP, agent version itself). The server upserts an `AssetSource` row with `sourceKind = "polaris-agent"` and `externalId = managedAgent.id`, then re-projects the asset — `polaris-agent` is at the top of the projection priority for hostname / serialNumber / manufacturer / model / os / osVersion, beating AD / Entra / Intune because the agent runs ON the host and reads DMI/SMBIOS directly while inventory sources infer from elsewhere. **MAC is written inline** alongside the projection step — `primaryMac` is normalized to colon-uppercase, reconciled into `AssetMacAddress` with `source="polaris-agent"` (preserving entries from other sources), and the freshest entry becomes `Asset.macAddress`. MAC isn't owned by `projectAssetFromSources` (see "Fields the projection does NOT own"), so every discovery path including the agent writes it directly. Falls through cleanly when DMI fields are unreadable (Linux `product_serial` is often 0400 on hardened deployments; the agent's DynamicUser sees empty and AD / Intune fill in instead). Compatible source kinds: `manual`, `activedirectory`, `entraid`, `windowsserver` (anywhere we can install software via stored SSH/WinRM credentials). Explicitly NOT compatible with `fortimanager` / `fortigate` — Fortinet appliances can't run third-party binaries. When any stream resolves to `"agent"`, the periodic puller (probe + telemetry + systemInfo + fastFiltered) becomes a no-op for that stream — the agent owns its own cadence and the synthetic ticking-loop pass returns success-with-zero-write so the `polaris_probe_total{transport="agent"}` counter increments but no DB sample is created. `recordProbeResult` and the `record*Result` writers early-return on agent-mode so the agent's real pushed samples are the only signal that drives the state machine; the inbound `/api/v1/agents/samples` handler calls `recordProbeResult(..., null, { fromAgent: true })` to bypass that guard for the agent's real RTTs. Per-stream credential pickers on the asset edit modal hide when polling-method is `"agent"` — the agent's own per-asset bearer (issued at install time) is implicit.

**Polaris Agent API surface (Phase 2 — inbound + operator-facing routes).** Three inbound endpoints handle the agent runtime: `POST /api/v1/agents/enroll` is public — body carries the one-shot enrollment token plus the cert fingerprint the agent observed during its TLS handshake. The server cross-checks the pin against the value baked into the `ManagedAgent` row at install kickoff (mismatch → 400 + `agent.install_failed` event + bearer revoke), and on success atomically swaps the enrollment token for a long-lived bearer (atomic via `agentTokenService.consumeEnrollmentToken`), transitions `installStatus → "active"`, and emits `agent.enrolled`. `POST /api/v1/agents/samples` (bearer-gated via `requireAgentBearer`) accepts a discriminated-union body keyed on `stream: "responseTime" | "telemetry" | "interfaces" | "storage"` — sample rows are enqueued through `sampleWriteBuffer.enqueue*` (same 2 s batched-flush path the periodic puller uses) with `req.managedAgent.assetId` stamped server-side so a stolen bearer cannot write samples for a different asset. The probe stream additionally calls `recordProbeResult` with `fromAgent: true` to drive the five-state machine; telemetry/interfaces/storage bump the corresponding `last*At` stamps directly. `GET /api/v1/agents/config` (bearer) returns the resolved cadences + which streams are agent-mode + an `ETag` so the agent can issue `If-None-Match` to short-circuit polls when nothing changed. The handler **self-heals polling-method drift on every call** — `consumeEnrollmentToken` already stamps the asset's five per-stream polling columns to `"agent"` at enroll time, but assets enrolled before that landed (pre-commit d43b9d8) or assets where an operator manually cleared a column afterward would otherwise inherit source defaults (ICMP / not-delivered) and the agent's CPU/mem/interfaces/storage pushes would be ignored by the resolver. When `/config` sees any of `responseTimePolling` / `cpuMemoryPolling` / `temperaturePolling` / `interfacesPolling` / `lldpPolling` is not `"agent"`, it re-stamps all five in a single update, invalidates the monitor-settings cache for that scope, and writes one `monitor.polling_overridden_by_agent` Event with the prior values for audit. Idempotent — once stamped, subsequent /config polls find no drift and skip the write. `POST /api/v1/agents/heartbeat` (bearer) bumps `lastSeenAt` + refreshes `agentVersion`. The three operator-facing endpoints on `/api/v1/assets/:id/agent` are: `GET` (any auth role) returns the redacted `ManagedAgent` row (hashes stripped) or 404; `POST /install` (assets-admin) validates the credential type matches the chosen OS (ssh for linux/darwin, winrm for windows), captures the live Polaris HTTPS cert fingerprint via `httpsManager.getServerCertFingerprint()`, creates the `ManagedAgent` row in `pending`, mints a 10-min-TTL enrollment token, emits `agent.install_kickoff`, and (Phase 2) returns the enrollment token in the response so an operator can stand up the agent by hand — the SSH/WinRM file-push that automates this work lands in Phase 4 via `agentInstallService`. `DELETE` (admin-only) is a two-phase op: synchronous `revokeBearer` is always immediate; `?force=true` additionally hard-deletes the row and emits `agent.force_removed` (for unreachable hosts). Without force, the row is left in place with `installStatus="revoked"` so the Phase-4 async remote-uninstall job has something to drive.

**Polaris Agent remote install (Phase 4a — SSH).** `src/services/agentInstallService.ts` fires the async background work when the operator clicks Install Agent: open ssh2 connection → SFTP upload binary + agent.conf + installer script to `/tmp/` → `sudo -n bash /tmp/polaris-agent-install.sh` → cleanup. The installer script (embedded as a string in the service, not shipped alongside the binary so it stays version-locked with FortiOS-style "Polaris release == one bundle" semantics) writes `/usr/local/bin/polaris-agent` + `/var/lib/polaris-agent/agent.conf` (Linux — under systemd's `StateDirectory=` so the agent can rewrite it after enroll; `/etc/` is read-only via `ProtectSystem=strict`) or `/etc/polaris-agent/agent.conf` (macOS) + a systemd unit (Linux) or launchd plist (macOS). The systemd unit uses `DynamicUser=yes` so the agent runs as a non-root system-managed user; the macOS plist runs the agent as root (operators tighten this in a follow-on if needed). State transitions: `pending → uploading → enrolling → active` (the last step happens when the agent's first POST /enroll lands). The `ManagedAgent.installCredentialId` column stores which SSH credential was used, so the default DELETE path can reuse it for the remote uninstall mirror — `sudo -n bash /tmp/polaris-agent-uninstall.sh` stops + disables the service, removes the unit/plist/binary/config, and on success hard-deletes the ManagedAgent row (audit trail lives in Event). Binaries live under `<STATE_DIR>/data/agents/<version>/`; the manifest at `<STATE_DIR>/data/agents/manifest.json` names the current default version + maps platform-arch tuples to filenames. The URL stamped into `agent.conf`'s `server_url` resolves in this order: (1) `Setting.agent.serverUrlOverride` — operator-settable from Server Settings → Maintenance → Polaris Agent card via the **Server URL stamped into agent.conf** input (PUT `/server-settings/agents/server-url` with `{url}`; empty string clears the override and falls back to the cert-derived default); (2) `POLARIS_PUBLIC_URL` env var (legacy operator override, still honored for scripted deployments); (3) first DNS SAN on the running HTTPS leaf cert + live HTTPS port — the natural default, since operators generate the cert with the hostname they expect Polaris to be reachable at and the agent's pin is verifying that exact cert; (4) cert CN + port; (5) first IP SAN + port (covers IP-only certs in isolated networks); (6) `POLARIS_PUBLIC_HOST` env + PORT; (7) `localhost` + PORT. The install kickoff route guards against producing `https://localhost:<port>` for a non-loopback target. Reverse-proxy / TLS-termination shops set either the UI override or `POLARIS_PUBLIC_URL` so the agent dials the proxy's public hostname rather than the upstream's internal IP. The Polaris Agent card surfaces the **effective** URL pre-filled (so operators can see what install would use right now) and the **cert-derived default** as helper text so it's obvious what they'd inherit if they cleared the override.

**Polaris Agent remote install (Phase 4b — WinRM).** Windows hosts install via the same agentInstallService entry point but use a different transport: instead of SFTP-uploading the binary, the PowerShell installer running on the host **pulls the binary over HTTPS** from a new public endpoint `GET /api/v1/agents/binary/:filename` (filename whitelist-checked against `data/agents/manifest.json`, directory-traversal protected, no-store cache). The pull is gated by a server-cert pin: `[Net.ServicePointManager]::ServerCertificateValidationCallback` is wired to compare the leaf SHA-256 against the pin embedded in the PowerShell command, then restored after the download. We avoid implementing the WS-Management `Send` verb (chunked stdin file upload) entirely — saves ~1000 lines of XML envelope code that's only used here. The WinRS shell verbs we DO implement live in `src/utils/winrm.ts`: `CreateShell` → `RunCommand` → `Receive` (polled loop until `CommandState=Done`) → `DeleteShell` (always in a `finally`). PowerShell is invoked via `-EncodedCommand` (UTF-16-LE base64) so command-line escaping never bites. The installer creates the service via `New-Service` (canonical, ships in every Windows since 5.1) and stamps recovery actions via `sc.exe failure`. Deliberately avoids `Get-WmiObject Win32_Service` (DCOM-based, removed in PowerShell 7+) and `Remove-Service` (PowerShell 6+ only, missing from stock Server 2019); uses `sc.exe delete` for the uninstall. The host's `%ProgramFiles%\Polaris\Agent\polaris-agent.exe` + `%ProgramData%\Polaris\agent\agent.conf` are written with admin-only ACLs (the bearer is in agent.conf so it must not be world-readable). Same install lifecycle as SSH: `pending → uploading → enrolling → active`, with the agent's first POST /enroll flipping to active. WinRM needs to be enabled on the target host on port 5986 (HTTPS preferred) or 5985 (HTTP fallback); the credential must have local admin rights.

**Polaris Agent in-app build (Server Settings → Maintenance → Polaris Agent).** Operators click Build to produce all six platform binaries (linux/darwin/windows × amd64/arm64) right from the web UI; no shell access needed. Driven by `src/services/agentBuildService.ts` which spawns one `go build` per platform serially (parallel calls thrash the shared GOCACHE), writes binaries under `<STATE_DIR>/data/agents/<version>/`, and writes `manifest.json` atomically (`.tmp` + rename) only after all six succeed. Version comes from `getAgentVersion()` (reads `agent/VERSION` text file with a 5s mtime cache) — DECOUPLED from `getAppVersion()` so Polaris patch releases that don't touch `agent/` produce zero build noise. Install scripts (`deploy/setup-rhel.sh`, `setup-ubuntu.sh`, `setup-windows.ps1` and their `-nodb` variants) install Go alongside Node; the Dockerfile pulls `golang-go` from bookworm-backports. UI gates the Build button on `goAvailable()` and surfaces a clear "install Go and reload" notice when missing. The card shows a per-platform inventory grid (size / built-when / present-✓), a drift hint when `manifest.currentVersion` lags `agent/VERSION`, a queued-builds strip (FIFO depth 3; 4th simultaneous click 409s with "queue full"), per-build × cancel buttons, and an Auto-build toggle. **Build queueing:** two operators clicking near-simultaneously land in a stable FIFO order (Node's single thread guarantees no interleave between the queue-length check and the enqueue); each build re-reads `getAgentVersion()` at start-of-run so a queued build picks up a VERSION bump that landed during its wait. **Cancellation:** `state.activeChild` holds the running `go build` ChildProcess; DELETE sends SIGTERM, then SIGKILL after 5s grace. The `manifest.json` is only written after ALL platforms succeed so a cancelled build leaves partial binaries under `data/agents/<version>/` but the previous manifest still points at the previous version's files (operator-visible: nothing happened). **Per-version retention:** after every successful build the helper prunes deletable version directories — deletable = NOT `manifest.currentVersion` AND NOT in use by any live `ManagedAgent` AND NOT within the most-recent N (env `POLARIS_AGENT_KEEP_VERSIONS`, default 3). A manual "Clean up old binaries" button on the card fires the same helper. **Auto-build:** `src/jobs/autoBuildAgents.ts` fires 60s after boot, refuses unless `manifest.json` exists (operator opted in), Polaris's `getAgentVersion()` differs from `manifest.currentVersion`, Go is available, AND `Setting.agent.autoBuildOnVersionMismatch !== false`. Actor stamped as `"system:auto-build-on-version-change"` so the audit trail distinguishes this from operator clicks. Skipped builds emit `agent.build.auto_skipped` with the reason so operators see the staleness on the Events page.

**Polaris Agent upgrade-already-installed (`POST /assets/:id/agent/upgrade` + bulk).** `startUpgrade` in `src/services/agentInstallService.ts` does an SSH/WinRM-driven binary swap that preserves `agent.conf` — bearer + cert pin survive, agent reconnects with the same identity. SSH path SFTPs the new binary to `/tmp/polaris-agent.new` and runs an embedded upgrade shell script that stops the systemd unit / launchd job, `install -m 0755`s over the old binary, restarts. WinRM path runs an embedded PowerShell `-EncodedCommand` that re-uses the install-flow's cert-pin-verified `Invoke-WebRequest` to pull the new binary from `/api/v1/agents/binary/:filename`, then `Stop-Service` → atomic `Move-Item` → `Start-Service`. State transitions: `active → upgrading → active` on success, `active → upgrade_failed` on remote error. Per-asset Upgrade button on the asset details modal Agent panel (active agents); bulk Upgrade-all line on the Maintenance Polaris Agent card with a Promise pool of 4. The bulk path's "N of M out-of-date" line is backed by `GET /server-settings/agents/installed-summary` which returns a per-version histogram (cheap groupBy on `ManagedAgent.agentVersion`).

The `ManagedAgent` table tracks each install (lifecycle: pending → uploading → enrolling → active → revoked / uninstalled / upgrading / upgrade_failed; one row per asset, FK cascade). See the plan at `~/.claude/plans/the-app-needs-a-glowing-knuth.md` for the implementation roadmap.

**Where the polling method comes from.** Per-stream dropdowns appear at four tiers in the UI:
- **Manual tier** — Assets page → Monitoring Settings modal → Manual Monitoring section. Accepts any method (covers any source).
- **Integration tier** — each integration's edit modal → Monitoring tab → Cadence & Retention section. Methods filtered to the integration's source kind.
- **Class override** — Assets page → Monitoring Settings modal → Class Overrides. Methods re-render when the source picker changes; integration-scoped overrides are pinned to the integration's source kind.
- **Per-asset** — asset edit modal → Monitoring tab. Methods filtered to the asset's source kind.

`/api/v1/assets/:id/effective-monitor-settings` returns the resolved values plus per-field provenance (`asset` / `class` / `integration` / `manual`) so the asset edit modal can render tier badges. Each "Inherit" option in every dropdown is labeled with the resolver's expected fallback ("Source default: REST API" / "Source default: ICMP" / "Not delivered for this source") so operators see what they'd inherit without clicking through.

**Credential resolution.** A polling method that needs authentication (`snmp`, `winrm`, `ssh`, `rest_api`) walks four tiers, most-specific first: per-stream asset credential (`Asset.responseTimeCredentialId` / `telemetryCredentialId` / `interfacesCredentialId` / `lldpCredentialId`) → asset's generic `Asset.monitorCredentialId` → class-override per-stream credential (`MonitorClassOverride.responseTimeCredentialId` / etc — surfaced by `resolveMonitorSettings` onto the resolved `*CredentialId` fields, looked up in the dispatcher via `loadClassOverrideStreamCredential`) → integration fallback. The integration fallback is FMG/FortiGate-discovered firewalls reading `Integration.config.monitorCredentialId` (SNMP) or the stored API token (REST API), and AD-discovered hosts reading the integration's `bindDn` / `bindPassword`. The asset's monitor credential picker is type-agnostic — the operator pairs it with their chosen polling method. Credential / method mismatches fall through silently at probe time to a clear error; setting a SNMP credential on an asset whose `responseTimePolling` is `winrm` is allowed but the probe will report "No WinRM credential selected" until one is attached.

**Branch-class FortiGate workaround.** Small-branch FortiGates (60F/61F/91G class) sometimes 404 the FortiOS REST sensor-info / interface endpoints on FortiOS 7.4.x. Operators flip the relevant streams to `snmp` at the integration tier (covers the whole fleet) or per-asset (one box at a time) — the resolver routes the affected streams over `FORTINET-FORTIGATE-MIB::fgHwSensors` + IF-MIB while leaving the working streams on REST API. **IPsec tunnels always go via REST** regardless of the interfaces polling method — SNMP has no equivalent and we don't want flipping "Interfaces" off REST to silently kill IPsec history. LLDP is decoupled from interfaces because the FortiOS REST endpoint and SNMP LLDP-MIB don't always agree on coverage; the transport resolver dispatches LLDP independently in `collectSystemInfo`, overlaying a `collectLldpOnlySnmp` / `collectLldpOnlyFortinet` call when the LLDP method differs from the interfaces method.

**REST API telemetry + temperature for managed FortiAPs.** The same `/api/v2/monitor/wifi/managed_ap` row that the probe path consumes carries `cpu_usage`, `mem_free` / `mem_total` (in MB), and `sensors_temperatures` per AP. `fetchFortinetControllerInventory` parses those fields once at fetch time into `entry.apTelemetry` so the 30 s controller-inventory cache absorbs all three consumers (probe + telemetry + temperature) — one upstream call per controller serves N APs on every telemetry tick. When the resolved `cpuMemoryPolling` is `rest_api` on an `access_point` asset, `collectTelemetry` dispatches to `collectTelemetryFortiapRest` which converts MB → bytes (`memTotalBytes = memTotalMb × 1024²`, `memUsedBytes = (memTotal − memFree) × 1024²`) and writes the standard `AssetTelemetrySample` shape; `memPct` is left null because the bytes form is more useful for charts across AP models with different RAM sizes. When the resolved `temperaturePolling` is `rest_api`, `collectTemperatures` dispatches to `collectTemperaturesFortiapRest` which projects `sensors_temperatures` into the standard `AssetTemperatureSample[]` shape (one row per sensor). APs whose model doesn't publish CPU/memory/temperature (some older indoor units) come back with `data: []` or null fields rather than an error — the System tab renders "no sensors" instead of a failure pill. Managed FortiSwitches still have no REST telemetry path (the controller-status table reports up/down only); operators wanting switch telemetry flip to SNMP at the integration or per-asset tier.

**REST API probe path for managed FortiSwitches / FortiAPs.** Managed FortiSwitches and FortiAPs aren't directly REST-able — the only authoritative up/down signal is the parent FortiGate's `managed-switch/status` (switches) or `wifi/managed_ap` (APs) inventory table. When the resolved `responseTimePolling` is `rest_api` AND the asset's `assetType` is `switch` or `access_point` AND the asset was discovered through an FMG or FortiGate integration, `probeAsset` dispatches to `probeFortinetController` instead of hitting the asset's own IP. The helper resolves the controller via `Asset.fortinetTopology.controllerFortigate` (FMG) or the integration's own host (standalone FortiGate), fetches the appropriate inventory once, and looks up the asset by serial. `status === "Connected"` (switches) or `status ∈ {"online","connected"}` (APs) is success; everything else (including "not present in the controller's table") is failure. The **per-controller inventory cache** is module-local in `monitoringService.ts` with a 30s TTL keyed on `(integrationId, deviceName, "switches"|"aps")` — N switches or APs sharing one controller make exactly one controller call per cache window, important for FMG proxy mode (concurrency=1). The result flows through the standard `recordProbeResult` → five-state machine → `failureThreshold` → `monitor.status_changed` Event pipeline like any other probe, so a switch that goes Disconnected and stays that way for `failureThreshold` consecutive probes (default 3) ends up as `down`. Cadence implication: at the default 60s `intervalSeconds` and threshold 3, "down" detection takes ~3 minutes; operators on slower cadences should adjust accordingly.

**Per-class FortiSwitch / FortiAP direct polling and auto-Monitor flag:** The FMG/FortiGate integration's `config` JSON accepts `fortiswitchMonitor` and `fortiapMonitor` blocks, each `{ enabled: boolean, snmpCredentialId: string | null, sshCredentialId: string | null, addAsMonitored: boolean, autoMonitorInterfaces: AutoMonitorSelection | null, streams: ClassStreamsBlock }`, plus a `fortigateMonitor: { addAsMonitored: boolean, autoMonitorInterfaces: AutoMonitorSelection | null, pullSnmpLocation: boolean, pushGeocodedCoords: boolean, streams: ClassStreamsBlock }` block. **Phase 2** added the `streams` field on each class block — the per-stream config consumed by the resolver under that class's assetType (see "Monitor Settings Hierarchy" above). AD / Entra / Windows Server gained matching `workstationMonitor` / `serverMonitor` blocks with the same `streams` shape.
