Shape per stream cell: `{ polling: PollingMethod | null, credentialId: uuid | null, intervalSeconds: number | null, timeoutMs: number | null, mibId: uuid | null, failureThreshold?: number | null }` (failureThreshold only honored on `responseTime`; FortiAP omits the `storage` stream entirely). The integration-tier resolved `responseTimePolling` decides which credential gets stamped on a freshly-discovered switch/AP — `ssh` picks `sshCredentialId`, anything else falls back to `snmpCredentialId`. The `autoMonitorInterfaces` field is the "Auto-Monitor Interfaces" selection — see the dedicated subsection below for its three modes. Edited from the **FortiSwitches** and **FortiAPs** subtabs (full block) and the **FortiGates** subtab (auto-Monitor checkbox only) of the Monitoring tab. The two switch/AP flags are independent and drive these discovery-time outcomes:

| `enabled` (direct polling) | `addAsMonitored` | Stamped on new switch/AP |
|---|---|---|
| false | false | ensure monitored=false (sweep off) |
| false | true | `monitored=true`; resolver picks ICMP source default |
| true (with credential) | false | `monitorCredentialId=<id>`; ensure monitored=false (sweep off) |
| true (with credential) | true | `monitorCredentialId=<id>`, `monitored=true` |

Discovery always stamps `discoveredByIntegrationId=<integration>` on any of these "non-empty" rows so the resolver can pick the source default polling method. Existing switch/AP assets are *only* re-stamped with a credential when their existing `monitorCredentialId` is null OR matches the integration's class credential — operator-pointed credentials are preserved. The `monitored` flip is gated by `Asset.monitorOverride`: discovery enforces `monitored = addAsMonitored` on every cycle EXCEPT when override is true (operator's explicit divergence). Override is computed at operator-write time as `monitored XOR addAsMonitored` and clears the moment the two converge — so re-enabling monitoring on an Auto-Monitor=ON integration's asset (or disabling on an Auto-Monitor=OFF) takes the asset back out of override on the same write.

For **FortiGates**, `fortigateMonitor.addAsMonitored` drives a bidirectional sweep on the same model — `buildFortigateMonitorStamp()` runs on both create and update paths and sets monitored to match the flag (skipping HA standby members and asset rows with `monitorOverride=true`). The polling method comes from the resolver (REST API source default for fortimanager / fortigate sources).

For **AD / Entra / Windows Server** integrations, `workstationMonitor.addAsMonitored` and `serverMonitor.addAsMonitored` carry the same semantics on workstation- and server-typed assets. The Entra and AD sync paths call `buildMonitoredSweep()` from `src/services/monitorOverrideService.ts` on every asset write to enforce the flag.

**Integration save sweep.** When the operator changes any per-class `addAsMonitored` value on an integration's edit modal and clicks Save Changes, the PUT /integrations/:id handler runs `sweepMonitoredForIntegration()` (bulk SQL UPDATE that sets `monitored = addAsMonitored` on every non-override asset under this integration matching the affected class) followed by `recomputeMonitorOverrideForIntegration()` (refresh override across every asset under this integration so previously-overridden assets that just converged auto-clear). Writes one `integration.auto_monitor_swept` Event with the touched-asset count when the sweep was non-trivial. Pairs with the frontend confirm modal (see "Operator confirm flow" below) that protects against accidental fleet-wide disables.

**Operator confirm flow.** The Add/Edit integration modal's Monitoring tab → per-class subtabs render an **Enable Auto-Monitoring** (blue) / **Disable Auto-Monitoring** (red) button instead of a checkbox (`_classAddAsMonitoredHTML` and `_fortigateAddMonitoredHTML` in `public/js/integrations.js`). The button toggles a hidden checkbox of the same id the save path already reads, so persistence is unchanged. On Save Changes the frontend POSTs the proposed flag map to `/integrations/:id/auto-monitor-assets/preflight` and, when any class reports `wouldDisable > 0`, surfaces a confirm modal listing the per-class impact ("Disabling Auto-Monitoring will sweep monitoring OFF for N FortiSwitch(es) — P protected by per-asset override"). Decline aborts the save with no API call.

**Auto-Monitor Interfaces (per-class):** Each `*Monitor` block above also carries an `autoMonitorInterfaces` field that pre-selects which interfaces on every discovered asset of that class are added to `Asset.monitoredInterfaces` (the "Poll 1m" pin-list scraped on the response-time cadence). Multi-block union — each block is optional, presence = on, and the apply pass takes the **union** across whichever blocks are present:

```ts
autoMonitorInterfaces: {
  byNames?:    { names: string[] }                                                    // explicit ifNames from the aggregated checklist
  byPatterns?: { patterns: string[]; regex: boolean; onlyUp: boolean }                // wildcards (* and ?) when regex=false, raw anchor-free regex when regex=true
  byTypes?:    { types: Array<"physical"|"aggregate"|"vlan"|"loopback"|"tunnel">; onlyUp: boolean }
  byLldp?:     { neighborTypes: Array<"firewall"|"switch"|"access_point"|"server"|"workstation"|"router"|"printer"|"other"> }
} | null                                                                              // null (or all keys missing) = feature off
```

Edited from a card on each Monitoring subtab (FortiGates / FortiSwitches / FortiAPs). Each block has its own master checkbox so operators can mix and match (e.g. "By pattern: `wan*` + By LLDP: switch" on FortiSwitches). `onlyUp` filters candidate interfaces to those with `operStatus === "up"` on their latest `AssetInterfaceSample`; available on byPatterns + byTypes, intentionally not on byNames (explicit names always pin even when the link is down). Default `onlyUp: true` for byTypes, `false` for byPatterns. **By LLDP** semantics (UI label: **By LLDP (includes inferred interfaces)**): for each interface on the discovered asset, look up its `AssetLldpNeighbor` row AND any peer-inferred edge from `Asset.fortinetTopology` and pin if any match resolves to a Polaris asset whose `assetType` is in the selected set AND `monitored=true` — automatically tracks fleet topology (a new FortiGate uplink to a managed switch gets pinned on the next discovery, no operator action). The inferred half (`loadInferredLldpByAsset` in `autoMonitorInterfacesService.ts`) covers managed FortiAPs whose FortiSwitch consumes LLDP without re-publishing via SNMP LLDP-MIB — same data source as the inferred Neighbor column on the System tab (see `peerInferredLldpService`). Class-aware: `fortigate` walks switches whose `controllerFortigate` names this FortiGate and pins on `switch.uplinkInterface`; `fortiswitch` walks APs whose `parentSwitch` names this switch and pins on `ap.parentPort`; `fortiap` resolves the AP's own `parentSwitch` + `uplinkInterface` and pins on the AP-local port. Apply logic lives in `autoMonitorInterfacesService.ts` (pure resolver `resolvePinnedInterfaces` + DB-bound `applyAutoMonitorForClass`); the apply pass runs as Phase 2c at the end of every successful discovery and from the integration edit modal's **Save Changes** button via `POST /integrations/:id/interface-aggregate/apply`, which runs once per per-class block whose selection is non-null so operators don't have to wait for the next discovery cycle. **Strictly additive**: never strips operator-pinned interfaces, only adds. Removing a name from the selection on subsequent saves doesn't unpin it on existing assets — operator-owned. The "By name" UI pulls its checklist from `GET /integrations/:id/interface-aggregate?class=...`, which aggregates latest-per-(asset,ifName) `AssetInterfaceSample` rows and returns one row per distinct ifName with a device count and the matching device list. **The "By interface type" checklist is sourced from the same aggregate response** at lazy-load time (frontend filters `rows[*].ifType` against the canonical `IF_TYPES` set in `autoMonitorInterfacesService.ts`) so operators only see ifType values that actually appear on this integration's discovered devices — a fleet whose tunnels register as FortiOS `ipsec` (normalized to `tunnel` in `normalizeFortiIfType`) shows `tunnel`; a fleet with no tunnels won't show it at all. Saved-selection types not present in the latest aggregate are preserved across renders so a stored value isn't silently dropped. The card's live preview block calls `POST /integrations/:id/interface-aggregate/preview` with the in-flight selection (across all enabled blocks) AND the previously-sent selection as `baselineSelection`; the response carries a `diff: { addedCount, removedCount, addedSample, removedSample }` block that the card renders inline as `Change since last edit: +X added · −Y removed (hostname/ifName, …)` so operators see exactly what each checkbox toggle just changed. First preview-after-modal-open uses the saved selection as baseline (so diff is 0); each subsequent toggle's diff is per-click. The **Test against fleet** button under the By pattern block fires the same preview endpoint with *only* the patterns block populated so the operator can verify their regex without noise. Results above the rough warn threshold (currently 500 pinned interfaces total across the three classes — defined as `AUTO_MONITOR_INTERFACE_WARN_THRESHOLD` in `public/js/integrations.js`) trigger a confirm modal on Save Changes. Legacy single-mode shape (`{mode: "names"|"wildcard"|"type", ...}`) from earlier releases is auto-coerced on read (Zod preprocess + apply path + frontend renderer all call `coerceLegacySelection`) and one-shot-migrated by `migrateAutoMonitorInterfacesShape` at boot.

**Decommission sweep for managed switches/APs.** Discovery tracks two new `DiscoveryResult` arrays — `switchInventoriedDevices` and `apInventoriedDevices` — listing the controller FortiGates whose `managed-switch/status` / `wifi/managed_ap` query returned successfully (including 404, which means the feature isn't licensed but the controller is reachable). At the end of the run, in the same pass that deprecates stale subnets (Phase 2), `syncDhcpSubnets` flips any switch/AP whose `discoveredByIntegrationId` matches this integration AND whose `fortinetTopology.controllerFortigate` is in the inventoried-devices set AND whose serial/hostname is no longer in the discovery's sighting set to `status="decommissioned"`. Switches/APs behind a controller whose inventory query *failed or timed out* are left alone (we didn't get a fresh answer). Re-discovery by serial number flips a decommissioned asset back to `active` (or `storage` for FortiSwitches reported as `Unauthorized`). Each decommission writes one `asset.fortiswitch.decommissioned` or `asset.fortiap.decommissioned` Event with the reason `missing-from-controller`.

**CMDB-vs-monitor query policy (proxy mode):** All CMDB-side data is queried natively from FMG (`/pm/config/device/<name>/...` or `/dvmdb/...`), NOT via `/sys/proxy/json`. Native FMG calls don't hit the per-device proxy throttle, so they parallelize freely even when `useProxy=true`. Only **live monitor** endpoints — DHCP leases, ARP, switch-controller detected-device, managed-switch/AP status, wireless-controller managed_ap, system resource usage — go through the proxy because they must be served fresh from the FortiGate. Currently native-from-FMG: device roster + per-device interface config + DHCP server CMDB + interface IPs + firewall VIPs + system/global (geo coords). Currently proxied (or direct): all `/api/v2/monitor/...` endpoints. The mgmt-IP resolver (`resolveDeviceMgmtIp`) reads from `/pm/config/device/<name>/global/system/interface` filtered by the integration's configured `mgmtInterface` — *not* the `ip` field on the FMG device DB record (that field can be a public/NAT IP and is not what Polaris should use to reach the device).

**Per-device transport (`useProxy` toggle):** The FMG integration has two per-device query transports, selectable in the integration edit modal. The UI checkbox is labeled "Query each FortiGate directly (bypass FortiManager proxy)" — *checked = direct, unchecked = proxy*. The on-disk field is still `useProxy` (true=proxy); the UI just inverts the semantics so the more aggressive option (direct) is the explicit affirmative action. The modal also surfaces a "more than 20 FortiGates → switch to direct" recommendation since proxy mode polls one device at a time.
- **Proxy mode** (default, `useProxy: true`): live `/api/v2/monitor/...` queries funnel through FMG's `/sys/proxy/json`. Parallelism is force-clamped to 1 because FMG drops parallel proxy connections past very low parallelism, surfacing as `fetch failed` on random calls. CMDB-side queries still run native-from-FMG and are NOT subject to this throttle (see "CMDB-vs-monitor query policy" above).
- **Direct mode** (`useProxy: false`): FMG is only used to enumerate the managed FortiGate roster + resolve mgmt IPs (still via the interface CMDB lookup, not the device-DB `ip` field — that one can be a NAT/public IP). Per-device live queries go direct to each FortiGate's management IP using shared REST API credentials stored in `config.fortigateApiUser` / `config.fortigateApiToken`. Each managed FortiGate must have the same REST API admin provisioned with a trusthost that includes Polaris. Delegates per-device work to `fortigateService.discoverDhcpSubnets` and remaps the device name back to FMG's label. Unlocks `discoveryParallelism` (up to 20).

**Reservation push (`pushReservations` toggle):** Both the **FortiManager** and **standalone FortiGate** integrations expose a `pushReservations: boolean` flag (default `false`) in their `config` JSON, edited from the **DHCP Push** tab on the integration's Add/Edit modal. When enabled, every manual reservation created on a subnet that this integration discovered is written to the FortiGate at create time; the Polaris row only flips `sourceType="manual" → "dhcp_reservation"` and stamps `pushStatus="synced"` after the write+verify round-trips successfully. **Transient device-side failures** (FortiGate offline, FMG unreachable, network timeout, generic 5xx — classified by `classifyPushError` in `reservationPushService.ts`) **persist the Polaris row with `pushStatus="pending"`** and the `retryQueuedReservationPushes` job pushes it once the gate recovers (see "Queued push lifecycle" below). **Permanent failures** (4xx, verify mismatch, auth failure) still abort the create — no Polaris ghost when the device write fundamentally can't land. Transport is dispatched by `buildTransportForIntegration()` in `reservationPushService.ts` based on integration type:
- **FMG, proxy realtime** (`type=fortimanager`, `useProxy: true`): the reserved-address row is written via FMG's `/sys/proxy/json` endpoint (`fmgProxyRest()` in `fortimanagerService.ts`) — FMG forwards the call to the FortiGate using its own stored device credentials. Lands on the FortiGate's running config in real time; FMG sees the change on its next config sync.
- **FMG, direct realtime** (`type=fortimanager`, `useProxy: false`): the device's management IP is resolved via FMG (`resolveDeviceMgmtIpViaFmg()`), then the write goes straight to the FortiGate's REST API using `config.fortigateApiUser` / `config.fortigateApiToken`.
- **Standalone FortiGate** (`type=fortigate`): the write goes directly to the FortiGate's REST API using the integration's own `apiUser` / `apiToken`. There is no FMG layer to choose, so `useProxy` doesn't apply.

Push scope resolution: `reservationPushService.findScopeIdForCidr` matches the subnet's CIDR against the FortiGate's `system.dhcp.server` table by reconstructing each scope's network from `default-gateway` + `netmask` (fall back: `ip-range[0].start-ip` inside the subnet). The matched `scopeId` and the new entry's `entryId` are stamped on the Reservation as `pushedScopeId` / `pushedEntryId` so unpush hits the exact device-side row without re-resolving. **Required FortiManager admin profile changes:** `Device Manager → Manage Device Configurations → Read-Write` is the only sub-permission that needs to flip from the existing read-only baseline; all other Device Manager and Policy & Objects sub-items can stay where they are. Importantly, `Install Policy Package or Device Configuration` should remain **None** — Polaris never triggers installs in this path. The blast radius warning surfaced in the modal is real: FMG profiles have no per-object DHCP-reservation knob, so granting Manage Device Configurations write enables write across every CMDB tree on every FortiGate in the ADOM. The modal also recommends direct mode + a per-FortiGate Custom profile (`Network → Custom → Configuration → Read/Write`) for shops that want least-privilege.

Description written to the FortiGate carries two formats keyed on whether operator notes are set: `Polaris/<username>: <notes> [<hostname>]` when notes are non-empty, `Polaris/<username>: <hostname>` when notes are blank (`Polaris: …` when no authenticated user is in scope, which currently never happens through the UI but covers system-initiated paths). The notes-first ordering exists because operators type Notes specifically as the FortiGate-side reservation comment; the bracketed hostname trails so `subnetRefreshService.extractHostnameFromDescription` can still recover a hostname during subnet refresh if Polaris ever loses it. Capped at 64 chars to fit comfortably in the FortiOS reserved-address `description` field across versions (older FortiOS 6.2 capped at 35; 7.x supports up to 255) — under truncation the trailing `[<hostname>]` is what gets clipped first, which is the right call (notes are the operator's intent; hostname is recoverable from Polaris itself). The "Polaris/" prefix lets a FortiGate admin looking at the device immediately identify entries written by Polaris and who pushed them.

`sourceType` flip on push success: when the FortiGate write verifies, the Polaris row's `sourceType` is updated from `manual` to `dhcp_reservation`. Two reasons: (1) the entry really is a DHCP reservation on the device now, and the UI badges should reflect that; (2) the next discovery run sees a matching `dhcp_reservation` row and the existing conflict-detection gate at `integrations.ts:2402` (`if (existingRes.sourceType === "manual") upsertConflict(...)`) is bypassed cleanly — no spurious conflict raised against our own echo. `pushedToId` remains the audit-trail answer to "did Polaris push this?" (and the `reservation.push.succeeded` Event also pins the origin in the audit log).

**Queued push lifecycle.** When create-time push hits a transient failure (or the pre-flight check sees the firewall asset is monitored AND `monitorStatus="down"` and skips the transport attempt entirely), the Polaris reservation is **kept** with `pushStatus="pending"`, `pushQueuedAt=now`, `pushAttempts=0..1` (0 on the monitored-down pre-flight skip, 1 after a real failed attempt), and `pushError=<message>`. `sourceType` stays `"manual"` because nothing has been written to the device yet — only successful push flips it. `pushedToId` IS stamped at queue time so the UI can render the target integration name without a second fetch, but no `pushedScopeId`/`pushedEntryId` (the device-side row doesn't exist yet).

The `retryQueuedReservationPushes` job (60s tick in `src/jobs/retryQueuedReservationPushes.ts`) drives `reservationService.retryPendingReservations`, which scans every `pushStatus="pending" AND status="active"` row, oldest first by `pushQueuedAt`. Each row passes through four per-row gates before the push attempt:

1. **Eligibility re-check** — subnet deprecated → cancel queue (`pushStatus=null`); subnet's `fortigateDevice` cleared → cancel; integration deleted / disabled → cancel; `pushReservations` flipped to false → cancel. Cancellation never deletes the Polaris row — operator's claim on the IP stands; they just won't get a device push for it. Writes `reservation.push.queued.cancelled` Event with the specific reason.
2. **Discovery-supersede** — another active reservation now at this `(subnetId, ipAddress)` from a discovery cycle? Flip to `pushStatus="failed_permanent"` with `pushError="IP collided during queue — discovered <sourceType> by <MAC>"`. Discovery row wins. Writes `reservation.push.queued.collided` (warning).
3. **Monitored-gate gate** — firewall Asset (joined on `hostname=fortigateDevice + discoveredByIntegrationId`) is monitored AND `monitorStatus !== "up"` → skip without incrementing `pushAttempts`. Recovery is signaled by the `monitor.status_changed → up` hook below.
4. **Unmonitored backoff** — no firewall Asset found, or `monitored=false` → compute backoff window `min(60 * 2^(pushAttempts-1), 1800)` seconds. If `pushLastAttemptAt + window > now`, skip without incrementing.

Past all four gates: increment `pushAttempts`, stamp `pushLastAttemptAt`, call `pushReservation`. Outcome stamped: success → standard `sourceType="dhcp_reservation"` + `pushStatus="synced"` + push pointers + clear queue cols + `reservation.push.queued.succeeded` (info). Transient failure → leave pending, update `pushError`, `reservation.push.queued.retry_failed` at **info** level (intentionally low-volume so a multi-day outage doesn't spam warning). Permanent failure → flip to `pushStatus="failed_permanent"` + `reservation.push.queued.failed_permanent` (warning) — operator action required.

**Latency-optimized retry on gate recovery.** `monitoringService.recordProbeResult` fires `triggerRetryAfterStatusChange(assetId)` whenever an asset transitions to `monitorStatus="up"`. The helper count-gates on `prisma.reservation.count({where: {pushStatus: "pending", subnet: {fortigateDevice: asset.hostname, discoveredBy: asset.discoveredByIntegrationId}}})` — zero pending = early return — so most up-transitions cost one indexed COUNT(*). When the count is non-zero, fire-and-forget invokes `retryPendingReservations` so the queued rows on the recovered FortiGate's subnets push within seconds of the gate coming back, instead of waiting up to 60s for the periodic tick.

**Edit / release while queued.** `updateReservation` and `releaseReservation` both early-branch on `pushStatus === "pending"`: edits just rewrite the queued payload (no device contact; MAC may be freely changed, still required for push-eligible subnets), and releases skip both the `unpushReservation` call AND the lease-release call (nothing on the device to clean up) — the cleaner `reservation.push.queued.released` (info) Event fires instead of the `reservation.unpush.failed` warning the regular release path would log.

**Operator-triggered retry.** `POST /reservations/:id/retry-push` (ownership-gated — own rows for `reservations:write`, all rows for `fullwrite`) calls `retryReservationNow` which bypasses the monitored-gate gate AND the unmonitored-backoff window. Still respects eligibility re-check and discovery-supersede. Allowed for `pushStatus IN ("pending", "failed_permanent")` so an operator can rescue a permanently-failed row after fixing whatever the permanent error called out — `failed_permanent` is flipped back to `pending` first so the retry path treats it uniformly.

**Discovery collision handling** in both `subnetRefreshService.ts` (per-subnet Refresh) and the full-discovery `syncDhcpSubnets` in `integrations.ts` adds two new behaviors when a fresh DHCP entry collides with an existing pending row at the same IP: (a) **fast-path adopt** — fresh entry is a `dhcp_reservation` with matching MAC → promote the pending row in place to `synced` with `pushedScopeId`/`pushedEntryId` stamped from the discovered entry; covers the "operator added it on the device while we were waiting" race and the "previous retry succeeded but Polaris missed the response" case; (b) **hard collision** — fresh entry is a `dhcp_lease` OR a `dhcp_reservation` with a DIFFERENT MAC → flip the pending row to `pushStatus="failed_permanent"` and SKIP the discovery create at this IP (the unique-on-active constraint would block it anyway). Operator must release the failed_permanent row to free the IP. To enable the fast-path-adopt to stamp device-side pointers without a separate REST roundtrip, the `DiscoveredDhcpEntry` shape in `fortimanagerService.ts` + `fortigateService.ts` carries `scopeId` + `entryId` (populated from `server.id` + `entry.id` at CMDB-read time) on `dhcp-reservation` entries.

Frontend MAC enforcement: the subnet IPs endpoint (`GET /subnets/:id/ips`) attaches a `pushEligible: boolean` field to its `subnet` payload, derived from `(integration.type === "fortimanager" || integration.type === "fortigate") && integration.config.pushReservations === true && fortigateDevice` (and IPv4 only — IPv6 reservations are not push-eligible in v1). The IP-panel reserve and auto-allocate modals read this and (a) re-label the MAC field as "MAC Address *" with a hint naming the target FortiGate, and (b) refuse to submit without a MAC. The backend `reservationService.createReservation` enforces the same rule server-side as the source of truth.

Release-time unpush is best-effort — a device-side failure logs `reservation.unpush.failed` (warning) but does not block the Polaris release, so an offline FortiGate doesn't stop operators from letting go of an IP. **MAC update on pushed reservations:** `PUT /reservations/:id` accepts `macAddress` (string; empty clears the stored MAC). When the originating subnet is push-eligible AND the MAC value changes, `reservationService.updateReservation` calls `updatePushedReservation()` in [reservationPushService.ts](src/services/reservationPushService.ts) which PUTs the new MAC + a refreshed description to the FortiOS reserved-address entry and verifies by read-back BEFORE the Polaris row is written — a device failure throws and the Polaris row stays untouched, so the two views never drift. Two ownership paths: Polaris-pushed reservations use the pinned `pushedScopeId`/`pushedEntryId`; discovered (never-pushed) reservations resolve the scope by CIDR and find the entry by IP, and the row is upgraded to "Polaris-pushed" on success. The FortiOS `description` is regenerated from the (possibly updated) hostname so it stays current as a side effect — but Polaris does NOT re-push for hostname-only changes; the description only refreshes when MAC changes. Audit lands as `reservation.push.updated` (info) on success or `reservation.push.update_failed` (warning) on failure. Clearing a MAC on a push-eligible subnet is rejected with 400 (DHCP reservations are MAC→IP — operators release the reservation instead). IP changes are still out of scope: an IP move = release + re-reserve.

**Quarantine push (`pushQuarantine` toggle):** Both the **FortiManager** and **standalone FortiGate** integrations expose a `pushQuarantine: boolean` flag (default `false`) in their `config` JSON, edited from the **Quarantine Push** tab on the integration's Add/Edit modal (alongside the DHCP Push tab). When enabled, `quarantineAsset()` pushes MAC-based address-group entries to every FortiGate sighted by this integration within the `quarantine.sightingMaxAgeDays` window. If the toggle is `false` (or absent), quarantine push is skipped entirely for that integration's sightings — the asset's `quarantineTargets` array is left empty (or only populated from integrations that do have the toggle enabled). Release-time unpush iterates the recorded `quarantineTargets` directly (which only contain integrations that were enabled at push time) — toggling off `pushQuarantine` after quarantine has been pushed does not prevent unpush on release. Transport is dispatched by `buildTransportForIntegration()` in `assetQuarantineService.ts` and follows the same FMG-vs-standalone routing as reservation push.

**Verbose debug logging (`verboseLogging` toggle):** Every integration type (FortiManager, FortiGate, Entra ID, Active Directory, Windows Server) exposes a `verboseLogging: boolean` flag (default `false`) in `config`, edited from the **Debug** section at the bottom of the integration's edit modal General tab. When on, four things light up — all routed to pino at `info` level with a `verbose: true` payload tag, visible via `journalctl -u polaris -f -o cat`:
1. **Per-step discovery logs:** the `onProgress` callback in [src/api/routes/integrations.ts](src/api/routes/integrations.ts) emits one info-level line per step (`discover.dhcp`, `discover.leases`, `discover.fortiswitches`, etc.) alongside the existing Event row.
2. **Per-phase sync logs:** `syncDhcpSubnets`'s 19 phase markers (1, 2, 2a, 2b, 2c, 3, 3b, 3c, 4, 5, 6, 7, 7b, 7.5, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 13.5) each emit a `discovery.phase.complete` line with elapsed ms when the next phase starts.
3. **Per-job worker logs:** every monitor job published for an asset owned by this integration gets `verboseDebug: true` stamped on its pg-boss payload; the worker handler emits `monitor.worker.pickup` + `monitor.worker.finish` lines with the worker slot id (`probe-W07`, `floating-F03`, etc.), `jobId`, `cadence`, `assetId`, outcome, and elapsedMs.
4. **Per-phase monitor handler logs:** the `verboseDebug` flag is forwarded into `WorkItemLabels.verbose`, which `runSystemInfoFor` reads to wrap the handler body in an `AsyncLocalStorage<MonitorPhaseContext>` (see `monitorPhaseStorage` + `startPhase` near `withSnmpGate` in `monitoringService.ts`). Every collector inside the chain — `collectSystemInfo`, `collectSystemInfoSnmp` (storage / interfaces / lldp / wireless walks), `collectSystemInfoFortinet` (cmdb / monitor-interface / ipsec / lldp REST calls), `recordSystemInfoResult` (assoc-IPs txn, lldp persist, wireless persist, update_asset), `persistLldpNeighbors` (lldp_match_index, find_existing, lldp_upsert, deleteMany — the legacy `createMany` + per-row `update_txn` pair collapsed to one bulk `INSERT … ON CONFLICT (id) DO UPDATE` raw-SQL statement so the connection isn't held across N pipelined updates inside a transaction; `persistWirelessStations` follows the same shape) — emits one `monitor.phase` line with `phase`, `assetId`, `cadence`, `elapsedMs`, and contextual extras when the span closes. No-op closure when verbose is off, so zero per-tick allocation in the steady state. **Two `withSnmpGate` + `buildLldpAssetMatchIndex` logs ARE always-on** regardless of the flag: a WARN line when an SNMP collector waits >5s in the per-host FIFO behind another collector, and an INFO start/complete pair around the LLDP match index rebuild (`lldp_match_index.rebuild_*`) — both bounded in volume by their own gating (gate wait threshold + 60s cache TTL respectively), and both diagnose the leading "systemInfo handler wedges at 300s" hypotheses without needing any toggle flipped.

**Auto-disable after 30 minutes.** When `verboseLogging` is enabled the backend stamps `verboseLoggingEnabledAt` (ISO timestamp) in the integration's `config` JSON. Every subsequent read of the flag (`isVerboseLoggingActive()` in `integrations.ts`) enforces a 30-minute TTL — expired flags are treated as `false` without a DB write. The `expireVerboseLogging()` function (called every 30 s from `discoverySlowCheck`) writes `verboseLogging: false` and clears `verboseLoggingEnabledAt` back to the DB when the window is up, so the checkbox in the edit modal reflects the true state on next open. Legacy rows created before this feature (no timestamp) are treated as active and self-correct the next time the operator toggles the flag. The UI checkbox label shows an amber countdown ("— auto-disables in N min") while the window is open.

No restart needed — the next discovery cycle and next monitor tick pick up the flag. Default off everywhere so existing installs see no extra log volume. Operator workflow: flip the checkbox in the integration's Debug section → `journalctl -u polaris -f -o cat | grep <integration-name>` (or `| jq 'select(.verbose==true)'`) → flag clears itself after 30 minutes.

---

## FMG Discovery Workflow

> See `docs/fmg-discovery.md` for the at-a-glance decision tree (transport mode, roster filtering, per-class stamping, push toggles) — that doc is the operator-facing reference; this section is the phase-by-phase narrative and data shapes.

### Asset projection priority table

For each discovery-owned Asset field, the table below lists the ordered set of `AssetSource.sourceKind` rows that `projectAssetFromSources()` consults. First non-empty observed value wins. Inferred sources are skipped. Source kinds not listed for a field = the projection deliberately doesn't consult them. The actual rules live in `src/utils/assetProjection.ts` — keep this table in sync when editing them.

| Asset field         | Priority (high → low)                                                                                                                                                                        |
|---------------------|----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------|
| `hostname`          | `polaris-agent` (`hostname` — `os.Hostname()` on the host itself) → `ad` (`dnsHostName` containing a dot — FQDN only) → `intune` (`deviceName`) → `entra` (`displayName`) → `ad` (`dnsHostName` short or `cn` fallback) → `fortigate-firewall` (`hostname`) → `fortiswitch` (`switchId`) → `fortiap` (`name`) → `fortigate-endpoint` (DHCP client id) |
| `serialNumber`      | `polaris-agent` (DMI `product_serial` / IOPlatformSerialNumber / Windows registry) → `intune` → `fortigate-firewall` → `fortiswitch` → `fortiap`                                              |
| `manufacturer`      | `polaris-agent` (DMI `sys_vendor` / ioreg manufacturer / BIOS registry, alias-normalized) → `intune` (alias-normalized) → `fortigate-firewall` / `fortiswitch` / `fortiap` (always literal `"Fortinet"`) → `fortigate-endpoint` (`hardwareVendor`, alias-normalized) |
| `model`             | `polaris-agent` (DMI `product_name` / ioreg model / BIOS registry) → `intune` → `fortigate-firewall` → `fortiap` → `fortigate-endpoint`                                                       |
| `os`                | `polaris-agent` (os-release `PRETTY_NAME` / sw_vers / Windows edition string) → `ad` → `intune` → `entra` → `fortigate-endpoint`                                                              |
| `osVersion`         | `polaris-agent` (os-release `VERSION_ID` / sw_vers `ProductVersion` / Windows build) → `intune` → `entra` → `ad` → `fortigate-firewall` → `fortiswitch` → `fortiap` → `fortigate-endpoint`     |
| `learnedLocation`   | `ad` (`ouPath`) → `fortigate-endpoint` (FortiGate device name as site label) → `fortiswitch` (`controllerFortigate`) → `fortiap` (`controllerFortigate`)                                       |
| `ipAddress`         | `fortigate-endpoint` (live DHCP/ARP) → `fortigate-firewall` (`mgmtIp`) → `fortiswitch` (`mgmtIp`) → `fortiap` (`mgmtIp`)                                                                       |
| `latitude` / `longitude` | `fortigate-firewall` — three tiers walked in order on the same source: (1) `snmpGeocodedLatitude` / `snmpGeocodedLongitude` (set when the integration's `pullSnmpLocation` is on AND a sysLocation pulled+geocoded successfully) → (2) `metavarLatitude` / `metavarLongitude` (FMG per-device metavars `Latitude` / `Longitude`; FMG-only, undefined on standalone) → (3) `latitude` / `longitude` (CMDB `gui-device-*` mirrored by FMG / fetched direct by standalone). Each picker validates the (lat,lng) pair as a whole via `isValidGeoCoord` so a half-valid tier falls through. |
| `snmpLocation`      | `fortigate-firewall` (`snmpLocation`) — FortiGate-only. Surfaced on the asset details General tab independent of whether geocoding produced usable coords.                                    |

**Fields the projection does NOT own** (and why):
- `macAddress` / `macAddresses[]` — DHCP discovery + Intune writes feed these directly to Asset; merge happens inline in the discovery sync.
- `status` / `quarantine*` — multi-actor (discovery / quarantine code / decommission job / manual operator) — too many writers for projection to arbitrate.
- `assetType` — usually inferred at create time from OS string and stable thereafter; manual recategorization is sticky.
- `location`, `department`, `assignedTo`, `notes`, `tags`, `monitor*`, `dnsName` — operator-owned or system-owned, not from discovery sources.

**FMG/FortiGate projection apply pass (slice 4f cutover):** The five FMG/FortiGate discovery pathways (DHCP-reservation / DHCP-lease / device-inventory / MAC-table / ARP) each still write Asset fields opportunistically inline (set-when-empty for OS / manufacturer / learnedLocation, set-always for osVersion, etc.). After Phase 10 stamps the `fortigate-endpoint` source rows, **Phase 11 of `syncDhcpSubnets` re-projects every touched asset** and writes back any field where projection priority disagrees with the inline-written value. Fixes the long-standing bug where Intune's verbose `osVersion = "10.0.19045"` gets overwritten by FortiOS's coarser `osVersion = "10.0"` on every device-inventory pass, plus mirror cases where AD's `"Windows 10 Pro"` should beat Intune's `"Windows"`. Only writes fields where the projected value is non-null AND differs from current Asset; quiet syncs skip the round-trip. `ipSource` is set to `<integrationType>:fortigate-endpoint` whenever the projected ipAddress wins, mirroring the existing inline ipSource convention. **Inline-only fields** (not subject to Phase 11): `lastSeenSwitch`, `lastSeenAp` (no source kind besides fortigate-endpoint observes them; "stamp inline, never project" is correct), `macAddresses[]` (multi-source merge happens inline at write time), `status` / `quarantine*` / `assetType` / operator-owned fields.

> **Multi-source asset model (Phase 2 cutover, infrastructure scope) and projection layer (Phase 3b.1 cutover):** Every discovered Fortinet infrastructure asset is on the new `AssetSource` model — firewalls (`sourceKind="fortigate-firewall"`), managed FortiSwitches (`sourceKind="fortiswitch"`), and managed FortiAPs (`sourceKind="fortiap"`) each get an explicit row keyed on `externalId = serialNumber` with a rich source-shaped `observed` blob. Discovery-owned Asset fields (hostname, model, osVersion, manufacturer, serialNumber, ipAddress, latitude, longitude, learnedLocation for switches/APs) come from `projectAssetFromSources()` instead of inline merge logic — every update path upserts the source first, fetches all sources, computes projection, then writes a single Asset.update; every create path projects from a synthetic single-source array (no DB roundtrip) and uses the result in the Asset.create payload. Firewall `learnedLocation` deliberately stays inline (set to the firewall's own hostname / site label) since the projection layer leaves it null for firewall sources by design. Firewall: serial, hostname, model, osVersion, mgmtIp, lat/long, managedBy: "fortimanager"|"fortigate". FortiSwitch: serial, switchId, model, osVersion, mgmtIp, **baseMac** (management MAC of the FortiLink-peer interface, cross-joined at discovery time from the `/api/v2/monitor/switch-controller/detected-device` rows where `is_fortilink_peer===true` and `switch_id===<this switch's switch-id>`; null when the join misses), controllerFortigate, uplinkInterface, state, connected, joinTime. FortiAP: serial, name, model, osVersion, mgmtIp, baseMac, status, controllerFortigate, parentSwitch, parentPort, parentVlan, uplinkInterface (AP-local port from `wan_status.interface` or LLDP `local_port` — `lan*` physical / `wbh*` wireless bridge), peerSource, meshUplink, parentApSerial, plus the live telemetry snapshot the discovery query already paid for: cpuPct, memFreeMb, memTotalMb, sensorTemperatures. Re-discovery keys on `Asset.serialNumber` via the in-memory `findBySerial` index — the source row exists for the per-source detail view rather than for primary lookup, so the discovery hot path is unchanged. **Endpoint scope (post-Phase-4d):** every endpoint asset the FMG/FortiGate sync touches gets a unified `sourceKind="fortigate-endpoint"` source row (externalId = MAC, normalized colon-uppercase) covering all five discovery pathways — DHCP reservations, DHCP leases, device-inventory (FortiOS `device/list` with hardware/OS/user fingerprinting), switch-port MAC table, ARP enrichment. Whichever pathway found the device contributes its observed fields (hostname, ipAddress, ipSource, os, osVersion, hardwareVendor, model, learnedLocation, lastSeenSwitch, lastSeenAp, discoveredVia); pathways that didn't run leave their fields null. Stamping happens at end-of-sync via a touched-asset-id Set populated as each pathway updates an asset; the upsert helper also sweeps any "manual" Phase-1 placeholder source on the same asset. Backfill startup job `backfillFortigateEndpointSources` covers existing endpoint rows that predated the cutover.

### FMG worker (per-integration, two lanes)

Every code path that talks to FortiManager funnels through a per-integration `FmgWorker` (`src/services/fmgWorker.ts`), which exposes two lanes. The FMG concurrency constraint — "FMG drops parallel API calls past 1-2 for the same API user" — is specifically about `/sys/proxy/json` passthrough calls, not native FMG endpoints. Native endpoints (`/pm/config/...`, `/dvmdb/...`, auth) hit FMG's own database and don't share that constraint, so serializing them was leaving discovery throughput on the table.

- **Proxy lane** — strict single-consumer FIFO (concurrency=1). Honors the hard FMG limit. Used for every `/sys/proxy/json` call: per-device monitor scrapes in proxy mode (DHCP leases, device inventory, managed switches/APs, detected-device MAC table, ARP table), proxy-mode reservation push, proxy-mode quarantine push, and the operator-facing manual `/sys/proxy/json` proxy endpoint.
- **Native lane** — unbounded. Just tracks inflight count for observability. Used for every native FMG call: device roster (`/dvmdb/...`), management-IP resolution (`/pm/config/device/<n>/global/system/interface`), DHCP server CMDB, interface config CMDB, switch/AP CMDB rosters, firewall VIPs, geo coords, auth, testConnection.

Dispatch happens inside `rpc()` (fortimanagerService) by inspecting the JSON-RPC payload's first param URL — callers don't pick the lane manually. Cross-feature serialization still holds on the proxy lane: an operator clicking "Reserve IP" mid-discovery still has the reservation-push proxy call wait behind in-flight discovery proxy calls. Native parallelism lets discoveryParallelism actually deliver on its promise: in direct mode, mgmt-IP resolution for cache-cold devices is now parallel (was the dominant serial bottleneck on fresh installs); in proxy mode, the ~7 native CMDB scrapes per device overlap with each other and with the proxy lane's serial monitor scrapes.

Exposed metrics: `polaris_fmg_worker_queue_depth{integrationId}` (proxy lane queued tasks), `polaris_fmg_worker_inflight{integrationId}` (proxy lane 0/1), `polaris_fmg_worker_native_inflight{integrationId}` (native lane current concurrent count).

### Direct-mode discovery flow (warm-cache producer + FMG-verify producer)

Direct-mode (`useProxy=false`) `fortimanager.discoverDhcpSubnets` runs **two concurrent producers** feeding one `discoveryParallelism`-wide FortiGate work pool:

- **Warm-cache producer** — queries `Asset` rows for this integration where `assetType="firewall" AND monitorStatus="up" AND ipAddress IS NOT NULL` and dispatches each one immediately using its cached `ipAddress` (no FMG round-trip). The query is built in `buildFmgWarmCacheIps()` in `integrations.ts` and passed in via the new `warmCacheIps?: Map<string,string>` parameter on `discoverDhcpSubnets`. On big fleets this fills the worker pool to its `discoveryParallelism` cap from `t=0` instead of dripping in behind the serial FMG resolver.
- **FMG-verify producer** — for FortiGates NOT in the warm cache (cache-cold, monitor-down, or new in the FMG roster), serially resolves each mgmt IP through the FMG worker and dispatches as each IP lands. Same shape as the legacy resolver path; the difference is that the verify list is now a strict subset of `devicesData` instead of the whole set.

**Cache-miss fallback** lives inside `processDevice`: if the first direct REST call fails AND the IP came from the warm cache (`cachedNames` set), it submits one `resolveDeviceMgmtIp` to the FMG worker, retries at the freshly-resolved address, and updates `mgmtIpByDevice` + the firewall Asset's IP via the standard sync path. If the re-resolve returns the same IP (or no IP), the FortiGate is treated as unreachable and the device is skipped. Self-healing without a second discovery cycle.

Proxy mode (`useProxy=true`) ignores the warm cache — every per-device monitor call funnels through `/sys/proxy/json` which is FMG-bound, so a cached mgmt IP offers no speedup. The FMG worker's proxy lane serializes those proxy calls along with any concurrent reservation push / quarantine push against the same integration. The native lane runs CMDB scrapes (DHCP server config, interface CMDB, VIPs, geo coords, etc.) concurrently both across devices and with the proxy lane, so per-device discovery is genuinely faster in proxy mode than the previous single-lane architecture even though the proxy calls themselves are still serial.

`fortimanagerService.ts` connects to FortiManager via JSON-RPC and discovers:

- **DHCP scopes** → Subnet records (`discoveredBy`, `fortigateDevice`)
- **DHCP reservations** → Reservations (`sourceType: dhcp_reservation`). Discovery walks two sources: the CMDB tree at `/pm/config/device/<name>/vdom/root/system/dhcp/server` (every configured static reservation, regardless of client state) and the live monitor at `/api/v2/monitor/system/dhcp` (currently-active leases plus reservations whose target is online). The two are **merged** — CMDB is the base set, monitor adds anything not already covered, and CMDB wins on overlap by IP. This is intentional: monitor only returns reservations whose target is currently leasing, so trusting only monitor would silently drop static reservations whose target device happens to be offline at discovery time. **The FortiGate is authoritative for CMDB DHCP reservations:** Phase 5b at the end of every per-device sync releases any active `dhcp_reservation` row whose subnet's `fortigateDevice` is in `DiscoveryResult.dhcpReservationsInventoriedDevices` (the CMDB DHCP query succeeded this cycle) but whose `(subnetId, ip)` isn't in `result.dhcpEntries`. Covers both fresh-from-CMDB rows AND Polaris-pushed manual reservations that flipped to `dhcp_reservation` on first sight — operator-deleted-on-gate is the same observable state in both cases. Push pointers (`pushedToId`/`pushedScopeId`/`pushedEntryId`) are cleared on release so a future re-reservation makes its own push. Writes a `reservation.dhcp_reservation.released` Event. `dhcp_lease`, `fortiswitch`, and `fortinap` source types are intentionally NOT swept: leases age out via `expireReservations` + the stale-reservation alert system, and fortiswitch/fortinap rows are device-presence indicators whose lifecycle is owned by Phase 2b decommission.
- **DHCP leases** → Reservations (`sourceType: dhcp_lease`); captures `expire_time`, `access_point`, `ssid`
- **Interface IPs** → Reservations (`sourceType: interface_ip`) — note: discovery no longer mirrors these into `Asset.associatedIps`. The System tab's interface scrape (run on the monitoring cadence once monitoring is enabled on the firewall asset) is the single source for `Asset.associatedIps` per-interface entries. **Secondary IPs are included** alongside primaries: the FortiOS `system.interface` `secondary-ip` child table is iterated in both transport paths (FMG adds `"secondary-ip"` to the CMDB `fields` list; standalone reads the full interface object) and each row is pushed as a `DiscoveredInterfaceIp` with `role: "secondary"`. Phase 4's notes builder labels them as **Secondary IP** on the resulting reservation so operators can tell at a glance.
- **Virtual IPs (VIPs)** → Reservations (`sourceType: vip`). The FortiGate is authoritative for its own VIPs: **Phase 5b** at the end of every per-device sync releases any active `vip` reservation whose `vipInfo.device` is in `DiscoveryResult.vipInventoriedDevices` (the gate's VIP query succeeded this cycle) but whose IP isn't in the gate's current VIP list. **VIP succession**: when a DHCP lease/reservation lands at an IP whose VIP has just disappeared, Phase 5 converts the existing row in-place — `sourceType` flips to `dhcp_*`, `vipInfo` clears, MAC fills in if empty, but operator-edited `hostname` / `owner` / `notes` survive (only the canonical `"fortimanager-vip"` owner and the `"Firewall VIP ..."` notes are replaced). Pending VIP-merge conflicts on the row are auto-rejected. Writes `reservation.vip.replaced` (Phase 5 in-place) or `reservation.vip.released` (Phase 5b sweep) Events.
- **FortiSwitch devices** → Asset records (`assetType: switch`); via FMG proxy to `/api/v2/monitor/switch-controller/managed-switch/status`. `fortinetTopology` stamped with `{ role: "fortiswitch", controllerFortigate, uplinkInterface }` so the Device Map renders the FortiLink uplink as an edge.
- **FortiAP devices** → Asset records (`assetType: access_point`); via FMG proxy to `/api/v2/monitor/wifi/managed_ap`. `fortinetTopology` stamped with `{ role: "fortiap", controllerFortigate, parentSwitch, parentPort, parentVlan, uplinkInterface, peerSource, meshUplink, parentApSerial }`. The row's parse is centralized in `src/utils/fortiapMonitorRow.ts` (`parseFortiapMonitorRow`) so the FMG proxy path and the standalone FortiGate REST path can't drift on field handling. IP picker order (first non-empty wins, `0.0.0.0` normalized to empty): `ip_addr → ip_address → local_ipv4_address → local_ipv4_addr → wtp_ip → connecting_ip`. MAC picker: `base_mac → board_mac → mac`. Model derives from `model → wtp_profile → deriveFortiapModelFromSerial(serial)` — operators have reported APs whose live model field comes back blank from /managed_ap even on healthy units, so the serial-prefix fallback (`FP234F` → `FortiAP-234F`) closes the gap. `uplinkInterface` is the AP-local port (lan1/lan2/wbh0…) extracted from `wan_status[].interface` first, falling back to the matching LLDP entry's `local_port`. The same parse also stages the live telemetry snapshot (`cpuPct`, `memFreeMb`, `memTotalMb`, `sensorTemperatures`) into the AssetSource observed blob so the Sources tab shows fresh values immediately after a discovery cycle. **Switch-port attribution is LLDP-first**: the managed_ap response carries an `lldp[]` array per AP (FortiOS surfaces what the AP itself sees on its lan1 interface); the shared extractor in `src/utils/fortiapLldp.ts` picks the first row whose `system_description` starts with `"FortiSwitch-"`, taking `system_name` as parentSwitch and `port_id` as parentPort (NOT `port_description` — that's operator-set free text). LLDP is authoritative because the AP itself reports its uplink, and works even when FortiOS filters managed-AP MACs out of `detected-device` (which it does on many releases — the symptom is the legacy `Resolved 0/N` log line). `peerSource` records `"lldp"` when this path resolved the link. **The `detected-device` MAC table fallback** still runs but only for APs where LLDP gave nothing (gated on `peerSource !== "lldp"`); when it resolves, `peerSource` is stamped `"detected-device"`. APs that resolve via neither path render hanging off the FortiGate directly. **Mesh topology**: the same managed_ap response carries `mesh_uplink: "ethernet"|"mesh"` and `parent_wtp_id` (= the parent FortiAP's serial) when this AP is a wireless-mesh leaf — both are surfaced on `fortinetTopology` so the topology graph can render the mesh edge to the parent AP rather than just the wired uplink.
- **FortiGate geo coordinates** → `Asset.latitude` / `Asset.longitude` on the firewall Asset, resolved through three tiers in priority order (highest first): **(1) SNMP-geocoded sysLocation** — when `Integration.config.fortigateMonitor.pullSnmpLocation` is enabled, Phase 3 pulls `sysLocation` from each FortiGate via REST (`fetchFortigateSysLocation()` → `GET /api/v2/cmdb/system.snmp/sysinfo`; reuses the existing FMG-proxy / standalone-FortiGate transport so it works regardless of network reachability to the FortiGate's management IP) and runs the value through `geocoderService.geocode()` (Nominatim with 90-day positive+negative cache + 1 req/sec rate limit). One pull per FortiGate per discovery cycle — HA cluster members share the same sysLocation by physical co-location, so the result is stamped on every member's observed blob. **(2) FMG metavars** — `Latitude` / `Longitude` per-device metavars read from `/dvmdb/adom/<adom>/device` using `option: ["get meta"]` and parsed via `extractMetavarCoordsFromFmgDevice()`. Operator-managed convention. Standalone FortiGate has no metavars; this tier is FMG-only. **(3) CMDB GUI coords** — `gui-device-latitude` / `gui-device-longitude` from `/pm/config/device/<name>/global/system/global` (FMG) or `/api/v2/cmdb/system/global` (standalone). Pre-feature behavior preserved as the lowest-priority tier. Each tier validates the (lat,lng) pair as a whole via `isValidGeoCoord` (rejects null/NaN/(0,0)/out-of-range); a half-valid tier falls through instead of mixing values. **Coord write-back** — when `fortigateMonitor.pushGeocodedCoords` is also on AND geocoding succeeded AND the FortiGate's current CMDB coords don't match the geocoded pair within ~1e-5° (≈1.1 m via `coordsClose()`), Phase 3 calls `pushCoordsToFortigate()` (`src/services/fortigateCoordPushService.ts`). FMG mode writes to BOTH per-device metavars (`Latitude` / `Longitude`) AND CMDB (`gui-device-*`); standalone writes only CMDB. FMG-mode CMDB writes land in FMG's CMDB but require an operator Install Device Configuration in FMG to reach the live FortiGate — Polaris does not trigger installs. Best-effort: per-target failures are logged via `integration.coords.push_failed` Events but never unwind the Asset writes that already landed. Both toggles default off; existing installs see no behavior change.
- **SNMP sysLocation (raw)** → `Asset.snmpLocation` + `Asset.snmpLocationFetchedAt`. Captured independently of whether geocoding produced usable coords so operators see what the FortiGate is reporting via SNMP even when Nominatim can't resolve it (gibberish strings, internal site codes, etc.). FortiGate-firewall assets only. Surfaced on the asset details General tab. Does **not** overwrite `Asset.learnedLocation` — firewalls keep their hostname as the location label so the assets list Location column stays meaningful at-a-glance (operators identify a firewall by name, not street address). `Asset.location` (the operator-overridable field) is never touched by discovery.
- **HA cluster awareness** → `Asset.fortinetTopology.{haMode, haRole, haPeerSerial}` on each firewall member. FMG already exposes `ha_mode` + `ha_slave[]` on every device record returned by `/dvmdb/adom/<adom>/device` — the helper `extractHaFromFmgDevice()` in [fortimanagerService.ts](src/services/fortimanagerService.ts) reads them at zero extra call cost, normalizing both string (`"a-p"`/`"a-a"`/`"standalone"`) and integer (`1`/`2`/`0`) `ha_mode` encodings. The current primary is identified by matching `ha_slave[].sn` against the top-level `device.sn` (FMG keeps these in sync across failover; `idx === 0` is the fallback when the sn match fails). The standalone FortiGate integration covers the same ground via a new chain (Chain G) in `fortigateService.discoverDhcpSubnets` that calls `GET /api/v2/monitor/system/ha-peer` (404 / empty → standalone). Discovery never keys per-member Asset lookups on `device.sn`/`device.hostname` (both flip on failover) — instead Phase 3 of `syncDhcpSubnets` iterates `device.haMembers[]` and looks up each Asset by the **member's own serial**, which is stable across failover. The standby member gets `ipAddress=null` (cluster IP only routes to active), inherits the cluster's `model`/`osVersion`/`latitude`/`longitude` (HA members run identical hardware/firmware at the same site), and defaults to `monitored=false` on first create. Standby Assets are tagged `ha-standby` and have notes "(HA standby member)" for auditability. The Phase 2a decommission sweep is HA-aware: any Asset whose `serialNumber` appears in `haMembers[].serial` (across every discovered device this cycle) is treated as "still configured" even when its hostname is absent from the top-level roster — prevents standby flapping AND prevents the previously-primary asset from being decommissioned after a failover.
- **FortiSwitch / FortiAP MACs** → Updates Asset `lastSeenSwitch` / `lastSeenAp`
- **CMDB roster (configured managed switches + APs per FortiGate)** → captured into `DiscoveryResult.cmdbSwitchSerials` / `cmdbApSerials` via native FMG CMDB reads at `/pm/config/device/<n>/global/switch-controller/managed-switch` and `/pm/config/device/<n>/vdom/root/wireless-controller/wtp` (no `/sys/proxy/json` wrapper — bypasses proxy-mode concurrency=1). Defensive: a switch/AP that's authorized at FMG but currently offline / in a brief post-config-push window may be missing from the live status query; the decommission sweep (`Phase 2b — Decommission stale FortiSwitches/FortiAPs`) treats serials surfaced via CMDB as "still known" so they aren't declared stale. Standalone FortiGate path leaves these arrays empty — its live `managed-switch/status` query already returns disconnected switches with status="Disconnected", so the CMDB cross-check is redundant.
- **Full FortiSwitch MAC table** → captured into `DiscoveryResult.switchMacTable` (one row per `(switchId, portName, mac)` pair). Format-string for `/api/v2/monitor/switch-controller/detected-device` widened to include `ipv4_address|ipv6_address|device_name|host_src|device_type|os_name|is_fortilink_peer`. Sync Phase 7.5 walks this table and stamps `Asset.lastSeenSwitch = "<switchId>/<portName>"` for every endpoint asset whose MAC is on a FortiSwitch port (skipping infrastructure assets and `is_fortilink_peer` rows). The legacy AP→switch attribution still consumes the same data internally for its detected-device fallback path.
- **FortiGate ARP table** → captured into `DiscoveryResult.arpTable` via `/api/v2/monitor/network/arp` per FortiGate. Authoritative IP↔MAC binding for any subnet the FortiGate routes. Sync Phase 7.5 fills `Asset.ipAddress` from ARP **only when the asset's IP is currently empty** — conservative rule that avoids IP-recycling churn (a different device taking over the same DHCP lease later); aggressive overwrite would need a freshness-aware policy.

### FMG proxy field filtering

FortiOS monitor endpoints support field selection via the `format` query parameter (pipe-separated):

```
/api/v2/monitor/switch-controller/managed-switch/status?format=connecting_from|fgt_peer_intf_name|join_time|os_version|serial|switch-id|state|status
```

**Do not use `?fields=`** — that is the CMDB filter syntax and does not work on monitor endpoints.

### FortiSwitch fields (managed-switch/status)

| API field | Meaning | Maps to |
|-----------|---------|---------|
| `switch-id` | Switch hostname | `hostname` |
| `serial` | Serial number | `serialNumber` |
| `connecting_from` | Management IP of the switch | `ipAddress` |
| `fgt_peer_intf_name` | FortiGate interface/FortiLink the switch is on | `learnedLocation` |
| `os_version` | Firmware version | `osVersion` |
| `join_time` | Unix timestamp when switch was first authorized | `acquiredAt` (only update if older) |
| `state` | `Authorized` / `Unauthorized` | `status: storage` if Unauthorized |
| `status` | `Connected` / `Disconnected` | informational |

When a discovered value conflicts with an existing manual reservation, a `Conflict` record is created instead of silently overwriting. Admins accept (apply discovered values) or reject (keep existing) via the conflict slide-over panel on the Events page.

### Stale-subnet deprecation (Phase 2)

After all per-device polling finishes, `syncDhcpSubnets` deprecates subnets whose `fortigateDevice` is no longer in `DiscoveryResult.knownDeviceNames` — the full roster of FortiGates configured in FortiManager, captured up front from `/dvmdb/adom/<adom>/device` with **no `conn_status` filter**. An offline FortiGate stays in `knownDeviceNames` and its subnets are left alone; only devices that have been *removed* from FMG are treated as stale. Devices filtered out by `deviceInclude`/`deviceExclude` also remain in the roster for the same reason — changing a filter shouldn't nuke previously-discovered subnets. Phase 2 is skipped entirely if the run was aborted.

### Stale-firewall decommission (Phase 2a)

Right after Phase 2, `syncDhcpSubnets` flips firewall Asset rows discovered by this integration whose `hostname` is no longer in `knownDeviceNames` to `status="decommissioned"` and writes one `asset.fortigate.decommissioned` Event each. Same `knownDeviceNames` semantics as Phase 2 — pre-filter, any conn_status — so an offline-but-still-configured FortiGate is left alone, and changing a deviceInclude/exclude filter doesn't churn firewall asset state. A decommissioned firewall is reactivated by the existing Phase-3b firewall update path on a future cycle when the device returns to the FMG roster. Skipped on aborted runs along with Phase 2.

### Auto-Monitor Interfaces apply pass (Phase 2c)

After Phase 2 + 2b finish, `syncDhcpSubnets` runs `applyAutoMonitorForClass` from `autoMonitorInterfacesService.ts` for each per-class block (`fortigate` / `fortiswitch` / `fortiap`) whose `Integration.config.<klass>Monitor.autoMonitorInterfaces` is non-null. Resolves the selection (names / wildcard / type) against each discovered asset's latest `AssetInterfaceSample` rows and unions the result into `Asset.monitoredInterfaces` — strictly additive, never strips operator pins. Idempotent: skips the per-asset write when nothing would change. Runs on `mode in {"full", "finalize"}`, so both standalone-FortiGate runs and FMG finalize hit it; per-device FMG syncs (`mode: "skip-deprecation"`) do not. Writes one `integration.auto_monitor_interfaces.applied` Event per class when something actually changed; silent otherwise. First-run edge case: when `onlyUp` is set but no system-info pass has run yet, the resolver finds no candidates and the apply pass quietly no-ops; the next discovery cycle catches up. The same logic is exposed via `POST /integrations/:id/interface-aggregate/apply` and is invoked by the integration edit modal's **Save Changes** button so saving immediately applies the selection to existing assets.

### Dependency tree recompute (Phase 12)

After every infra-asset write phase has settled (Phase 3 / 3b firewall + switch + AP creates and updates) AND the projection apply (Phase 11) has reconciled multi-source fields, `syncDhcpSubnets` calls `recomputeDependencyTree(integrationId)` from `dependencyTreeService.ts`. The recompute walks `Asset.fortinetTopology.controllerFortigate` (FortiGate→switch) + `parentSwitch` (switch→AP) + `inferInterfaceTopology` (switch→switch chains, MCLAG aggregates) + `AssetLldpNeighbor.matchedAssetId` (cross-vendor / edge cases) into a parent→child DAG, prunes via **physical-first** BFS layer assignment from each FortiGate (layer 1) outward — interface + LLDP edges form the primary adjacency, controller edges are a fallback for assets the physical pass didn't reach, with simple-path detection so a 3+ switch daisy chain that's only LLDP-visible between siblings (not back to the FortiGate) still chains correctly instead of collapsing every switch into a same-layer sibling of the FortiGate. MCLAG pairs and any branching / cycled controller-only component still attach as siblings. Kept edges prefer the most-physical `detectedVia` (interface > lldp > controller) for the audit-trail row, and replaces this integration's `source="computed"` rows in `AssetDependencyParent` plus updates `Asset.dependencyLayer` for in-scope assets — all in one transaction. `source="override"` rows (operator pins via the admin override endpoints) are never touched. Multi-parent rows model MCLAG / dual-homed switches; "all-down" semantics in the reconciler then mean a redundant uplink keeps an asset polling normally. Runs on `mode in {"full", "finalize"}` only — per-device skip-deprecation passes skip the recompute since they see only a partial slice of the fleet. Best-effort: failures `syncLog("error", ...)` and don't block the sync return. Pairs with the `dependencyReconciler` 60s job which evaluates the suppression flag from this DAG.

### Firewall tag reconcile (Phase 13.5)

Right after the Phase 13 map-region pass, `syncDhcpSubnets` calls `reconcileFirewallTagsForIntegration(integrationId)` from `firewallTagService.ts`. The reconciler rebuilds each in-scope asset's `firewall:<hostname>` breadcrumb tag set from `Asset.fortinetTopology.controllerFortigate` (managed switches / APs) plus `AssetFortigateSighting` rows within `quarantine.sightingMaxAgeDays` (DHCP-discovered endpoints) and writes only the per-asset diff. The strip allowlist is scoped to this integration's currently-known firewall hostnames so tags pointing at FortiGates owned by other integrations or operator-typed `firewall:fake` survive untouched. FortiGate firewall assets themselves are never tagged. Gated to `mode in {"full", "finalize"}`. Best-effort: failures `syncLog("error", ...)` and don't block the sync return. Pairs with three inline lifecycle hooks in this same sync: Phase 2a calls `applyFirewallDecommission(hostname)` per stale firewall (strip + registry-row removal); Phase 3 firewall create calls `seedFirewallTagRegistry(hostname)`; Phase 3 firewall update calls `applyFirewallRename(old, new)` when projection writes a different hostname. No periodic safety-net job — every input is written by discovery, so end-of-discovery is the natural reconciliation point.

Discovery can be triggered manually or runs automatically on each integration's `pollInterval` via `discoveryScheduler.ts`.

---

## FortiGate Discovery Workflow (Standalone)

`fortigateService.ts` talks directly to a single standalone FortiGate (one not managed by FortiManager) via the FortiOS REST API. It consumes the same `DiscoveryResult` shape as `fortimanagerService` — the sync pipeline in `integrations.ts` handles both identically.

Scope is the same as FMG (DHCP scopes + reservations + leases, interface IPs, VIPs, managed FortiSwitches, managed FortiAPs, device inventory). Key differences from the FMG path:

- **Endpoint style** — requests go straight to `/api/v2/cmdb/...` and `/api/v2/monitor/...` on the FortiGate, no JSON-RPC wrapper
- **Scoping** — `vdom` query param (default `root`) instead of FMG `adom`
- **Device identity** — the FortiGate itself is the single entry in `result.devices`; its hostname is resolved from `/api/v2/monitor/system/status`
- **Auth** — Bearer API token from System > Administrators > REST API Admin (optional `access_user` header for parity with FMG; FortiOS ignores it)
- **HA discovery** — per-FortiGate query fan-out is now **seven** parallel chains, not six. Chain G calls `GET /api/v2/monitor/system/ha-peer` to enumerate cluster members; the response's `serial_no` is the calling unit (current primary, since the cluster IP routes only to active), and each peer in `results[]` is added to `haMembers[]` with `isPrimary: false`. 404 / empty results are normalized to `haMode: "standalone"` so the downstream sync has a single branch. Phase 3 of `syncDhcpSubnets` consumes `haMembers[]` identically to the FMG path — one Asset per physical serial, role/IP swaps on failover with no conflict rows. The standby member's `model` / `osVersion` are inherited from the cluster's REST response (HA members run identical builds); the FMG path additionally pulls these from `ha_slave[].name` for member hostnames.

---

## Entra ID / Intune Discovery Workflow

`entraIdService.ts` queries Microsoft Graph via OAuth2 client-credentials flow to sync registered devices as assets. **Produces assets only** — no subnets, reservations, or VIPs — so it uses a dedicated `syncEntraDevices` path in `integrations.ts` rather than the shared `syncDhcpSubnets` pipeline.

- **Auth** — `POST https://login.microsoftonline.com/{tenantId}/oauth2/v2.0/token` with `grant_type=client_credentials`, scope `https://graph.microsoft.com/.default`. Tokens are cached in-memory by `tenantId:clientId` until expiry.
- **Endpoints** —
  - Always: `GET /v1.0/devices` (paged via `@odata.nextLink`, `$top=999`, hard cap 10,000). Requires `Device.Read.All` (application permission, admin consent).
  - When `enableIntune=true`: `GET /v1.0/deviceManagement/managedDevices`. Requires `DeviceManagementManagedDevices.Read.All`. Merged onto Entra devices via `azureADDeviceId ↔ deviceId`; Intune data wins on any shared field.
- **Device identity** — the Entra `deviceId` (GUID) is the stable key. Persisted as the `externalId` on per-source `AssetSource` rows (`sourceKind="entra"`/`"intune"`). Phase 4d retired the legacy `Asset.assetTag = entra:{deviceId}` mirror — re-discovery and search both query AssetSource directly. Existing assetTag values on rows discovered before Phase 4d are preserved (back-compat); new Entra-discovered assets have `assetTag = null`.
- **Multi-source asset model (Phase 2 cutover)** **and the projection layer** (Phase 3b.1 cutover) — the Entra/Intune sync is on the new `AssetSource` model. Each Graph endpoint that contributed to a discovered device produces its own row: `sourceKind="entra"` for the `/v1.0/devices` view (trustType, accountEnabled, SID, registration/sign-in timestamps, compliance flags, original entra `displayName`) and `sourceKind="intune"` for the `/v1.0/deviceManagement/managedDevices` view (serial, manufacturer, model, Ethernet+WiFi MACs, `userPrincipalName`, `chassisType`, `complianceState`, `lastSyncDateTime`, original intune `deviceName`). Both rows share the same `externalId = deviceId.toLowerCase()` but different `sourceKind`s. Hybrid devices have both rows; Entra-only and Intune-only devices have just one. **Discovery-owned Asset fields** (hostname, os, osVersion, learnedLocation, serialNumber, manufacturer, model) come from `projectAssetFromSources()` — every update path (primary existing-asset, SID-takeover, duplicate-Entra-registration "incoming wins" auto-resolve) upserts the entra/intune sources first, re-fetches all sources for the asset (which may include AD on hybrid devices so the projection's AD-FQDN-first hostname rule kicks in), computes projection, and writes a single Asset.update with projected + non-projected fields. The create path projects from a synthetic source array built directly from the new device's observed blobs (entra/intune as appropriate per `dev.sources`) — pure, no DB roundtrip needed for new assets. After upsert, `upsertEntraIntuneSources` **sweeps stale entra/intune rows on the same Asset whose externalId differs from this device's deviceId** — covers the duplicate-Entra-registration "incoming wins" auto-resolve path so the prior identity doesn't orphan-link a future discovery to the wrong asset. The `entraIdService` `DiscoveredEntraDevice` shape carries a `sources: ("entra"|"intune")[]` field plus original `entraDisplayName` / `intuneDeviceName` so the sync can split the merged record back into source-shaped blobs without lossy heuristics.
- **Re-discovery** — Assets are matched in this order: (1) any `AssetSource` row of `sourceKind in ("entra","intune")` with matching `externalId = deviceId.toLowerCase()`, (2) on-prem SID match — resolves through `entra.observed.onPremisesSecurityIdentifier` or `ad.observed.objectSid` (hybrid-joined devices the AD integration already created — Entra **takes over** in that case, signaled by the SID-matched asset having no entra/intune source yet), (3) hostname collision against an untagged asset → a `Conflict` (entityType `"asset"`, deduped on `proposedDeviceId`) is created for admin/assetsadmin review. "Untagged" under the new model means **the asset has no entra/intune source AND no AD source**. The slide-over panel renders a side-by-side comparison; **Accept** adopts the existing asset (writes the Entra assetTag + fills empty fields from the snapshot), **Reject** creates a separate asset with the Entra tag so future runs find it by tag.
- **Asset type** — inferred from Intune `chassisType` (`desktop/laptop/convertible/detachable` → `workstation`; `tablet/phone` → `other`); Entra-only devices default to `workstation`. Admins can recategorize via the asset edit UI; re-discovery only overwrites `assetType` if it is still `other`.
- **User** — Intune `userPrincipalName` → `Asset.assignedTo`. Entra-only runs do not populate this field.
- **MACs** — Intune supplies both `wiFiMacAddress` and `ethernetMacAddress` per managed device. Both are stored as separate entries in `Asset.macAddresses[]` with sources `"intune-wifi"` and `"intune-ethernet"`, and the asset's primary `macAddress` column is set to whichever was most recently updated (Ethernet preferred when both are reported on the same sync, since modern Windows / iOS / Android randomize the WiFi MAC per network and the Ethernet MAC is the more stable identity). The asset details panel renders both in the "All MACs" list with pretty source labels ("Intune — Ethernet" / "Intune — Wi-Fi"). The `mac-collision` conflict pathway only matches against the **Ethernet MAC** for the same randomization reason; WiFi MAC is informational and used by FortiGate DHCP-discovery's MAC-based asset matching to broaden the chance of an incoming DHCP lease landing on the right asset row.
- **Disabled devices** — `accountEnabled` is fetched for every Entra device. When `includeDisabled=true` (default), disabled devices are synced as `decommissioned` assets and get an `entra-disabled` tag. When `includeDisabled=false`, they are skipped entirely — matching the AD integration's `includeDisabled` behavior.
- **Filters** — `deviceInclude` / `deviceExclude` arrays match against `displayName` with wildcard support (`LAPTOP-*`, `*-lab`).

---

## Active Directory Discovery Workflow (On-premise)

`activeDirectoryService.ts` queries an on-premise domain controller via LDAP simple bind (over LDAP or LDAPS) and syncs computer objects as assets. **Produces assets only** — no subnets, reservations, or VIPs — so it uses a dedicated `syncActiveDirectoryDevices` path in `integrations.ts`.

- **Library** — `ldapts` (Promise-based LDAP client; TypeScript types bundled).
- **Auth** — simple bind using `bindDn` (full DN of a read-only domain user) and `bindPassword`. No Kerberos/GSSAPI. Default port 636 (LDAPS) or 389 (plain LDAP).
- **Query** — paged subtree search under `baseDn` with filter `(&(objectCategory=computer)(objectClass=computer))`, page size 1000, hard cap 10,000. Search scope `sub` (default) or `one`.
- **Device identity** — AD `objectGUID` (decoded as lowercase hex). Persisted on `Asset.assetTag` as `ad:{guid}` when the AD integration creates the asset.
- **Disabled accounts** — `userAccountControl & 0x2` (ACCOUNTDISABLE). When `includeDisabled=true` (default), these still sync but are created/updated with `status = decommissioned` and get an `ad-disabled` tag. When `includeDisabled=false`, they're skipped.
- **Attribute mapping** — `dNSHostName` (fall back to `cn`) → `hostname`+`dnsName`; `operatingSystem` → `os`; `operatingSystemVersion` → `osVersion`; `description` → `notes` (only if empty); `whenCreated` → `acquiredAt` (only if older); `lastLogonTimestamp` (Windows FILETIME) → `lastSeen` **only if newer than the existing value** — never regresses fresher data from Entra/Intune; `distinguishedName` OU path → `learnedLocation`; `operatingSystem` fed through `inferAssetTypeFromOs()` → `assetType` (only if still `other`).
- **Note on `lastLogonTimestamp`** — this attribute replicates approximately every 14 days by design. Use it as a coarse "last seen" signal; it will lag reality.
- **Filters** — `ouInclude` / `ouExclude` arrays match against the computer's full `distinguishedName` with wildcard support (e.g. `*OU=Workstations*`, `*OU=Servers,OU=HQ*`).
- **Forward-DNS IP backfill** — AD computer objects don't carry an IP, so `syncActiveDirectoryDevices` runs a forward-DNS pre-pass (A/AAAA lookup via `getConfiguredResolver()`) before the per-device upsert loop. Fills `Asset.ipAddress` on **new devices** and **existing devices whose `ipAddress` is empty**; devices that already have an IP from any source (FortiGate DHCP/ARP, Entra/Intune, operator-typed) are skipped — DNS can lag the real network state and we don't want to overwrite authoritative data. Failures (NXDOMAIN, resolver timeout, etc.) leave the IP null and retry on the next discovery cycle; a temporarily-missing DNS record self-heals. Bounded concurrency via the existing `batchSettled` helper (50 in parallel). Result Map keyed by `objectGuid.toLowerCase()` so each update/create looks it up cheaply. IP source stamped `ipSource = "activedirectory-dns"` to distinguish background AD-driven lookups from the operator-triggered `POST /assets/:id/forward-lookup` (which stamps `"dns"`). The Prisma extension in `src/db.ts` auto-populates `AssetIpHistory` rows on the write. If the configured DNS resolver factory throws (misconfigured DoH URL, etc.), the pre-pass logs a warning event and the rest of the sync proceeds without IP backfill — non-fatal.
- **Monitor default for realm-monitorable hosts** — Computer objects whose `operatingSystem` contains "windows" or "linux" are stamped with `discoveredByIntegrationId` *on first sight* so the polling-method resolver can see the AD source link. The default response-time polling method for AD-discovered assets is **ICMP** (the source default for the activedirectory source kind) — modern hardened Windows hosts often refuse WinRM by default and most domain-joined Linux doesn't enable SSH. Operators who want the bind-credentials probe set `responseTimePolling = "winrm"` (Windows) or `"ssh"` (realm-joined Linux) on the asset; the dispatcher's AD bind-credentials fallback (`probeAsset` in `monitoringService.ts`) routes those calls through the integration's `bindDn`/`bindPassword` — WinRM SOAP Identify against `https://<host>:5986` for Windows, SSH connect+auth on port 22 for Linux. **The bind DN must be in UPN form (`user@domain.com`) or down-level form (`DOMAIN\user`)** so WinRM/realmd accept it; raw LDAP DN form (`CN=svc,OU=...`) authenticates against LDAP bind but fails the probe. No separate Credential row is needed — the integration's bind credentials cover any AD asset whose `monitorCredentialId` is null. The legacy "discovery stamps `monitorType = activedirectory`" pathway was retired in step 3i of the polling-method redesign; existing AD-discovered assets that were on the bind-creds probe before the redesign keep that behaviour because the migration job (3e) projected `monitorType="activedirectory"` to `responseTimePolling="winrm"` / `"ssh"` per `getAdMonitorProtocol(os)` before the column was dropped.

### Hybrid-join cross-link (AD ↔ Entra ID)

Active Directory and Entra ID identify the same hybrid-joined device with two unrelated GUIDs (AD `objectGUID` vs Entra `deviceId`). The reliable cross-link is the on-prem **SID** — AD's `objectSid` equals Entra's `onPremisesSecurityIdentifier`.
