- **AD discovery is on the multi-source asset model** (Phase 2 cutover) **and the projection layer** (Phase 3b.1 cutover): the AD sync writes an `AssetSource` row with `sourceKind="ad"`, `externalId=<objectGUID>`, and a rich source-shaped `observed` blob containing the raw LDAP fields (objectSid, cn, dnsHostName, distinguishedName, ouPath, OS+version, description, whenCreated, lastLogonTimestamp, accountDisabled). Lookups for re-discovery (GUID match), hybrid-cross-link (SID match), and hostname-collision detection all read from `AssetSource` (joining Entra rows for SID matches via `observed.onPremisesSecurityIdentifier`). The legacy `assetTag = "ad:{guid}"` and `tags = ["ad-guid:{guid}", "sid:{SID}", ...]` markers are still written for back-compat with the rest of the codebase; they're retired in Phase 4. **Discovery-owned Asset fields** (hostname, os, osVersion, learnedLocation, serialNumber, manufacturer, model) come from `projectAssetFromSources()` instead of inline merge logic — for the existing-asset path the AD source is upserted first, then all of the asset's sources are re-fetched and projected; for the create path the AD source's observed blob is projected synthetically (single-source array, no DB roundtrip needed). Operator-owned fields (status, lastSeen, acquiredAt, assetType, notes, tags, monitor stamps, dnsName) keep their inline logic — projection only owns what discovery owns. Single Asset write per device per cycle.
- Both services still stamp `sid:{SID}` (uppercase) in the asset's `tags` array.
- AD additionally stamps `ad-guid:{guid}` (lowercase hex) in `tags` so the AD GUID stays findable even after Entra takes over the primary `assetTag`.
- **Priority rule:** when both sources have the same device, Entra "takes over" the existing Asset row by upserting its own AssetSource entra/intune row keyed on `deviceId`. If AD created the asset first, the next Entra run finds it via the SID cross-link (entra.observed.onPremisesSecurityIdentifier ↔ ad.observed.objectSid) and stamps the entra source row alongside the existing ad source row — both AssetSource rows now live on the same Asset. If Entra created it first, the next AD run finds it via SID and stamps the ad source row in the same way. Phase 4d retired the assetTag mirror; AssetSource.externalId is the only canonical lookup key going forward.
- **MAC cross-link (Entra-only, tertiary).** When the Entra deviceId lookup misses AND the SID lookup misses (or the device has no SID — e.g. cloud-only Azure AD joined with no on-prem identity), the Entra sync falls through to a third match: incoming **Ethernet MAC** against `Asset.macAddress` and `Asset.macAddresses[]` history (any entry, any source). MAC matches are treated as positive identity confirmation — same physical box, just got a new Entra deviceId (re-enroll, re-image, NIC swap into a previously-known device) — and silently take over the matched asset the same way SID does. WiFi MAC is *not* used for this cascade because it randomizes per-network on modern Windows / iOS / Android. Every silent take-over writes one info-level `syncLog` event (`MAC cross-link: ...`) so the merge is auditable. This pathway replaced the noisy `mac-collision` conflict flavour, which was raising operator-review queues that almost always resolved as "yes, same device."
- **Integration filter** — `assetMatchesIntegrationFilter` for AD prefers the AD AssetSource's `observed.ouPath` over the merged `Asset.learnedLocation`. Source-side data is authoritative because the merged `learnedLocation` field can drift between integrations (a FortiGate sighting can overwrite AD's OU path), but the AD source's own observation is fixed by what LDAP returned at the last AD sync. Falls back to `learnedLocation` for callers that haven't loaded sources yet (back-compat).
- **Conflict records** — the asset-conflict schema now carries `proposedAssetFields.assetTagPrefix` (`"ad:"` or `"entra:"`) so the accept/reject route applies the correct tag. Entra-only conflicts predating this field still default to the Entra prefix for backward compatibility.

---

## Background Jobs

| Job | Schedule | Purpose |
|-----|----------|---------|
| `expireReservations` | Every 15 min | Mark reservations past `expiresAt` as `expired` |
| `discoveryScheduler` | Per-integration `pollInterval` | Auto-trigger FMG / FortiGate / Windows Server / Entra ID / Active Directory discovery |
| `ouiRefresh` | Periodic | Refresh IEEE OUI database for MAC vendor lookup |
| `pruneEvents` | Nightly | Delete Event records older than 7 days |
| `updateCheck` | Periodic | Check for software updates |
| `clampAssetAcquiredAt` | Once at startup | Clamp `acquiredAt` down to `lastSeen` on any Asset row where the invariant was violated |
| `normalizeManufacturers` | Once at startup | Idempotent: seed default manufacturer aliases on a fresh install, load the in-memory cache used by the Prisma extension in `src/db.ts`, and rewrite any existing `Asset.manufacturer` / `MibFile.manufacturer` values that the alias map canonicalizes to something different. Mutations to the alias map at runtime (`POST/PUT /manufacturer-aliases`) re-run `applyAliasesToExistingRows()` in the background so admin edits propagate to historical data without a restart. |
| `seedAssetTypes` | Once at startup | Idempotent. Defense-in-depth seed of the eight built-in `AssetTypeDef` rows (the registry-cutover migration is the authoritative seed; this catches fresh Docker volumes / restored backups where the migration didn't run). After the seed, `refreshCache()` populates the in-memory map used by `isKnownAssetType()` in `src/utils/assetTypes.ts` — which is the synchronous validator backing every `/assets` and `/monitor-settings` Zod schema. Failures log but never block boot — the validator falls back to accepting the eight built-in names while the cache is cold. |
| `seedManufacturerProfiles` | Once at startup | Idempotent (marker `Setting.seedManufacturerProfilesSeededAt`). Slice 6a — converts the hardcoded `VENDOR_TELEMETRY_PROFILES` constant into `ManufacturerProfile` + `ManufacturerProfileMetric` + `ManufacturerProfileMetricOverride` rows. One profile per distinct canonical manufacturer; the Fortinet sub-family entries (FortiSwitch / FortiAP) become `modelPattern`-keyed overrides under the umbrella Fortinet profile. The memory metric row + override carry the bytes-form `composition` blob via `memoryQueryToComposition()` so the editable profile lines up with the hardcoded baseline's multi-OID memory queries. Calls `refreshProfileCache()` at the end so the in-memory cache is warm. The hardcoded constant stays runtime-authoritative — this job only owns persistence. Best-effort: failures log and never block boot. |
| `backfillManufacturerProfileMemoryComposition` | Once at startup | Idempotent (marker `Setting.backfillManufacturerProfileMemoryCompositionAt`). Stamps the multi-OID memory `composition` blob onto existing `ManufacturerProfileMetric` + `ManufacturerProfileMetricOverride` rows that predate the column. Pairs with the inline emission in `seedManufacturerProfiles` so fresh installs get composition from the start; this job is for installs that ran the seed before the column existed. Two safety checks per row: composition stays null, AND the existing `defaultSymbol` / `symbol` matches the hardcoded entry's primary OID (operator-edited rows are skipped). Refreshes the profile cache on completion. |
| `backfillAssetSources` | Once at startup | Idempotent. Walks every Asset row and upserts `AssetSource` rows derived from the legacy `assetTag` / `sid:` / `ad-guid:` tag conventions (entra → "entra:" assetTag; ad → "ad:" assetTag; fortigate firewall → "fgt:" assetTag) **plus a Fortinet-infrastructure fallback** keyed on `manufacturer="Fortinet" + non-empty serialNumber + assetType` — `firewall`/`switch`/`access_point` map to `fortigate-firewall`/`fortiswitch`/`fortiap` respectively. Catches pre-tag firewalls and the un-tagged switch/AP fleet in one pass. ad-guid breadcrumbs become `inferred=true` AD recovery rows; everything else falls through to a single "manual" row keyed on Asset.id. Pairs with the shadow-write Prisma extension in `src/db.ts` which keeps the table fresh between restarts whenever an asset's `assetTag`, `tags`, or `discoveredByIntegrationId` change. |
| `resolveStaleReservationConflicts` | Once at startup | Idempotent cleanup. Auto-rejects pending reservation Conflict rows whose stored proposed values now match the live Reservation values on every field listed in `conflictFields` — i.e. conflicts that were raised by an old discovery run but where the values have since come back into sync. Pairs with the inline fix in `upsertConflict` that prevents new lingering conflicts going forward. |
| `scrubLegacySidGuidTags` | Once at startup | Idempotent. Phase 4b cleanup of the multi-source asset model: strips legacy `sid:<SID>` and `ad-guid:<GUID>` entries from `Asset.tags`. Both signals now live on AssetSource and the tag mirroring is redundant. Pairs with discovery-side cuts in `syncEntraDevices` / `syncActiveDirectoryDevices` that stop writing the markers going forward. Leaves `Asset.assetTag` (entra:/ad:/fgt:) and `prev-*` breadcrumb tags untouched — those need parallel migration of searchService + conflict resolution before retirement. |
| `cleanupStaleDnsResolvedReleased` | Once at startup | Idempotent. Hard-deletes every `Reservation` row with `sourceType="dns_resolved" AND status="released"`. Before the release→delete cutover, `releaseRows` / `releaseDnsResolvedAt` flipped dns_resolved rows to `status="released"` and left them in place, permanently occupying the (subnetId, ipAddress, "released") slot under the `@@unique([subnetId, ipAddress, status])` index. That blocked later status transitions at the same target — surfacing on prod as discovery-sync P2002 errors where the inline retry's `update→released` step itself couldn't complete because the slot was already taken. dns_resolved is a system-created fallback marker with no audit value, so the rows are deleted outright. No marker — the deleteMany IS the converge check. |
| `mergeFortiswitchEndpointGhosts` | Once at startup | Idempotent. Merges orphan `fortigate-endpoint` assets that represent a managed FortiSwitch's own management MAC (created by DHCP/ARP/MAC-table pathway before FortiSwitch discovery captured `baseMac` in any cycle) INTO the corresponding `assetType="switch"` asset that lacks a MAC. Cursor-pages through `Asset.macAddress IS NULL` switches; for each, joins to its `fortiswitch` source `observed.mgmtIp` and looks for a sibling endpoint whose `ipAddress` matches AND `lastSeenSwitch` starts with `<switch.hostname>/` AND carries a `fortigate-endpoint` source. Exactly-one match → transactional merge: re-points `AssetMacAddress` / `AssetAssociatedIp` / `AssetIpHistory` / `AssetFortigateSighting` rows (delete-on-conflict for unique violations), stamps the adopted MAC on the switch, drops the orphan's AssetSource rows, cascade-deletes the orphan. Multiple matches log a warn and skip. Pairs with the inline MAC-fallback lookup in the FortiSwitch update path in `syncDhcpSubnets` which prevents recurrence going forward. |
| `mergeDuplicateHostnameAssets` | Once at startup | Idempotent. Broader sibling of `mergeFortiswitchEndpointGhosts` — catches every shape of accumulated duplicate-hostname Asset row that the narrower NULL-MAC job misses (workstation ghosts where the FortiGate created one `fortigate-endpoint` per MAC sighting, Phase-1 backfill `manual`-only placeholders, post-baseMac FortiSwitch ghosts, FortiAP equivalents). Groups every Asset row by `lower(hostname)`, walks groups with ≥2 rows, picks a canonical by source-kind priority (entra/intune/ad/polaris-agent (1) > fortiswitch (2) > fortiap (3) > fortigate-firewall (4) > fortigate-endpoint (5) > manual (6) > no sources (7); tiebreak by most-recent `lastSeen` then `updatedAt`), then transactionally transfers `AssetMacAddress` / `AssetAssociatedIp` / `AssetIpHistory` / `AssetFortigateSighting` rows from each sibling onto the canonical with delete-on-conflict for unique violations. Cascade-delete on the sibling clears its `AssetSource` rows, every sample/rollup table, LLDP/wireless rows, pending `Conflict` rows pointing at it (so the queue empties naturally), and `AssetDependencyParent` rows on either side (the 60s `dependencyReconciler` tick recomputes from authoritative topology). Scalar-field absorption fills empty canonical fields from each sibling (macAddress / ipAddress / serialNumber / manufacturer / model / os / osVersion / assignedTo / notes / learnedLocation / acquiredAt) and keeps the more-recent `lastSeen`; tags union-merge. Tie-safety: groups where two rows share the top tier AND have non-null disagreeing primary MACs are skipped as "genuinely two devices" and logged at warn with the asset ids for operator review. **Dry-run mode**: set `POLARIS_GHOST_MERGE_DRY_RUN=1` to log every decision (host + canonical id + ghost ids + source kinds + tiers) without writing — use on first deploy to review choices, then unset the env var and let the next restart actually merge. Writes one `asset.duplicate_merged` Event per merged group for the UI audit trail. No marker — the query itself is the converge check; re-runs without the env var find zero candidates once convergent. |
| `migrateMonitorStatusRename` | Once at startup | Idempotent. Renames the recovery state on `Asset.monitorStatus` from the legacy value `"pending"` to `"recovering"`. The state machine in monitoringService writes `"recovering"` exclusively going forward; this catches rows stamped before the rename. Marker key `monitorStatusRenamePendingMigratedAt`. Recovery: delete the marker and restart. |
| `migrateAutoMonitorInterfacesShape` | Once at startup | Idempotent (`autoMonitorInterfacesShapeMigratedAt` marker). Rewrites every FMG/FortiGate integration's `Integration.config.{fortigateMonitor|fortiswitchMonitor|fortiapMonitor}.autoMonitorInterfaces` from the legacy single-mode discriminated union (`{mode: "names"|"wildcard"|"type", ...}`) into the multi-block union (`{byNames?, byPatterns?, byTypes?, byLldp?}`). Pairs with the Zod-layer + apply-route + frontend-renderer `coerceLegacySelection` calls that handle the mixed state between deploy and this job running. Already-new-shape rows pass through unchanged on re-runs. Recovery: delete the marker and restart. |
| `migrateSystemInfoCadenceLinkage` | Once at startup | Links `systemInfoIntervalSeconds` at the integration tier to each integration's discovery `pollInterval`. Idempotent (`systemInfoCadenceLinkageMigratedAt` marker). For every integration whose stored `Integration.config.monitorSettings.systemInfoIntervalSeconds` equals the literal hardcoded default (600s), rewrites the field to null so the resolver's pollInterval-derived tier kicks in (`pollInterval × 3600`). Operators who explicitly typed any other value are NOT touched. Cuts interface-sample volume by 24× at the default 4h pollInterval. Writes one `monitor_settings.systemInfo_cadence_linked` Event per affected integration with `{previousValue: 600, pollIntervalHours, newDerivedCadenceSec}`. Invalidates the resolver cache at the end so the next monitor tick picks up the change without a process restart. Recovery: delete the marker and restart. See "Integration pollInterval linkage" under Monitor Settings Hierarchy. |
| `migrateMonitorSettingsPerClass` | Once at startup | Phase 2 of the monitoring-tab redesign. Idempotent (`monitorSettingsPerClassMigratedAt` marker). For every `fortimanager` / `fortigate` / `activedirectory` / `entraid` / `windowsserver` integration: (1) seeds the per-class `streams` blocks under `config.<klass>Monitor.streams.<stream>` from the flat `config.monitorSettings` baseline (FMG/FortiGate populates `fortigateMonitor` / `fortiswitchMonitor` / `fortiapMonitor`; AD/Entra/WinSrv populates `workstationMonitor` / `serverMonitor`; FortiAP omits storage). (2) Folds integration-scoped `MonitorClassOverride` rows (`integrationId = <this integration>`) onto the matching `(class, stream)` cell — per-row values win over the flat baseline — and deletes the absorbed rows in the same transaction. Manual-scope rows (`integrationId IS NULL`) are intentionally left untouched. (3) FMG/FortiGate per-class `snmpCredentialId` / `sshCredentialId` legacy fields pre-populate stream `credentialId` slots: SNMP credential for SNMP-polling streams, SSH credential for SSH-polling streams. (4) Writes one `monitor_settings.per_class_migrated` Event per integration listing absorbed override ids + class blocks seeded. Invalidates the resolver cache at the end so the next monitor tick picks up the per-class shape without a restart. Recovery: delete the marker and restart. |
| `renameMonitorClassKeys` | Once at startup | Phase 0 of the tiered sample-retention work. Idempotent (`monitorClassKeysRenamedAt` marker). Copies legacy `fortiswitch` / `fortiap` per-class keys to the generic `switch` / `accessPoint` names in `Setting("monitorSettings")` / `Setting("manualMonitorSettings")` / every `Integration.config.monitorSettings`. Defensive — leaves old keys in place so the backwards-compat reader in `getMonitorSettings()` keeps working through the transition. |
| `migrateRetentionTiers` | Once at startup | Phase 1 of tiered retention. Idempotent (`retentionTiersMigratedAt` marker). Seeds `*DetailRetentionDays` / `*HourlyRetentionDays` / `*DailyRetentionDays` fields into the tier-3 JSON shapes and `MonitorClassOverride` rows. Phase 5 superseded these JSON fields with the global `Setting("sampleRetention")`; the migration stays in the boot block but its writes are unused going forward. |
| `consolidateSampleRetention` | Once at startup | Phase 5 of tiered retention. Idempotent (`sampleRetentionConsolidatedAt` marker). Pulls legacy single-tier retention values out of `manualMonitorSettings` and the legacy `monitorSettings` row into the new global `Setting("sampleRetention")` shape. Per-class breakdown (default / switch / accessPoint) preserved; hourly/daily seeded with 30/365 defaults. Safe on fresh installs. |
| `decommissionStaleAssets` | Every 24 hours | Move assets whose `lastSeen` is older than the configured inactivity threshold (months) to `decommissioned` status. Configured via Events → Settings → Assets tab; 0 disables. |
| `flagStaleReservations` | Every 6 hours (and 30 s after startup) | Scans active `dhcp_reservation` rows for ones whose target client hasn't been seen actively holding the IP within the configured threshold (`reservation.staleAfterDays`, default 60, 0 disables). For each fresh transition into "stale," writes one `reservation.stale` Event at warning level and stamps `staleNotifiedAt` so the alert doesn't refire. The discovery sync clears `staleNotifiedAt` (and `staleSnoozedUntil`) when the IP is seen active again, so a reservation that comes back online and goes silent later re-arms cleanly. Cold-start safety: a `reservationStaleDetectionStartedAt` Setting is stamped on first run and used as a per-row baseline floor (`max(createdAt, detectionStartedAt)`) so the first scan after migration doesn't flood every existing dhcp_reservation row before discovery has had time to populate `lastSeenLeased`. Rows with `staleIgnored=true` are excluded permanently until an admin un-ignores. |
| `discoverySlowCheck` | Every 30 s | Compares each in-flight discovery's elapsed time to its rolling-duration baseline (`discoveryDurationService`). Emits one `integration.discover.slow` event per run (and one per FortiGate inside an FMG run) when elapsed exceeds `max(avg + 2σ, avg × 1.5, avg + 60 s)`; baseline requires ≥3 prior successful runs. The `/integrations/discoveries` endpoint also calls the same checker inline so the sidebar and Integrations page flip amber within one 4 s poll cycle. Also calls `expireVerboseLogging()` on every tick to write `verboseLogging: false` back to the DB for any integration whose 30-minute verbose-logging window has closed. |
| `capacityWatch` | Every 10 minutes (and 60 s after startup) | Builds a capacity snapshot via `getCapacitySnapshot` and calls `recordCapacityTransition` so a flip into watch/amber/red writes one `capacity.severity_changed` Event independent of whether anyone has the Maintenance tab open. The route handler in `serverSettings.ts` also calls `recordCapacityTransition` on every `/pg-tuning` fetch so the audit trail lands in real time when an admin is actively viewing — the periodic job is the out-of-band safety net for "the DB is moments from dying and the UI is about to be unreachable." Best-effort: failures are logged at debug and never thrown. The first run on each fresh install also stamps the baseline `capacity.lastSeverity` Setting. |
| `backfillDependencyTree` | Once at startup (30s after boot) | Idempotent. Runs `recomputeDependencyTree()` so existing installs see populated `AssetDependencyParent` rows + `Asset.dependencyLayer` without waiting for the next scheduled discovery cycle. Failures log and never block boot. |
| `dependencyReconciler` | Every 60s (and 60s after startup) | Source of truth for `Asset.dependencySuppressed`. Calls `reconcileDependencySuppression()` — evaluates desired state under all-down multi-parent semantics in BFS layer order, writes only diffs, emits `monitor.dependency_suppressed` / `monitor.dependency_resumed` events on transitions. Independent `running` guard. The `recordProbeResult` event hook (`propagateAfterStatusChange`) calls the same reconciler on every `up ↔ down` transition as a latency optimization; the periodic tick is what guarantees correctness when the hook misses (server restart, race). |
| `retryQueuedReservationPushes` | Every 60s (and 60s after startup) | Source of truth for advancing queued DHCP reservations (`pushStatus="pending"`) into `synced` once the target FortiGate is reachable. Calls `reservationService.retryPendingReservations()` which scans all pending rows ordered by `pushQueuedAt asc`, runs per-row eligibility re-check (subnet drift → cancel queue + flip pushStatus=null; integration deleted/disabled → cancel), discovery-supersede check (another active row at same IP → flip to failed_permanent), readiness gates (monitored firewall must be `monitorStatus="up"`; unmonitored uses exponential backoff `min(60 * 2^(attempts-1), 1800)`s keyed on `pushLastAttemptAt`), then attempts the push and stamps the outcome. Emits per-row Events: `reservation.push.queued.succeeded` (info), `.retry_failed` (info — kept low-volume so sustained outages don't spam warning), `.failed_permanent` (warning), `.cancelled` (info), `.collided` (warning). Independent `running` guard. The `monitor.status_changed → up` hook in `monitoringService` fires `triggerRetryAfterStatusChange(assetId)` for latency optimization — count-gated against pending rows so most up-transitions cost zero work. No TTL — pending rows live forever until success, release, or operator-triggered retry-now (operator decision per the queued-push design). |
| `reconcileMapRegions` | Every 6 hours (and 60s after startup) | Periodic safety net for the map-region tag reconciler. Calls `reconcileMapRegions()` from `mapRegionService` — add-only pass over every operator-drawn region, never strips. Renames and deletes are handled inline by their dedicated CRUD paths and end-of-FMG/FortiGate discovery also reconciles via Phase 13; this tick catches anything those paths missed (server restart mid-edit, firewall coords updated outside discovery, etc.). Writes one `region.tags_reconciled` Event on cycles that touched at least one asset. Independent `running` guard. Best-effort: failures log at debug. |
| `reconcileDnsResolvedReservations` | Every 30 minutes (and 30s after startup) | Periodic sweep that calls `reconcileDnsResolvedForAllAssets()` from `dnsResolvedReservationService`. For every Asset with `ipAddress` set, reconciles a `dns_resolved` Reservation row at the IP — creates one when the asset is eligible (active/maintenance/storage/quarantined, IPv4, IP falls inside a non-deprecated subnet, no authoritative reservation already there), updates the existing system row's hostname/MAC when they've changed, releases the row when the asset becomes ineligible, and releases stale rows from prior IPs when the asset's IP has moved. Backfill + safety net for the Prisma extension hook in `src/db.ts` which handles real-time reconcile on asset writes. Independent `running` guard. Per-asset reconcile is best-effort — failures log at warn level and never throw. |
| `runSampleRollup` | Hourly tick every 30 min (60s after boot), daily tick at 02:30 UTC (90s after boot) | Populates the twelve sample-rollup tables (`*_hourly` and `*_daily`) via `sampleRollupService.rollupHourly()` / `rollupDaily()`. Two ticking loops with independent `running` guards so a slow hourly tick can't block daily and vice versa. Each tick stamps `Setting("sampleRollup.<tier>.lastSuccess")` with per-table row counts; capacityService consumes those timestamps for the `sample_rollup_lagging` watch reason. Lookback windows: hourly 2 h (covers samples late from the 2 s sampleWriteBuffer flush across an hour rollover), daily 2 d. Idempotent — INSERT...ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE rewrites buckets in place. Observed via `polaris_sample_rollup_duration_seconds{tier,table}` histogram + the standard `polaris_job_duration_seconds{job="sampleRollup.<tier>"}` / `polaris_job_total{job="sampleRollup.<tier>", outcome}`. |
| `monitorAssets` | Light loop every 5 s, heavy loop every 30 s (independent `running` guards) | Handles four independent cadences per monitored asset: **(1) response-time probe** when `lastMonitorAt + (Asset.monitorIntervalSec ?? monitor.intervalSeconds)` has elapsed (FortiOS REST, SNMP `sysUpTime`, WinRM SOAP Identify, SSH connect+auth, ICMP ping). The `activedirectory` monitor type dispatches to WinRM for Windows or SSH for realm-joined Linux, both reusing the AD integration's bind credentials. Writes one `AssetMonitorSample` per probe (`responseTimeMs` null = packet loss), updates `Asset.monitorStatus` / `lastResponseTimeMs` / `consecutiveFailures`, and emits one `monitor.status_changed` Event on `up ↔ down` transitions (threshold is `monitor.failureThreshold`). **(2) Telemetry pull** when `lastTelemetryAt + (telemetryIntervalSec ?? monitor.telemetryIntervalSeconds)` has elapsed — CPU% + memory snapshot via FortiOS `/api/v2/monitor/system/resource/usage` or SNMP. The SNMP path consults `vendorTelemetryProfiles` for the asset's `manufacturer + os + model` and queries vendor-specific OIDs first when the profile resolves through `oidRegistry` (i.e. the relevant MIB has been uploaded to Server Settings → Credentials → MIB Database). Built-in profiles cover Cisco IOS/IOS-XE/NX-OS (`cpmCPUTotal5secRev`, `ciscoMemoryPoolUsed`+`ciscoMemoryPoolFree` — all three seeded into `oidRegistry`'s built-in table so the probe works without uploading CISCO-PROCESS-MIB or CISCO-MEMORY-POOL-MIB), Juniper Junos (`jnxOperatingCPU`, `jnxOperatingBuffer` % — both seeded into the built-in table so the probe works without uploading JUNIPER-MIB), Mikrotik (`mtxrSystemUserCPULoad` — not seeded; RouterOS CPU resolves via the HOST-RESOURCES-MIB `hrProcessorLoad` fallback path), Fortinet FortiSwitch (matched before the generic Fortinet entry via `model="FortiSwitch"`; CPU uses `fsSysCpuUsage` @ 12356.106.4.1.2 scalar percent; memory uses the bytes form — `fsSysMemUsage` @ 12356.106.4.1.3 + `fsSysMemCapacity` @ 12356.106.4.1.4 — NOT a percent like the FortiGate variant. Disk uses the same shape: `fsSysDiskUsage` @ 12356.106.4.1.5 + `fsSysDiskCapacity` @ 12356.106.4.1.6, consumed by `collectSystemInfoSnmp`'s vendor disk fallback when HRM returns no disks. All five symbols seeded into `oidRegistry` so the probe works without uploading FORTINET-FORTISWITCH-MIB), Fortinet SNMP path (`fgSysCpuUsage`, `fgSysMemUsage` — both seeded into `oidRegistry`'s built-in table so FortiGate CPU/memory work without uploading FORTINET-FORTIGATE-MIB, matching the always-on hardware-sensor temperature fallback), HP/Aruba ProCurve (`hpSwitchCpuStat` — seeded into the built-in table so the probe works without uploading STATISTICS-MIB), and Dell PowerConnect / Force10 (`rlCpuUtilDuringLastMinute` under the RADLAN enterprise 89, seeded into the built-in table so the probe works without uploading the RADLAN MIB). When a vendor query yields nothing — no profile match, MIB not uploaded, or the device doesn't expose the OID — each metric independently falls back to standard HOST-RESOURCES-MIB (`hrProcessorLoad`, `hrStorageRam`). Writes one `AssetTelemetrySample` per tick. **Temperature scrapes dispatch separately** via `collectTemperatures`, driven by `temperaturePolling` (not `cpuMemoryPolling`) — `runTelemetryFor` calls both in parallel on each telemetry tick so an operator can run CPU/memory over REST while temperature scrapes over SNMP (the branch-class FortiGate workaround when `/api/v2/monitor/system/sensor-info` is unreliable). Temperature pulls from FortiOS `/api/v2/monitor/system/sensor-info` (filtered to type=temperature) or SNMP ENTITY-SENSOR-MIB (entPhySensorType=8/celsius) with the FortiSwitch / Fortinet `fgHwSensorTable` / FortiAP `fapTemperature` scalar fallbacks. Writes N `AssetTemperatureSample` rows per successful scrape; failures leave the prior rows in place so the System tab keeps showing the last known reading. **(3) System info pull** when `lastSystemInfoAt + (systemInfoIntervalSec ?? monitor.systemInfoIntervalSeconds)` has elapsed (default systemInfo cadence is now linked to the originating integration's `pollInterval` × 3600 unless an operator pinned an explicit tier-3 value — see the "Integration pollInterval linkage" tier under Monitor Settings Hierarchy) — interfaces (FortiOS `/monitor/system/interface` + CMDB merge for `alias`/`description`, or SNMP IF-MIB including `ifAlias` for the operator-set label; both paths capture ifInErrors/ifOutErrors and FortiOS errors_in/out) + storage (SNMP HOST-RESOURCES-MIB `hrStorageTable`; when that returns no disk rows, the SNMP path falls back to a vendor disk scalar query — currently FortiSwitch `fsSysDiskUsage` / `fsSysDiskCapacity` — and emits one StorageSample per matched profile; gated on the resolved `storagePolling === "snmp"` so the rows are dropped when the operator hasn't opted in) + IPsec tunnels (FortiOS `/monitor/vpn/ipsec` plus a parallel `/cmdb/vpn.ipsec/phase1-interface` lookup that resolves each tunnel's `parentInterface` — the FortiOS CLI `set interface` value used by the System tab to nest tunnel rows under their parent in the Interfaces table; phase-2 selectors are rolled up to a single up/down/partial state per phase-1 with summed byte counters; ADVPN dynamic shortcuts are filtered out by their `parent` field) + LLDP neighbors (FortiOS `/monitor/system/interface/lldp-neighbors` for fortinet probes, SNMP LLDP-MIB walk for SNMP probes — `lldpRemTable` joined to `lldpLocPortTable` for the localPortNum→ifName mapping plus `lldpRemManAddrTable` for management IPs). Writes N `AssetInterfaceSample` rows + M `AssetStorageSample` rows + K `AssetIpsecTunnelSample` rows + L `AssetLldpNeighbor` rows (replaced in full per asset on each successful pass — neighbors that aren't in the latest scrape are deleted in the same transaction; matchedAssetId is resolved at persist time by joining management IP / chassis MAC / system name against the asset inventory); also mirrors per-interface IP+MAC into `Asset.associatedIps` (preserving manual entries). **(4) Fast filtered scrape** rides the response-time cadence when the asset has any `monitoredInterfaces`, `monitoredStorage`, or `monitoredIpsecTunnels` pinned (and the full systemInfo pass didn't already run this tick). Calls `collectFastFiltered` which performs one collector round-trip — interfaces + storage on SNMP, interfaces + (conditionally) IPsec on FortiOS — and only writes sample rows for the pinned subset. **The SNMP path uses wire-level filtering** (`collectFastFilteredSnmp`): a small discovery walk maps pinned ifNames → ifIndex (and pinned mountPaths → hrStorageIndex when storage is pinned), then one batched `snmpMultiGet` pulls only the column-at-index OIDs the fast pass needs (~13 per pinned interface, ~3 per pinned storage). On a 48-port switch with one pinned interface that's ~110 OID-fetches in one PDU vs ~770 across 16 column walks under the legacy full-walk path — drops fast-cadence p90 from multi-second to sub-second on busy fleets and cuts the Capacity Advisor's recommended `POLARIS_MONITOR_FAST_WORKERS` proportionally. LLDP and wireless are always omitted on the fast cadence (LLDP rides the heavy pass; wireless isn't a fast-pin target). The FortiOS REST path still pulls the full `/api/v2/monitor/system/interface` payload and filters in code — the endpoint has no per-name query param. Storage and IPsec rows ride the same monitorInterval setting (default 60s) so the operator gets sub-minute disk-usage and tunnel-state history for chosen mountpoints / tunnels without re-walking the full tables. ICMP/SSH cannot deliver telemetry/system-info; WinRM/AD return supported=false until WMI Enumerate-over-WS-Management lands. **Worker scheduling**: cadences are split across two independent ticking loops. The **light loop** (probe + fastFiltered) ticks every 5 s with concurrency `cpus().length * 2` (clamped 8..64, env-overridable via `POLARIS_PROBE_CONCURRENCY`); the **heavy loop** (telemetry + systemInfo) ticks every 30 s with concurrency `cpus().length` (clamped 4..32, env-overridable via `POLARIS_HEAVY_CONCURRENCY`). Each loop has its own `running` guard, so a 75-second wedged systemInfo on a dead host blocks ONLY the next heavy tick — the light loop keeps firing on its own clock and probes stay close to their configured cadence regardless. Within a pass, workers don't reserve assets; they just pull the next item from the cadence queue. `runMonitorPass({cadences})` selects which cadences a given call owns; the default (no `cadences` arg) covers all four for legacy callers like `POST /assets/:id/probe-now`. **Five-state monitor machine**. `Asset.monitorStatus` is one of `up | warning | recovering | down | unknown`, driven by two counters: `consecutiveFailures` (existing) + `consecutiveSuccesses` (added with the hierarchy work). The resolved `failureThreshold` doubles as the recovery threshold — same number of confirmations gates both directions. (UI label note: `recovering` shows as **Recovering**, `unknown`/null shows as **Pending**; same blue color, different labels.)

| Previous status | Probe success | Probe failure |
|---|---|---|
| `unknown` (never probed) | → `recovering` (cs=1) | → `warning` (cf=1) |
| `recovering` (was down, now succeeding) | → `up` if cs ≥ threshold else stay `recovering` | → `down` if cf ≥ threshold else stay `recovering` |
| `up` | stay `up` | → `warning` (cf=1) |
| `warning` | → `up` if cs ≥ threshold else stay `warning` | → `down` if cf ≥ threshold else stay `warning` |
| `down` | → `recovering` (cs=1) | stay `down` |

Either counter resets to 0 on the opposite outcome, so an in-flight transition is always unambiguous. The Status pill on the assets list and asset details modal renders the five colors: green (up), amber (warning), blue **Recovering** (was-down, now succeeding), red (down), grey (`monitored=false`, "unmonitored"). The `unknown` / null state (asset never probed) reuses the same blue CSS treatment but with the label **Pending** so operators can distinguish "we don't know yet" from "we know it's recovering". The `monitor.status_changed` audit Event still fires only on `up ↔ down` transitions; the per-tick warning/recovering churn shows up visually in the asset System tab's intermittency bar (one cell per 1h-window probe sample, replayed through the same state machine forward from `unknown`).

**Heavy-cadence suppression**: telemetry / systemInfo / fastFiltered run **only** when `monitorStatus === "up"`. Every other state — including `warning` (mid-degradation), `recovering` (mid-recovery), `down`, and `unknown` — suppresses heavy cadences so the System tab stops accumulating samples that aren't going to be reliable. The cheap response-time probe keeps firing in every state.

**No probe backoff** — down hosts keep probing at the resolved `intervalSeconds`. Recovery is detected within one base-cadence tick (worst case 60 s on default settings, vs. up to 30 min under the old bucketed backoff that was dropped along with the new monitor-settings hierarchy). Trades a small amount of worker budget on chronically-down hosts for fast recovery detection. `lastSystemInfoAt` doesn't advance during the outage (only bumps when a scrape returned data), so systemInfo is due immediately on recovery and the System tab refreshes naturally. The same "only on data" rule also stops a transport failure from silently emptying the System tab Interfaces table — the GET endpoint anchors interface display to `lastSystemInfoAt`, and bumping it without inserting any rows would point the query at a moment with zero samples. Once a day the job prunes the sample tables (`monitor.sampleRetentionDays` for monitor, `telemetryRetentionDays` for telemetry **and temperatures**, `systemInfoRetentionDays` for interface + storage + IPsec tunnels **and LLDP neighbors** — LLDP rides system-info retention because it's collected on the same cadence; the per-scrape full-replace already drops gone-away neighbors so the prune mostly catches rows for assets that have stopped scraping entirely). |

---

## Business Rules & Constraints

1. **No overlapping subnets** within the same block. Use `cidrContains()` / `cidrOverlaps()` from `src/utils/cidr.ts` before any subnet creation.
2. **Subnet must be contained within its parent block** — enforced at service layer.
3. **No duplicate IP reservations** — one `active` reservation per IP per subnet (`@@unique([subnetId, ipAddress, status])`).
4. **Block/subnet deletion protection** — HTTP 409 if any `active` reservations exist.
5. **CIDR normalization** — Host bits zeroed on write (e.g., `10.1.1.5/24` → `10.1.1.0/24`).
6. **sourceType tracking** — All discovered reservations carry a `sourceType`; manual entries default to `manual`.
7. **Conflict detection** — Discovery values differing from an existing manual reservation create a `Conflict` record rather than overwriting.
8. **Event archival** — Events older than 7 days are pruned; syslog (CEF) and SFTP/SCP archival are configurable.
9. **Asset `acquiredAt` ≤ `lastSeen`** — Enforced on every write via `clampAcquiredToLastSeen` in `src/utils/assetInvariants.ts`. If a write would leave `acquiredAt` later than `lastSeen`, `acquiredAt` is clamped down to match. Existing rows are repaired by the `clampAssetAcquiredAt` startup job.
10. **Decommissioned/disabled assets are not monitored** — Enforced on every Asset write via `clampMonitoredForStatus` in the Prisma extension in `src/db.ts`. Whenever a create/update/updateMany/upsert sets `status` to `decommissioned` or `disabled`, `monitored` is forced to `false` and `consecutiveFailures` is reset (matches the `monitored=false` branch of `validateMonitorConfig` in `assets.ts`). One-way: flipping status back to `active` does not auto-resume monitoring — re-enabling is operator-driven. Centralized at the DB layer so every write path benefits: the assets PUT route, the `decommissionStaleAssets` job, the integration FortiSwitch/FortiAP decommission sweep, and the Entra/AD syncs that set `status="disabled"` on disabled accounts.
11. **DNS-resolved reservations** — Any Asset with a primary `ipAddress` falling inside a known (non-deprecated) Subnet that has no existing active reservation gets an auto-created Reservation with `sourceType="dns_resolved"`, `createdBy="system:dns-resolved"`, carrying the asset's hostname (`hostname || dnsName`) and `macAddress` when available. Eligible asset statuses: `active`, `maintenance`, `storage`, `quarantined` (decommissioned/disabled assets release any existing row instead). IPv4 only. Surfaces in the Networks IP panel with a distinct "DNS Resolved" badge so operators don't hand the IP out to a manual reservation. Two reconcile paths: (a) the Prisma extension hook in `src/db.ts` fires `reconcileDnsResolvedForAsset(assetId)` after every `asset.create` / `asset.update` (gated to writes that touch ipAddress / status / hostname / dnsName / macAddress) / `asset.upsert`, and releases on `asset.delete`; (b) the `reconcileDnsResolvedReservations` periodic job sweeps every asset every 30 min as a safety net. Never pushes to FortiGates. Never raises Conflict rows — defers silently to manual / dhcp_reservation / dhcp_lease / interface_ip / vip / fortiswitch / fortinap / fortimanager / fortigate. The discovery sync (`syncDhcpSubnets` in `integrations.ts`) and `reservationService.createReservation` both call `releaseDnsResolvedAt(subnetId, ipAddress)` before creating an authoritative reservation so the unique-on-active constraint stays satisfied. See `src/services/dnsResolvedReservationService.ts`.

---

## Frontend

Vanilla JavaScript SPA served from `/public/`. No build step — plain ES modules.

- Multi-page layout with client-side navigation (`app.js`)
- Light/dark theme toggle
- Real-time discovery progress polling (`integrations.js`)
- Bulk operations (delete, release)
- PDF and CSV asset export
- Conflict resolution slide-over panel (Events page)
- First-run setup wizard (`setup.html`) backed by `src/setup/`
- Device Map page has an **Edit regions** toolbar button (admin/networkadmin only — gated by `canManageNetworks()` on the client; backend guard is the source of truth). The default map view never renders region polygons — they appear only after entering edit mode. Edit mode mounts `L.Control.Draw` (vendored leaflet-draw 1.0.4) for drawing new polygons, overlays existing regions on a dedicated `L.featureGroup`, and binds permanent name labels at each polygon's centroid. **Draw new** prompts for a name via `openModal()` and posts to `POST /map/regions`. **Click an existing polygon** opens a popup with **Rename** / **Change color** / **Send to Bottom Layer** / **Delete** actions. **Send to Bottom Layer** calls `poly.bringToBack()` so a region nested inside another (overlapping polygons where the outer one was drawn later and visually obscures the inner one) can be clicked through to — per-session only, the saved-on-server order doesn't change. **Drag a vertex** (or midpoint) reshapes the polygon in-memory — vertex editing is always on per polygon in edit mode (no separate Edit toolbar round-trip); every drag fires `editvertex` and marks the polygon dirty without issuing a PUT. **Right-click drag** pans the map (always on; context menu is suppressed inside the map container). Useful while drawing a polygon, since left-click is captured for vertex placement. **Save Regions** commits every dirty polygon via parallel PUTs, then exits edit mode; per-polygon failures alert the operator and leave edit mode open so the operator can retry or discard. **Discard Changes** reverts every dirty polygon to its loaded shape via `setLatLngs` (with an editing-handle refresh) and exits edit mode — rename/delete/draw-new actions taken during the session are NOT in the dirty bucket because they hit the server immediately on click, so Discard can't undo them. The save/discard buttons sit alongside the Edit-regions button in the toolbar; visibility flips via `setEditModeButtons` so only the relevant set is shown for the current mode. The draw plugin's CSS sprite assets live alongside the existing Leaflet marker images at [public/css/vendor/leaflet/images/](public/css/vendor/leaflet/images/) (`spritesheet.png`, `spritesheet-2x.png`, `spritesheet.svg`). Each region maps to a `region:<name>` tag stamped on every enclosed firewall asset (and cascaded to its managed FortiSwitches / FortiAPs by `fortinetTopology.controllerFortigate` hostname match) by the backend reconciler — see "Map Regions" under the API surface.
- Asset list shows a five-state Status pill (**Up** green / **Warning** amber / **Pending** blue / **Down** red / **Unmonitored** grey). For admin/assetsadmin the pill is **clickable** — single click toggles `monitored`; the server then recomputes `monitorOverride` (true when the new monitored value diverges from the discovering integration's per-class `addAsMonitored`, false when they match — including when the operator's click brings them back into agreement). On the asset details slide-in **System tab**, an **Asset Override** badge (`_assetOverrideBadge` in `public/js/assets.js`) renders next to the Status pill when `monitorOverride === true` so the operator can see at a glance which assets have been pinned away from the integration's auto-monitor default. The Type column pill is also clickable for admin/assetsadmin and opens a dropdown of the eight `assetType` values for inline reclassification. The bulk-action toolbar (visible when 2+ rows are selected) is built from three dropdowns and three labelled buttons: **Type ▾** / **State ▾** loop a per-asset `PUT /assets/:id` for the chosen `assetType` / `status`; **Monitoring ▾** (Enable / Disable) hits `POST /assets/bulk-monitor`; **Edit Tags** opens a tags-only modal (add or replace) that loops per-asset PUTs; **Quarantine selected** / **Release Quarantine** route through the dedicated `/assets/bulk-quarantine` and `/assets/bulk-quarantine/release` endpoints; **Delete selected** posts to `DELETE /assets`. The Type and State menus are populated dynamically from the `ASSET_TYPE_LABELS` / `ASSET_STATUS_LABELS` maps in `public/js/assets.js`; `quarantined` is intentionally not a State option (the regular asset PUT rejects that status — quarantine has its own flow). The Acquired date is intentionally omitted from the list — it lives only on the asset details slide-in (General tab) to keep the row narrow.
- Assets page header has a **Monitoring Settings** button (admin/assetsadmin only) opening a modal with two sections: **Manual Monitoring** (tier-3 settings for orphan assets — eight fields including `probeTimeoutMs`) and **Class Overrides** (cross-source list with add/edit/delete; the asset-source picker offers every integration plus a "Manual" entry). Same data store the integration Monitoring tab edits.
- Asset details slide-in **System** tab status block: just under the Status pill is a **last-hour intermittency bar** — one cell per probe sample over the past hour, colored by the resolved monitor state at that point (green=up / amber=warning / blue=recovering / red=down). The bar replays the five-state machine forward over the samples client-side using the asset's resolved `failureThreshold` from `/effective-monitor-settings`, so a brief blip shows up as a transient yellow run and a true outage as a sustained red run. Hovering a cell shows the timestamp + status. Hidden on unmonitored assets.
- Each asset row's Actions cell renders a **View Lease** button when the asset has an `ipAddress` and the row's `ipContext` resolves a non-deprecated containing subnet. Clicking it navigates to `/subnets.html#ip=<subnetId>@<ipAddress>`, where the network slide-over opens with the asset's IP scrolled into view and visually highlighted; reserve / release / edit happens in that panel directly. The Networks slide-over is also where the **View Asset** button (per-IP, on rows where the IP resolves to a Polaris asset) lives — clicking it navigates to `/assets.html#view=asset:<id>` so the asset details slide-in opens on the assets page.
- Asset edit modal is tab-based (General + Monitoring). The details modal has three tabs by default (General + System + **Sources**) and adds a fourth **SNMP Walk** tab for admins (admin-only on both the frontend and the backend). Controls: a **MIB** dropdown (optional — groups Standard MIBs and Uploaded MIBs), a **Base OID / Object name** input that switches label and validation based on the selected MIB, an SNMP credential picker (any stored SNMP credential), and a Max-rows input (default 500, capped at 5,000). **Standard MIBs** (System, IF-MIB, IF-MIB Extended, HOST-RESOURCES-MIB, ENTITY-MIB, ENTITY-SENSOR-MIB, LLDP-MIB, Fortinet FORTIGATE-MIB) pre-fill the Base OID and use the raw numeric walk (`POST /assets/:id/snmp-walk`) — results render as OID / Type / Value rows. **Uploaded MIBs** (from the MIB Database in Server Settings) switch the input to an object name field, render a **browse tree** beneath the buttons row (Tables + Scalars / Other sections from `GET /server-settings/mibs/:id/structure`, cached per mibId in `_snmpMibStructureCache`) where clicking a symbol fills the Object name input + highlights the row without auto-walking (typing in the input still works as the source of truth), and use the MIB-aware walk (`POST /server-settings/mibs/:id/walk` with `{ assetId, credentialId, objectName, maxRows }`) — results render decoded: `kind: "table"` as a 2D grid with column headers and index column (decoded enum labels with raw value in tooltip on hover), `kind: "scalars"` as OID / Symbol / Value rows (decoded value with raw in parentheses). A yellow banner fires when `decodedCount < rowCount / 2` (MIB doesn't match the asset's manufacturer). **No MIB** (default) uses raw numeric walk with manual OID entry. Abort is available for raw walks only (MIB-aware endpoint has no signal support). Copy results produces TSV for tables and one-per-line `symbol = decoded (raw)` for scalars. MIB selection, OID/object name, and credential persist across tab open/close via module-level variables (`_snmpWalkLastMibId`, `_snmpWalkLastObjectName`, `_snmpWalkLastOid`, `_snmpWalkLastCredId`). Uploaded MIBs load lazily on first tab wire from `api.mibs.listMibs({})` and are cached in `_snmpMibCache`. Each walk is audited as an `asset.snmp_walk` Event. The Monitoring tab on the edit modal renders an editable type dropdown for every asset; integration-discovered assets (FMG/FortiGate-discovered firewalls and AD-discovered Windows/Linux hosts) get an extra option representing the integration default (e.g. "FortiManager: \<name\> (default)") plus a hint pointing operators to SNMP for small-branch FortiGates whose REST sensor endpoint 404s. The **System** details tab leads with the monitoring section — status pill, source, last RTT/poll/consecutive failures, and an SVG response-time chart (1h / 24h / 7d / 30d / Custom) plus a "Refresh" button for user-or-above (kicks off all three streams — response-time probe, telemetry, system-info — and the toast names exactly which streams refreshed and which failed, e.g. `Refresh partial (probe 12 ms · telemetry) — interfaces: FortiManager direct-mode API token not configured`) — then a horizontal divider, then a single combined CPU+Memory chart (both lines on a shared 0–100% y-axis with one hover tooltip naming both values) over 1h / 24h / 7d / 30d / Custom, a Temperatures section (current sensor table — hidden when the device exposes no sensors — with each sensor name clickable to open a per-sensor slide-over chart), an Interfaces table with a "Poll 1m" checkbox column + clickable interface name + cumulative errors column + LLDP **Neighbor** column (rightmost; shows the first neighbor's `systemName` plus its remote `portId` as `<sysName> / <portId>` — the system name links to that asset's view modal when the neighbor matched a Polaris asset; a `+N` badge appears when the local port saw multiple neighbors and the slide-over enumerates all of them) (FortiOS-monitored phase-1 IPsec tunnels are nested under their parent interface as child rows here — orange "IPsec" badge, status pill, remote gateway in the IP column, cumulative in/out bytes; tunnels whose `parentInterface` lookup fails fall into a final "IPsec Tunnels (unbound)" group at the bottom of the same table), and a Storage table (Poll 1m checkbox, mount, used, total, %); empty-state messages render for unmonitored assets, ICMP/SSH-monitored assets, and WinRM/AD-monitored assets (the last is a placeholder until WMI Enumerate-over-WS-Management lands). The Interface column shows the operator-set **alias** as the primary label when present (FortiOS CMDB `alias` / SNMP `ifAlias`), with the real `ifName` rendered as a small subtitle and as the cell tooltip so the operator can still correlate to switch port labels. The Poll 1m checkboxes — interface, storage, and nested tunnel — write to `Asset.monitoredInterfaces` / `monitoredStorage` / `monitoredIpsecTunnels` respectively and pin the row for sub-minute polling on the response-time cadence. Clicking an interface name opens a **nested slide-over** whose header reads `<alias> (<ifName>)` when an alias is set, followed by an **Interface Comments** editor — a 255-char textarea + Save/Revert buttons (assets admin and admin only; everyone else gets a disabled box). Save writes to `AssetInterfaceOverride.description` via `PUT /assets/:id/interfaces/:ifName/comment` and is **Polaris-local only — never pushed to the device**. When no override is set, the device-reported FortiOS CMDB `description` is shown as the textarea placeholder ("Device says: …") so operators can see what's currently surfaced before deciding to type over it. Saving an empty box clears the override and the discovered description shows through again. Auto-refresh ticks don't clobber in-progress edits — the editor tracks a dirty flag and skips repopulation while the user is typing. Below the comment editor the body renders an **LLDP Neighbor card** (one card per neighbor on this interface; shows system name as a clickable link when matched to a Polaris asset, plus chassis ID + management IP + capabilities + system description; an "unmatched" badge when no Polaris asset resolved by mgmt IP / chassis MAC / hostname) followed by **two charts**: one combined Throughput chart (input + output on a shared bps axis, single tooltip naming both) and an in/out errors-per-interval chart. Clicking a mountpoint name opens a slide-over with a single usage chart that toggles between **Used %** and **Used bytes** (1h / 24h / 7d / 30d / Custom; defaults to 24h since storage rides the ~10m systemInfo cadence). The chart overlays a dashed **forecast** line projecting current growth toward 100% full — linear least-squares regression over the last 7 days of usedBytes samples (always 7d, independent of the chart's selected range; one extra `storageHistory` fetch in parallel with the range fetch, deduped when the operator picked 7d). A headline above the chart reads `Days until full: N` (color-coded red <30d / amber <90d / green ≥90d) or `Shrinking` / `No measurable growth` / `> 1 year` depending on the regression's `signal`. Projection extension is capped at one history-span (so a slow trend on a 1h range doesn't compress history to a sliver) AND at one year absolute. A vertical dashed "now" divider marks the projection origin. Forecast computation always runs; toggling the forecast off only hides the dashed overlay (the headline stays visible because the days-to-full number is the operationally useful piece). Clicking a tunnel name opens a similar nested slide-over with a status timeline (1h / 24h / 7d / 30d / Custom) and per-interval throughput charts; clicking a sensor name opens a per-sensor temperature slide-over (1h / 24h / 7d / 30d / Custom) with axis labels and a chart title. Closing only that panel returns to the asset details panel underneath. All charts share `_wireChartTooltip` for hover behaviour. The selected range on each chart type (response time, system tab CPU+memory, sensor, interface, IPsec tunnel, storage) is remembered per user via `localStorage` under `polaris-prefs-charts-<username>` — one JSON map keyed by chart id (`assetMonitor`, `assetSystem`, `assetSensor`, `assetInterface`, `assetIpsec`, `assetStorage`) so each surface keeps its own last selection independently. "Custom" range selections are not persisted (the from/to inputs reset on reopen). The storage chart's Used %/bytes view mode persists under `polaris-prefs-storage-view-<username>` (single string per user — same mental model across the fleet); the forecast-overlay visibility persists under `polaris-prefs-storage-forecast-<username>` as a `{ "<assetId>:<mountPath>": boolean }` map (per asset+mount — defaults on; only stored when explicitly toggled). Collapsed-state for nested rows in the Interfaces table (aggregate members, VLAN sub-interfaces, nested IPsec tunnels) is also remembered per user via `localStorage` under `polaris-prefs-iface-collapse-<username>` — one JSON map keyed by `assetId` to an array of collapsed parent ifNames. Per-asset because the same parent name on different assets can have wildly different children. Empty entries are pruned so the blob self-cleans when everything is re-expanded.
- The **Sources** tab on the asset details modal renders one card per `AssetSource` row attached to this asset, in stable presentation order (`entra` → `intune` → `ad` → `fortigate-firewall` → `fortiswitch` → `fortiap` → `manual`). Each card shows the source's friendly label (e.g. "Microsoft Entra ID"), the originating integration as a badge, an **Inferred** badge for phase-1-backfilled rows that haven't been replaced by real discovery yet, the `syncedAt` / `firstSeen` / `lastSeen` timestamps + the source's natural `externalId` as a metadata strip, and the source's `observed` blob rendered as a humanized key-value table (camelCase keys → "Title Case", ISO timestamps → human dates, booleans → Yes/No, complex values → JSON). Loaded once per modal open via `GET /assets/:id/sources`; failures fall through to an empty-state message so the rest of the modal still works. This is the operator-visible payoff of the Phase 1+2 multi-source foundation — see what each integration independently said about the device, side-by-side. **Admins** see a **Split** button per card (hidden when the asset only has one source, and on `manual` source rows): clicking detaches the chosen source onto a freshly-created Asset via `POST /assets/:id/sources/:sourceId/split`, after a confirm dialog. The current modal closes and the new asset's view modal opens so the operator can verify the move.
- The asset details modal **General** tab renders a **Dependency Tree** block beneath the details table on Fortinet infra assets (firewalls / switches / APs) — a vertical mini-tree showing this asset's effective parents above, the asset itself in the middle (rendered bold + non-clickable, with a layer annotation), and direct children below. Each row carries a status pip (▲ up / ▼ down / ● dep-down/pending/unmonitored) using the same priority as the Status pill (probe-down beats dep-down beats five-state). Hostnames are clickable buttons that pivot `openViewModal` to that asset, so operators can walk up or down the tree without leaving the modal. An **override active** badge appears in the header when the asset has any `source="override"` row; an `override` tag also marks override-pinned parents inline. Children render two layers down (direct children + their own children) with the deeper row indented and rendered slightly smaller so the firewall → switch → AP chain shows in one view; operators click through to walk further than two layers. The block is loaded in parallel with the Sources tab via `GET /assets/:id/dependencies`; the placeholder `<div id="asset-dep-tree-mount-<id>">` is filled after the modal body is rendered so the rest of the General tab paints immediately. Hidden entirely for endpoint assets (workstation / printer / etc.) which never have entries in the dependency DAG; standalone Fortinet infra assets show a one-line "Standalone" empty-state instead of the tree.
- Asset list status-column filter includes **Quarantined**. Per-row Actions cell shows a **Quarantine** button (deep-red) for assets-admin+ on assets that have at least one MAC on record; quarantined assets get a **Release Quarantine** button instead. Bulk bar has matching **Quarantine** / **Release Quarantine** buttons that appear only when the selection mix makes them relevant. Asset details panel adds a **Quarantine** tab for assets-admin+ (shown whenever the asset has MACs or is quarantined): displays quarantine status, per-FortiGate push targets table (FortiGate name, status badge, pushed MACs, pushed-at timestamp), DHCP sightings history, and Quarantine / Release / Verify Push action buttons.
- Server Settings → **Credentials** tab manages the stored SNMP / WinRM / SSH credentials (admin-only). Secrets are masked in every GET; resubmitting the mask preserves the stored value on PUT.
- Server Settings → **API Tokens** tab (admin-only, lazy-loaded) lists all bearer tokens with status badges (Active/Revoked/Expired), last-used IP, scopes, **per-token integration scope** (the list of FMG/FortiGate names this token can quarantine via, with inline red/amber alerts when an integration is disabled or has `pushQuarantine: false` so the operator sees at-a-glance that pushes will be skipped), and Revoke/Delete actions. Create-token form has name, scope checkboxes (from `knownScopes`), an integration multi-picker that appears only when `assets:quarantine` is checked (REQUIRED — at least one integration; each row is decorated with the same disabled / pushQuarantine-off alert text as the table), and optional expiry. The picker source is the `quarantineIntegrations` array on `GET /api-tokens` so admins don't need a separate `/integrations` round-trip. On creation a modal shows the raw token value with a Copy button — the only time it is visible.
- Server Settings → **Credentials** tab has a **MIB Database** card for managing SNMP MIB modules. **Visibility**: admin sees the full Server Settings page; **assets-admin** also sees a Server Settings link in the sidebar but the page is restricted to just the Credentials tab AND only the MIB Database card within it (every other tab is hidden client-side, and within the Credentials tab the Stored Credentials list + Manufacturer Profiles card are gated to admin inside `renderCredentialsTab()`; backend guards on `/server-settings/*` are the source of truth). Uploads are validated by `mibService.parseMib` (rejects anything that isn't a real ASN.1/SMI module — including binaries and arbitrary text); upload + delete remain admin-only, surfaced inline in the card and hidden for assets-admin. The form has a three-tier **Scope** selector: **Manufacturer-wide** (the most common case — covers every model from one vendor), **Device-specific** (overrides the manufacturer-wide MIB for one model only), or **Generic** (shared across all vendors, e.g. SNMPv2-SMI). Resolution priority at probe time is *device → vendor → generic → built-in seed*. The card also renders a **Vendor Profile Status** pill that shows, per built-in profile, whether each profile symbol resolves at the universal (manufacturer-only) scope and which MIB provided it; any model-specific MIBs uploaded under that manufacturer are listed beneath as "Model overrides". Uploads are wired into the SNMP telemetry probe via `oidRegistry` + `vendorTelemetryProfiles`: dropping in CISCO-PROCESS-MIB / CISCO-MEMORY-POOL-MIB / JUNIPER-MIB / MIKROTIK-MIB / FORTINET-FORTIGATE-MIB / etc. immediately starts populating CPU/memory on assets whose `manufacturer` matches the profile's regex. Until the MIB is uploaded the probe falls back to HOST-RESOURCES-MIB (which is null on most network gear). Each row carries a **Browse** button (open to admin + assets-admin) that opens an XL-sized two-pane modal driven by `GET /server-settings/mibs/:id/structure`: left pane lists Tables and Scalars / Other in collapsible sections (table entries show their column count inline); right pane renders the selected object's name, full OID, SYNTAX, ACCESS, STATUS, DESCRIPTION, an Enum Values table when the SYNTAX is `INTEGER { up(1), down(2), … }`, and a **Walk on asset…** button shown when the symbol is readable AND its OID resolved. Walk pivots inline within the same modal: an asset autocomplete (`GET /assets?search=&limit=25` — explicitly NOT the global `/search` endpoint, whose 8-per-group cap can drop firewalls), then an SNMP credential dropdown (`api.credentials.list()` filtered to `type === "snmp"`), then a Run Walk button that posts to `POST /server-settings/mibs/:id/walk`. Results render scalars as a flat decoded list (`<symbol>.<suffix>  =  <decoded>  (<raw>)`) or, when the walk lands on a table, a 2D `<table>` with column headers from the MIB and rows keyed by the SMI INDEX; cell tooltips carry the raw value when it differs from the decoded form. A **Copy results** button emits TSV (table view) or one-entry-per-line text (scalars). When `decodedCount < rowCount/2` a yellow banner reads "Decoded N / M rows — this MIB may not match the asset's manufacturer."
- Server Settings → **Credentials** also has a **Manufacturer Profiles** card (admin-only) — editable surface backed by the `ManufacturerProfile` data model. Lists every profile with caret-expandable detail; click a profile to see the seven metric rows (CPU / Memory / Temperature / Interfaces / LLDP / Storage / Wireless Stations). Each metric row has a **MIB → Symbol** chained picker — operator selects the MIB first (Built-in seed or any uploaded MIB scoped to this manufacturer), and the Symbol field becomes a dropdown populated by walking that MIB's structured parse via `GET /server-settings/mibs/:id/structure` (cached per-MIB in `_mfgMibSymbolsCache`); when the MIB is "Built-in seed" the Symbol stays a free-text input since seeded symbols don't have an enumerable directory. Per-model exception rows hang under each metric row with the same MIB → Symbol pattern + a Model regex input + Edit / Del buttons. +Add Manufacturer at the bottom; delete via the per-row Del button (cascade-removes metric rows, overrides, widgets). The legacy read-only **Vendor Profile Status** card was removed — this editable surface now covers that ground end-to-end. A **Custom widgets** section sits below the metric table — one card per `ManufacturerCustomWidget` with view + inline-edit modes plus a `+ Add widget` button. Fields: Name, Widget type (gauge / line / table), MIB (uploaded MIBs only — std MIBs rejected at save with a clear toast since the asset-side collector needs an enumerable symbol directory), Symbol type (scalar/table), Symbol (filtered picker driven by Type), Transform, Model regex (optional per-asset gate), Order, plus a per-widget-type **Display options** sub-form (gauge: min/max/unit/warning-at; line: unit; table: no options — renderer derives columns from the latest sample). Shadow stores `_mfgWidgetEditMib` / `_mfgWidgetEditType` / `_mfgWidgetEditWidgetType` keep the dependent pickers live before save. Backend: `POST/PUT/DELETE /server-settings/manufacturer-profiles/:id/widgets[/:widgetId]`. Read path on assets: `GET /assets/:id/custom-widgets` (consumed by the Custom MIB tab — `_loadCustomMibTabFor`). Samples populate on the next telemetry cadence tick via `collectAndRecordCustomWidgets`.
- Server Settings → **Identification** also has a **Manufacturer Aliases** card (admin-only) — sits between OUI Overrides and the OUI Database card. Lists every alias grouped by canonical name (table columns: Alias, Canonical, Edit/Del). Add form is a two-input row: Alias (the input string to rewrite) + Canonical (the stored value) + Add button. Edit opens a small `openModal` dialog; delete uses `showConfirm`. Every save refreshes the in-memory cache and runs the existing-row backfill in the background, so the change propagates to historical Asset/MibFile rows without a restart. The card also handles the underlying problem the OUI database creates: IEEE registers vendors under their legal name (`Fortinet, Inc.`) while discovery code stamps the marketing name (`Fortinet`), and without normalization they show up as two distinct values in every manufacturer dropdown, MIB scope picker, and vendor profile match.
- Server Settings → **Maintenance** tab (formerly "Database") groups everything operational: the Capacity Advisor card on top, then the Sample Retention card, then capacity grading, in-app updates, database engine info, storage breakdown, backups, restore, and backup history. The **Capacity Advisor** card shows per-lever current vs recommended for connection-pool sizes, monitor worker counts, queue mode, and PostgreSQL tuning settings; each lever has a per-row checkbox + a single **Stage selected** button that writes the chosen env-driven values to `.env` (restart-required) and routes the QUEUE_MODE lever through the existing `POST /queue-mode` endpoint. Advisory-only rows (`PG_MAX_CONNECTIONS` and PostgreSQL settings) have no checkbox because they require a PostgreSQL restart Polaris can't trigger. Cold-start badges appear next to cadence-driven rows while the `polaris_monitor_work_duration_seconds` histogram is still filling. The **Sample Retention** card holds the 27-input grid for `Setting("sampleRetention")` — 3 streams (Response time / CPU+memory+temperature / Interfaces+storage+IPsec) × 3 tiers (Detail / Hourly avg / Daily avg) × 3 classes (Default / Switches / Access points). Defaults 7/30/365; values are days, 0 means "keep forever for that cell". Save + Restore-defaults buttons; out-of-range values (≥3651 or negative) are blocked client-side. Takes effect on the next nightly prune and on the next chart history request (5-second cache TTL on reads). Below the advisor + retention cards, the **Database** card carries the status pill (`Healthy` / `Action recommended` / `Critical`) plus a list of capacity reasons with suggestions, then four side-by-side stat cards: App host (cpu/ram/disk + DB co-located flag), Database (current size, steady-state size, sample-table breakdown with dead-tuple % per table), Database engine + connection pool, and Monitoring workload (monitored asset count, monitored interface count, cadences, retention). The legacy `pg_tuning_needed` inline current→recommended rows have moved into the advisor card; the `pg_tuning_needed` reason itself still surfaces in the reasons list pointing at the advisor. The legacy `pgboss_recommended` / `pgboss_overdue` / `pgboss_pending` reasons and their inline "Enable on next restart" / "Cancel" buttons were absorbed into the advisor's QUEUE_MODE lever and no longer fire. When `capacity` is unavailable (e.g. statfs unsupported), the Database card degrades to a plain "Database" header with just the engine+pool sub-card so operators don't lose connection visibility. Driven by `GET /server-settings/capacity-advisor` (replaces the prior `/pg-tuning` fetch as the Maintenance tab data source — `/pg-tuning` stays as the source for the sidebar critical-alert check in `app.js`). Back-compat: `?tab=database` deep links automatically map to `?tab=maintenance`. The sidebar on every page shows a non-dismissible **red** alert when capacity severity is critical; amber / watch reasons are card-only — there's no sidebar nag for them.

---

## Environment Variables

```env
# Database
DATABASE_URL=postgresql://user:password@localhost:5432/polaris

# Optional: direct-to-PostgreSQL URL for operators running PgBouncer
# (or any connection multiplexer) in front of Postgres. When set,
# Polaris routes pg-boss queue ops, pg_dump backup/restore, and
# pg_stat_activity reads through this URL — they can't run through
# PgBouncer transaction-pool mode (LISTEN/NOTIFY, prepared statement
# cache, and COPY-heavy pg_dump protocol all break). Application
# queries (Prisma) still go through DATABASE_URL.
# Leave unset on direct-to-Postgres installs.
POLARIS_DB_DIRECT_URL=

# pg pool size for the Prisma driver adapter (default 25). Raise when running
# with many monitored assets so worker writes don't queue inside the adapter.
# Surfaced on the Maintenance tab by the Capacity Advisor card, which
# computes a recommended value from observable workload (worker ceiling +
# discovery reserve + HTTP/job overhead) and writes via the Stage button.
DATABASE_POOL_SIZE=25

# Other pool / worker env vars also surfaced + tunable by the Capacity Advisor:
#   POLARIS_PGBOSS_POOL_SIZE          (default 20; advisor recommends max(20, ceil(workerCeiling × 0.25)))
#   POLARIS_MONITOR_PROBE_WORKERS     (default 24) — pg-boss probe queue
#   POLARIS_MONITOR_FAST_WORKERS      (default 24) — pg-boss fastFiltered queue
#   POLARIS_MONITOR_HEAVY_WORKERS     (default 24) — pg-boss telemetry + systemInfo
#   POLARIS_MONITOR_FLOATING_WORKERS  (default 32) — cross-queue absorber
#   POLARIS_PROBE_CONCURRENCY         (cursor mode probe + fastFiltered cap)
#   POLARIS_HEAVY_CONCURRENCY         (cursor mode telemetry + systemInfo cap)

# App
PORT=3000
NODE_ENV=development
LOG_LEVEL=info

# Session — required in production; server refuses to boot without it
SESSION_SECRET=changeme

# Reverse-proxy trust — leave unset on direct-to-internet deployments; set
# to a hop count ("1"), "loopback", or CIDR only when behind a real proxy.
# Setting it without a proxy lets clients spoof X-Forwarded-For and bypass
# the login rate limiter.
TRUST_PROXY=

# Health check bearer token — auto-generated by the first-run setup wizard.
# When set, /health requires `Authorization: Bearer <token>`. Clear the
# value if you genuinely need an open endpoint (some L4 health probes
# can't carry a header); the Maintenance tab surfaces a `health_token_unset`
# watch-severity reason while it is unset.
HEALTH_TOKEN=

# Prometheus metrics bearer token — auto-generated by the first-run setup
# wizard. When set, /metrics requires `Authorization: Bearer <token>`.
# Auto-generated because the endpoint leaks fleet size, monitor health
# by status, monitor pass duration, and queue depth as recon data.
# Clearing surfaces a `metrics_token_unset` watch-severity reason on the
# Maintenance tab.
METRICS_TOKEN=

# TimescaleDB compression window (days). Default 7. Only consulted when the
# timescaledb extension is installed; chunks older than this are compressed
# (~10-30× storage reduction). Set 0 to disable compression entirely.
TIMESCALE_COMPRESS_AFTER_DAYS=7

# Persistent-state directory — optional. When set, the four runtime-mutable
# state items (.env, .setup-complete, data/backups, public/uploads) all live
# under this single directory. Leaving it unset keeps the legacy layout where
# each item lives at its historical path under the project root, so RHEL prod
# and dev installs see no behavior change. Resolved by `src/utils/paths.ts`;
# the Docker image pins this to /app/state so Unraid can persist state with
# one bind mount.
POLARIS_STATE_DIR=

# HTTPS (optional)
HTTPS_CERT_PATH=
HTTPS_KEY_PATH=
HTTPS_REDIRECT=false

# Azure SAML (optional)
AZURE_TENANT_ID=
AZURE_APP_ID=
AZURE_SAML_CALLBACK_URL=

# Syslog (optional)
SYSLOG_HOST=
SYSLOG_PORT=514
SYSLOG_PROTOCOL=udp

# SFTP archival (optional)
ARCHIVE_SFTP_HOST=
ARCHIVE_SFTP_PORT=22
ARCHIVE_SFTP_USER=
ARCHIVE_SFTP_PASSWORD=
ARCHIVE_SFTP_PATH=
```

Copy `.env.example` to `.env` before running.

---

## Deployment & Updates

The production instance is updated via the **in-app update mechanism** in **Server Settings → Maintenance**. When pushing changes, the user applies the update through that UI rather than manually redeploying. Keep this in mind when giving deployment advice — do not suggest `git pull` or manual restart steps unless asked.

Fresh installs are documented in `docs/INSTALL.md` with three platform sections (RHEL/Rocky/AlmaLinux 9, Ubuntu/Debian, Windows Server). The disk-sizing table at the top of that file is the single source of truth for DB-volume / app-volume / `/var/log` minimums and recommendations — keep it in sync whenever the runtime workload (sample-table cadences, retention defaults, update-staging cost) changes meaningfully. The setup wizard's preflight check uses `RECOMMENDED_DB_FREE_GB = 50` as the threshold for the warning banner; bump that constant in `src/setup/setupRoutes.ts` if the recommended minimum changes and update the docs table to match.

### Disk-space monitoring (post-install)

Three layers catch a filling DB volume before it crashes Postgres:

1. **Boot-time diagnostic** — `runStartupDiskCheck` in `src/utils/startupDiskCheck.ts` statfs's the app/state/backups dirs plus conventional PostgreSQL data directory candidates per platform. Emits an info/warn/error log line per volume. Non-fatal — never blocks boot. Catches the "polaris flapping with cryptic Prisma error because /var is full" symptom by turning it into a clear log line.
2. **Periodic capacityWatch job** — every 10 minutes, builds a capacity snapshot and calls `recordCapacityTransition` to write a `capacity.severity_changed` Event when severity flips (ok ↔ watch ↔ amber ↔ red). Events flow out through eventArchiveService → syslog/SFTP archival, so the alert reaches on-call independent of whether the UI is reachable.
3. **Maintenance tab volume bars** — Server Settings → Maintenance → Database card renders one bar per distinct filesystem (deduped by stat.dev) with severity-colored fill and the Polaris-named role(s) each volume serves. Severity tiering: **watch** at 20–30% free (Event-only), **amber** at 10–20%, **red** below 10% (drives the non-dismissible sidebar banner).

### First-run setup lock

The setup wizard is unauthenticated by design (the operator needs to reach it from a browser to provision the host). To stop a network attacker from re-running the wizard against an already-configured host whose `.env` got deleted/corrupted, finalize writes a `.setup-complete` marker at the project root. On every boot:

- `DATABASE_URL` set → app boots normally; marker is back-filled if missing (covers existing installs).
- `DATABASE_URL` missing AND no marker → wizard runs (fresh install).
- `DATABASE_URL` missing AND marker present → process logs a recovery message and exits 1; the wizard never starts.

To intentionally re-provision from scratch, an admin with shell access deletes both `.env` and `.setup-complete`.

---

## Getting Started

```bash
# Install dependencies
npm install

# Setup database
npx prisma migrate dev --name init

# Seed example data
npm run db:seed

# Start dev server (with hot reload)
npm run dev

# Run tests
npm test

# Build for production
npm run build && npm start

# Test FortiManager connectivity
npm run test:fmg

# Type check / lint
npm run typecheck
npm run lint
```

---

## Key Coding Conventions

- All IP math lives in `src/utils/cidr.ts`. **Never** do string manipulation on IPs elsewhere.
- Services (`src/services/`) contain **all business logic**. Route handlers are thin — validate input, call a service, return a response.
- All Zod schemas live co-located with their route file (top of file).
- Database calls go through service functions only — never raw Prisma in route handlers.
- All errors thrown by services must be instances of `AppError` (`src/utils/errors.ts`) with an `httpStatus` property.
- Use `async/await` throughout; avoid `.then()` chains.
- Write a unit test for every public function in `src/utils/` and `src/services/`.
- All audit-worthy actions (creates, updates, deletes, discovery events) must write an `Event` record.
- **Ask before committing.** After completing each logical change, do not commit automatically — ask the user whether to commit. Each logical change still gets its own commit (don't batch unrelated work), but the user decides when.
- **Before any commit, review CLAUDE.md, `touches.md`, and `primaries.md` for updates.** When the user approves a commit, re-read all three — CLAUDE.md sections (new models, fields, routes, services, jobs, env vars, or behavior shifts), the relevant `touches.md` entries (per-service writers/readers/invariants/checklists or cross-cutting writer/reader lists), AND the relevant `primaries.md` entries (canonical implementations whose file paths, function names, conventions, or checklists may have shifted; new patterns that have crystallized into a clear canonical and should be added).
